# How PARAGON works

PARAGON is a plain-text project and life management app. Everything you write is a markdown file in a folder you own. The app reads and writes those files; Apple Reminders and Apple Calendar stay in sync with them.

Every heading below opens and closes, so this page is a contents list you drill into rather than a wall of text. The box at the top searches it: type a word and only the parts that mention it are shown, already opened.

If a word is unclear, **Words in PARAGON** explains each of the app's own words in one or two sentences. On the Mac it is in the **Help** menu, and at the top of this window. On the iPhone it is in **Settings**, under **Help**.

Throughout: **right-click** on the Mac is **hold** (a long press) on the iPhone. Where this page says one, the other works on the other device. The app itself shows only the words of the device you use (since build 309): **Tap** and **Hold** on the iPhone and the iPad, **Click** and **Right-click** on the Mac.

## The idea

- **Goals** say what you want in life. They sit above everything else.
- **Projects** are things with an end: a race, a move, a report. Each one should serve a goal.
- **Areas** are things you keep up over time: health, home, a client. They also serve goals. An area can sit under another one as a **sub-area** — Mobility under Health, say, or Yoga, Pliability and Stretching under Mobility. Right-click an area (long-press on the phone) › **Part of** to put it under another, or to take it back out. Areas nest one level, no deeper, so the list stays a list.
- **Resources** are reference material: articles, checklists, ideas.
- **Archive** is where finished projects and closed areas go. They stay searchable.
- **Inbox** catches everything you have not sorted yet. It has a screen of its own; see **The Inbox** below.

That is PARA with a Goals layer on top. Every note answers "what does this serve?".

## How PARAGON looks

Every part of PARAGON has one colour, and the colour means the same thing everywhere. **Projects** and **All actions** are green. **Areas** and **Tags** are pink. **Resources**, **Recent**, **Templates** and **Search** are blue. **Archive**, **Deleted** and **Work** are grey. **Goals** and the **Map** are gold, and **Aspirations** a deeper gold. **Inbox**, **Snippets** and **Quick capture** are leaf green (since build 310; before, amber, which looked like the orange in **Plan**). **Today**, **Calendar**, **Plan** and the daily and weekly notes are purple. **Weekly review** and **Done** are teal. Blocks in **Plan** are orange. **Start the day** is orange and **Close the day** is lilac. Red is only for things that are overdue or will be deleted. The colour shows in the icon in the sidebar, in the headings, in the main button, in the small dot at the left of a list row, and in the links of a note of that part. Day and night each have their own shade: a deep one by day and a bright one at night. Some parts share a colour on purpose, as listed here; they are families. Anything that belongs to no part, such as **Browse** and **Settings** on the iPhone or **Set** in the date window, is purple, the colour of **Today**. PARAGON does not use Apple's blue (since build 310).

Every screen opens with a band in its part's colour (new in build 282). It is a soft gradient, from the part's colour to a slightly deeper shade of the same colour towards the bottom right. On it are the title in white, the date or the facts in small white words, and one big number, 34 points, bold. Since build 291 **Today** has no big number: it carries three small round buttons (today's note, **Start the day**, **Close the day**). A project, an area or a resource says, for example, "2 open tasks · 0 of 2 done". A goal's page says "40 %" with "of the way · Target 2027-08-15" under it. The band is the same on the Mac and the iPhone. By day it runs from the part's day colour to an end that is 18 % deeper (since build 316; before, to a slightly lighter end), so the white words are easy to read along the whole band. At night (since build 310) it is the deep day colour, running to a darker end, so the white words are easy to read; before, it was a dull, dusty colour at night. On **Today** the band's top row holds three small white circles: today's note, **Start the day** and **Close the day**. Their icons are in the deep day colour, by day and at night. On the iPhone every list screen (**Inbox**, **Plan**, **Actions**, **Goals**, **Projects**, **Calendar**, **Weekly review** and the others) opens with the same slim one-row band as **Today**, in its part's colour, with a count or the date in white (since build 310). On the Mac, the row you have chosen in the sidebar is filled in its part's deep day colour, with white words and a white count, by day and at night. Everything under the band is as before: the tinted panels, the 17-point coloured headings, the dots, the pop-ups, the text sizes and the icons. Nothing has moved and no function has changed.

Information sits in panels. A panel is white by day and dark grey at night, with a light tint of its part's colour over it (7 % by day, 13 % at night). At night it also has a thin line round it. Since build 310 the iPhone follows the same rule: the page is a very light grey by day and a soft dark grey at night, never pure black, and each card carries the tint of its part. The note's facts, its **Tasks** and its **Linked notes** are panels. Where you can change something, you see a bordered pop-up or a bordered button. Where you cannot, you see plain words. The main button of a screen is always in colour: it is filled in the deep day colour of its part, with white words, the same by day and at night. Since build 310 this is PARAGON's own button, so on the Mac it no longer turns grey when the window is behind another window. When it cannot be used yet, the fill is pale and its word is written in the part's colour, so you can still read it. Headings are in the colour of their part, not in grey capitals. Helper sentences, the second line of a row and small labels are in a soft grey that is easy to read (since build 310, darker by day than Apple's grey). The quietest words, such as the hours down the side of **Plan** and **Calendar**, are one step lighter than that and can still be read.

There is one set of text sizes. On the Mac the page title is 26 points (white, in the band at the top of the screen). The one big number in a band is 34 points, bold. A heading is 17 points, in the colour of its part. Text is 15 points and small labels are 12 points. Lists use 13 points, with 11 points for the one quiet second line of a row. The note editor uses 15 points for text, its headings step from 22 to 18 to 15 points, and code is 14 points. Edit and Read show the note at the same size. The header, the panels and the text of a note stop at 680 points wide and sit in the middle, so lines are easy to follow on a big screen. On the iPhone the text uses Apple's own styles, so it grows and shrinks with the text size you set in the iPhone's settings. Every icon is a hand-drawn mark in one line weight, in the app, the widgets and the share sheet. Since build 311 an icon takes its size from the text beside it: 16 points beside normal text, 12 points beside small text, 32 points beside the largest title. A small icon keeps a line of 1.3 points and never gets thinner. On the iPhone the icons beside text grow with it when you make text larger in the iPhone's settings; icons inside buttons that have no words, in the bars and in boxes of a fixed size keep their size. Each icon means one thing (since build 311): the ring is an open task (and also the status **Active** and a choice not picked), the ticked list is **All actions**, the four squares are **Areas**, the folder is **Browse**, the day line with two blocks is **Plan** and **Time blocks**, the round arrows with a tick are the **Weekly review**, the two pages are **Templates**, the hourglass is waiting, a plain clock face is a time of day on a task, a dash in a ring is **Dropped** or reminders deleted, and a cross in a ring is missed or cancelled. Apple's own back arrow, the small arrows at the end of a row, the fold triangles, the search field and the sidebar button are left as they are: they belong to the frame the iPhone and the Mac draw. An Apple symbol you chose for a note before build 311 stays until you change it (see **Your own icon**). On the Mac, **Search** is the one field in the toolbar at the top of the window, on every screen (since build 314 there is no **Search** row in the sidebar and no search field in the lists) (since build 299 there is no separate **Quick capture** button there; **New** does it; the screen was called New note before build 301) (Sync is the small clock ring with a word at the foot of the sidebar; a pill from build 292, a ring since build 301), and the **+** **New** button stays over the list column. On **Plan** and on the **Calendar**'s right-hand column, the page's own buttons are in that toolbar too: the day arrows with **Today**, **+ Block**, **Week** and the calendar-blocks button; **+ Block time** and the **Note** / **Schedule** switch. On the iPhone and the iPad they stay in the page. **Start the day** and **Close the day** stay in Today's top row, now as white circles in the band, and **Show all** stays in the Goals header. The version shows in **Settings › About** on every device (since build 315, as **Version**, for example 1.0 (316)), at the foot of the Mac sidebar and at the foot of **Browse** on the iPhone, as PARAGON 1.0 (315).

**An empty screen shows a way forward** (since build 315). Under the icon and the title is one small button in the part's colour, such as **Make a line wait…**, **Capture something…**, **New goal…** or **Open today's note**. A screen that fills by itself (**Deleted**, **Archive**, **Recent**, **Tags**, **Snippets**) has one short grey line that says how something gets there, like "A note you delete is kept here for 30 days". Before build 315 an empty list was an icon and one title.

A row in a list is a coloured dot (or the note's own icon), one title, and at most one quiet second line, for example "0 of 2 · Due in 12 d". That goes for every list, the **Projects** list included, and for the rows in **Goals** and **Aspirations**. In a list that mixes parts (**Search**, **Recent**, **Done** and the **Map**), the row shows the small icon of its part in place of the dot (since build 310), so you can tell the parts apart without the colour. A status such as Paused or Someday is a small capsule at the right. By day the words in a capsule are a deeper shade of its colour, so the smallest words are easy to read. An empty screen shows the part's icon in a soft circle of the part's colour, above one calm title (since build 291 the explaining sentence is gone; a warning such as "Still coming from iCloud" keeps its sentence). Counts beside headings, for example "Calendar 1", are small capsules in the heading's colour. The old text continues: the explanation in small grey, and a quiet bordered button where there is one.
**Dates and times (since build 309).** PARAGON writes every date one way.

- A date is **2026-11-12**: year, month, day.
- Where the weekday helps, it comes first: **Thursday 2026-11-12**. You see this form on **Today**, in **Start the day** and **Close the day**, on a day's heading in **Calendar**, **Calendar › Week** and **Plan**, on a day's card in **Done**, as the name of a daily note, in the due line of **Quick capture**, and in the next reminder in Settings.
- All other dates are plain: a target date, a deadline, a due date on a row, the day a project ended, a follow-up date, a backup, the sync line.
- A month alone is **2026-11**. A week is **Week 46**.
- A time is always 24 hours: **14:30**. It does not follow the region of your device.
- The weekday is always English, in full (**Thursday**) or short (**Thu**) where there is little room.
- **Today**, **Tomorrow** and **Yesterday** stay words where PARAGON used them before.
- This is only how PARAGON shows a date. Your files keep their names (`20261112.md`, `2026-W46.md`), and nothing inside a note is changed. The heading PARAGON writes into a new daily note is as before, for example "# Thursday, 12 November 2026", and into a new weekly note "# Week 46, 9 Nov – 15 Nov 2026".

**One name for each thing (since build 309).** The screen with your day on an hour ruler is **Plan** everywhere; the lane inside it is **Time blocks**. The screen that adds something is **New**, and its sixth button is **Inbox**. A button that adds one thing at once says **Add**. A screen with fields to fill in ends with **Save**. A line that says nothing is there, such as "No events" or "Nothing due today", is always small and grey, with no full stop.

**VoiceOver and text size (since build 309).** Every picture button has a name that VoiceOver reads, also **Previous day** and **Next day** in the Calendar, **Delete this one for good** in **Deleted**, **Open in Calendar** on an event, and **What you can write in a capture** in Quick capture. The text in the widgets follows your text size. Since build 311 the icons beside text do too: on the iPhone they grow when you make text larger. Icons in buttons without words, in the bars and in fixed boxes keep their size.

**Colours by day (since build 316).** Three things were made easier to read by day. Warning words in orange are a darker orange, #B35300 (before, Apple's orange #FF9500, which was too pale on white); at night they are #FFA23A, as bright as before. The small words on a band are full white. On the iPhone, a card carries 12 % of its part's colour by day (before 7 %), so you can tell a card from the page; in **Settings** and **Today** a card is #EDEAF5 on the page #F3F5F8. The night colours, and the cards on the Mac and the iPad, are unchanged. The exact values are in **Exact details (build 316)**.

## Movement

Since build 318. PARAGON moves only to show what changed: a ticked task fades and the list closes up, a group folds under its heading, a bar fills. Nothing bounces, glows or celebrates. Every movement takes between 0.15 and 0.35 seconds. The note is always written first, at once; the movement only draws what has already happened.

- **Ticking a task.** The row fades out and the list closes up in 0.25 seconds. Where a ticked task stays on the screen (a note's **Tasks** box, **Done**), the circle fills in 0.2 seconds and the words go grey in 0.2 seconds. A second press on a row that is fading away does nothing.
- **A row that leaves or moves** (a task moved to another day or another note, a deleted Inbox line, **Next action** on and off): it fades out where it was and the list closes up, in 0.25 seconds. If it moves to another group on the same screen, it fades in there.
- **A new line** (**Quick capture**, **Add to Inbox**, a new task or sub-task in a note): the list makes room and the line fades in, in 0.25 seconds.
- **Folding a group.** A group opens and closes in 0.2 seconds, and its small arrow turns with it.
- **The pages of a window** (**Date · Deadline · Repeat**, **Day · Week · Month · Notes**, **Actions | Waiting for**, **How it works | What's new** on the Mac) fade into each other in 0.2 seconds.
- **The focus timer's bar** comes up from the foot of the screen in 0.25 seconds and goes back down in 0.25 seconds.
- **Progress bars and the sync ring** fill to the new value in 0.35 seconds. While a sync runs the ring turns once a second.
- **Small controls** (buttons with 2 states, filter chips in **Search**, cards on the **Map**) change in 0.15 seconds.
- **The message at the foot of the screen** slides up in 0.2 seconds.
- **Reduce Motion.** When **Reduce Motion** is on in the device's Accessibility settings, nothing slides, grows or turns anywhere in PARAGON, and the sync ring does not turn. The screen changes at once. Only the message at the foot of the screen still fades in and out. On the iPhone and the iPad the switch is in **Settings › Accessibility › Motion**. On the Mac it is in **System Settings › Accessibility**.
- **Not animated, on purpose.** The Mac's sidebar, the groups of the **Projects** list and the **Templates** list. Showing and hiding the note with the **Note** button. Text while you type. The line through a ticked task appears at once; only the colour fades. There are no new taps you can feel.

## Your vault

The vault is one folder. Pick it once under Settings › Vault. Inside it the app keeps:

Since build 316, **Settings › Vault** shows the folder in plain words: its name and where it is, for example "PARAGON Vault · in iCloud Drive". The place is "in iCloud Drive", "in Dropbox", or "on this Mac" (on the iPhone "on this iPhone", on the iPad "on this iPad"). On the Mac the button **Show in Finder**, under **Choose folder…**, opens Finder with the vault folder chosen, and the full path shows when the pointer rests on the row. Before build 316 the row showed the raw path.

Since build 319, **Settings › Vault** has more rows. Which ones you see depends on where your notes are.
- On the iPhone and the iPad, **Show in Files** opens the Files app at the vault folder. It is there for every vault.
- While your notes are in a folder you chose: **Copy to PARAGON's own folder…**, with the grey line "PARAGON can copy the notes into a folder of its own in iCloud Drive and open it by itself on every device. Your old folder stays as it is. Nothing happens until you say so." The row is shown only when PARAGON can reach its folder in iCloud Drive.
- When another device has copied the notes to a new place: **Your notes are in a new place…** instead.
- When the notes are in PARAGON's own folder: the row says "PARAGON · in iCloud Drive", with the line "PARAGON keeps your notes in its own folder, iCloud Drive › PARAGON, and opens it by itself on every device." While the old folder can still be reached, there is also a line such as "Your old folder “PARAGON Vault” is still there, as it was on 2026-11-14. PARAGON only reads it." and the button **Go back to the old folder…**.
- **Choose folder…** and **Close vault** are as before.
The chapter **PARAGON's own iCloud folder**, below, explains all of this.

- `Inbox.md`
- `Goals/`, `Projects/`, `Areas/`, `Resources/`, `Archive/`
- `Calendar/` for daily notes (`20260906.md`) and weekly notes (`2026-W36.md`)
- `Templates/` for the note skeletons, which you can edit
- `.ams-para/` for the app's own settings and sync bookkeeping

You can open the same folder in NotePlan, Obsidian or any text editor. If the folder lives in iCloud Drive, your iPhone can use the same vault.

**Choosing a folder looks first (since build 308).** When you choose a folder, PARAGON looks inside before it writes anything and a sheet tells you what it found. While it looks it says "Looking at this folder…" and "Nothing is written." A folder that is already a vault says "This folder has 1,412 notes." and "It is a PARAGON vault.", with the button **Open it**. Any other folder says "This folder is not a PARAGON vault.", "It has 12 notes of its own. They stay as they are." (when it has notes) and "Setting it up adds the PARAGON folders, an Inbox note and templates.", with the button **Set it up here**. While iCloud is still listing the folder it says "iCloud is still listing this folder." and "PARAGON is waiting and has written nothing. This can take a minute."; it never calls a folder empty that is only still arriving, and a caution line appears when an iCloud folder may not be fully listed yet. A folder that cannot be read says "PARAGON cannot read this folder." and "Nothing was written. Choose another folder." **Cancel** (Escape on the Mac) leaves everything as it was. Starting the app and **Open … again** open your folder directly, without the sheet.

**Very long titles (since build 308).** A note with a very long title gets a shortened file name in the folder (at most 180 bytes). The full title is kept in the note's `title:` line and shown everywhere in PARAGON, and links find the note by that title. If two shortened names would be the same, the later ones get " 2", " 3".

## PARAGON's own iCloud folder

Since build 319. PARAGON has a folder of its own in iCloud Drive. Files and Finder show it as **PARAGON**, and PARAGON writes it as "iCloud Drive › PARAGON". A device that uses this folder opens it by itself at every start. There is no folder to choose and no folder to lose.

**Nothing is copied by itself.** Your notes stay in the folder you chose until you press **Copy my notes**. You copy them 1 time, on 1 device. The other devices follow.

**It is a copy, and the old folder is always kept.** PARAGON copies your notes, checks every file, and then uses the copy. Your old folder stays as it is. PARAGON never deletes the old folder and never deletes a note in it. You delete it yourself, when you are sure.

**First, on every device.** Open PARAGON 319 or newer 1 time on your iPhone, your iPad and your Mac. Each device then writes 1 small file into the notes folder that says "I have build 319". PARAGON does not copy the notes while a device is not ready.

**The offer.** Open **Settings › Vault** and press **Copy to PARAGON's own folder…**. The page **Copy your notes to PARAGON's own folder** opens. Opening it copies nothing. It says:
- what happens: "PARAGON copies your notes, checks every file, and then uses the copy. Your old folder stays as it is." Then the steps: a backup copy is made and checked, then your notes, pictures and settings are copied, and every file is compared with its original; only then does PARAGON switch;
- that your old folder stays as it is, with 1 small text file added that says where the notes went;
- that Reminders sync is paused while the notes are copied and goes on afterwards;
- which devices use your notes, 1 line each, for example "Martin's Mac: PARAGON 319, this device" and "iPhone: PARAGON 319, seen 2026-11-14".

The buttons are **Cancel** and **Copy my notes**. On the Mac, Escape is **Cancel**.

**When the main button is Check again.** PARAGON does not start the copy in these 3 cases, and the button **Copy my notes** is replaced by **Check again**:
- A device has not opened build 319 yet. The list shows "Another device: has not opened PARAGON 319 or newer yet, last synced 2026-11-10". Open PARAGON on that device, then press **Check again**.
- Another device started copying less than 1 day ago and has not finished. The page names the device. Finish it there, or wait 1 day.
- PARAGON cannot reach iCloud Drive on this device. Check that you are signed in to iCloud and that iCloud Drive is on for PARAGON.

**The copy, in this order.** The page is called **Copying your notes** and shows each step with a count.
1. Reminders sync is paused, and the text you are writing is saved.
2. "Reading your notes: 212 files…": every file is read, and fetched from iCloud when needed.
3. A backup copy is made outside the notes folder, in the place of your second copy, and checked file by file.
4. PARAGON checks that the device has room, and marks the new folder as "being filled".
5. "Copying 87 of 412 files…": every file is copied, the hidden folder `.ams-para` too.
6. "Comparing copies and originals: 300 of 824 files…": both folders are listed again and compared. Each file is counted 2 times, 1 time in each folder.
7. "Sending to iCloud: 87 of 412 files…": PARAGON waits until iCloud has taken every file, 20 minutes at most.
8. Both folders are compared 1 more time, so that what you changed during the wait is in the copy too.
9. "Finishing…": the new folder is marked "complete", the text file **PARAGON has moved these notes.txt** is added to the old folder, PARAGON opens the new folder, and Reminders sync goes on. (The name of this file still says "moved", because devices with build 319 look for exactly this name. The text inside it says "copied".)

**Stop** works until "Finishing…". If you stop, your notes stay where they are.

**The end.** The page **Your notes are in PARAGON's own folder** says how many notes and files were copied and checked, for example "125 notes, 412 files in all (14 MB): every one copied and checked." Press **Done**.

**When the copy is stopped halfway.** You pressed **Stop**, PARAGON was closed, the connection was lost, or a check failed. Then your notes are where they were: PARAGON goes on using the old folder, and Reminders sync goes on. The files copied so far stay in iCloud Drive › PARAGON with the mark "being filled". No device opens a folder with that mark as your notes. The next try from the same device goes on where it stopped. Another device may take the copy over after 1 day.

**On your other devices.** A device that still uses the old folder shows a bar at the top of the window:
- "Your notes are in a new place. Waiting for iCloud: 87 of 125 files." with **More…**, while iCloud still brings the files. You can go on working.
- "Your notes are in a new place: iCloud Drive › PARAGON." with **Open…**, when everything is there.

The page **Your notes are in a new place** says which device copied them and on which day, for example "On 2026-11-14 “MacBook Pro” copied them to iCloud Drive › PARAGON." Press **Open the new folder**. What you changed on this device since then comes along first. Then PARAGON switches and says **Your notes are open in their new place**. **Not now** closes the page; the bar stays.

**Reminders sync is paused there until the device has followed.** From the moment a device sees that the notes are in a new place, it does not sync the old folder with Reminders. **Sync now** then says "Reminders sync is paused: your notes are in another folder now." After **Open the new folder** the sync goes on by itself.

**Changes in the old folder after the copy.** A device with an older PARAGON, or a device that has not followed yet, still writes into the old folder. Every device that has copied the notes or followed looks at the old folder when it opens your notes, and then 1 time an hour at most. When files of yours changed there, a bar says "3 files were changed in the old folder since the copy." with **Look…**. The page lists them, with **Not now** and **Bring them over**. The rules of **Bring them over**:
- a new file is copied;
- a note changed only in the old folder, after the copy, replaces the one in the new folder; the replaced text is kept in a set-aside folder first;
- a note changed in both folders is kept 2 times; the old folder's version gets "(conflict 2026-11-14 1412)" in its name;
- an older copy never replaces a newer note; both are kept;
- nothing is deleted. A file deleted in the old folder stays in the new one.

The old folder is only read.

**Going back.** On a device that uses PARAGON's folder, and while the old folder can be reached, **Settings › Vault** has **Go back to the old folder…**. The page **Go back to the old folder** has **Cancel** and **Go back**. It is the same copy in the other direction: changes waiting in the old folder are brought over first, a backup copy is made and checked, everything is copied to the old folder, and every file is compared. A file in the old folder that is replaced, or that is no longer among your notes, is set aside in `.ams-para/Set aside by a move/`. It is not deleted. Your other devices follow by themselves, and iCloud Drive › PARAGON stays as it is.

**A new device, or a device that lost its folder.** Before the welcome screen, PARAGON looks into its own folder:
- Complete notes are there: **I found your notes here — use them?** with, for example, "iCloud Drive › PARAGON: 125 notes, last changed 2026-11-13." Press **Use them**. **Choose a different folder…** is there too.
- The notes are still arriving: "Your notes are in iCloud Drive › PARAGON and are still arriving on this iPhone: 87 of 412 files are here."
- A copy did not finish: PARAGON says so, and by which device. Finish it on that device.
- Nothing is there: the welcome screen with **Choose a vault folder…**, as before.

**Seeing the folder.** **Settings › Vault › Show in Files** on the iPhone and the iPad, **Show in Finder** on the Mac.

**Obsidian and other apps.** They may open and read iCloud Drive › PARAGON. Please do not let another app write into the old or the new folder while the notes are copied.

**What build 319 does even if you never copy your notes.** Each device writes 1 small file into the notes folder, in `.ams-para/devices/`. And PARAGON looks for the marks of a copy before every Reminders sync and every 30 seconds; this look only reads. A folder **PARAGON** may appear in iCloud Drive; it is empty until you copy your notes.

The exact rules, numbers and messages are in **Exact details (build 319)**. Build 320 changed the words from "move" to "copy"; the list is in **Exact details (build 320)**.

## Notes

A note starts with a small block of settings between two `---` lines, then the text. The settings are plain `key: value` lines, for example:

```
---
title: Perform IM Jönköping 2027
type: project
status: active
area: Health
goal: Train for and do IM 70.3 for five years
due: 2027-08-15
tags: [sport, training]
---
```

Useful keys: `status` (active, on hold, done, missed, dropped — see **How a note stands**), `area`, `goal`, `due`, `tags`, `icon` and `icon-colour` (a note's own icon: a project, an area, a goal, an aspiration or a resource, see **Your own icon**), `related`, `reviewed`, `order` (where the note sits when you have arranged the list by hand), `reminders-list` to sync into a Reminders list with a different name, `sync: false` to keep a note out of Reminders.

Several of these have a button at the top of the note as well, and it writes the same line: **Serves…** for `goal`, **In area…** for `area` (projects only), **Part of…** for `parent`, **Due** for `due`, **Tags…** for `tags`, and **Icon…** for `icon` and `icon-colour` (projects, areas, goals, aspirations and resources). On a goal, **Set a target date…** writes `target` and **Set a measure…** writes `measure`. On an aspiration, a goal or an area, **I looked at it today** writes `reviewed` (see **When you last looked at it**).

The top of a note is the band in its part's colour: the title (26 points, bold, white), the small white words (for example "2 open tasks · 0 of 2 done") and the big number. A note without tasks used to say its kind twice, in the band and in the panel's heading. Now the band says something else: a daily note says how far the day is from today (**Today**, **Yesterday**, **Tomorrow**, **3 days ago**, **In 3 days**), and any other note without tasks says **No tasks yet**. Under the band is a panel of facts in the note's colour. The panel's heading says what kind of note it is, for example **Project**. The note's icon sits beside it. Inside are the facts, each a small grey label with its value after it on the same line, level with the buttons beside them (**Tags…**, **Status**): **Status**, **In area** or **Part of**, **Serves**, **Horizon**, **Target**, **Measure**, **Looked at**, **Deadline**, **Tags**, **Reminders list:** and **Icon**. Only the ones that fit the kind of note are shown. A fact you can change is a bordered pop-up or button. A fact you cannot change is plain words. Before this look, the same facts were a row of small chips, and until build 290 the label sat over its value. On the Mac and the iPad, a goal's page in **Goals** and an aspiration's page in **Aspirations** have the same title and the same panel (see **Changing a goal's date and measure**). When the Mac window is short, this top part of the note, the title, the facts panel, **Tasks** and **Linked notes**, scrolls on its own, so the text under it always keeps at least 60 points of room.

You do not have to look at the block while you write. Since build 312 it is folded on every note. Above the text one line says **Settings block hidden**; press it and the block opens, and the line says **Settings block**. Press it again to fold the block away. Before build 312 the block was showing until you folded it; on the iPhone it took about half the screen. Build 312 folds it once, the first time it starts; if you open it after that, it stays open. The block stays in the file either way, and the buttons at the top of the note keep writing into it. The choice holds for every note until you change it.

**⌘Z** undoes your typing in the note you are in. Each note has its own undo, so after you open another note, ⌘Z does not reach back into the one you left. When anything other than your typing changes the note while it is open — your other device, **Settings block**, a tick or a new task in the task box, a button at the top of the note, or a title picked from the `[[` list — its undo starts again from that point. On the Mac, the grey word suggestions macOS can show after the cursor, and the grey answer after a sum such as "120+35=", are switched off in notes, because they made PARAGON crash. Spelling is still checked and underlined.

Links between notes use `[[Note title]]`; see **Linking notes with [[ ]]** below.

### Making one

**⌘N** opens one screen with six buttons across the top: **Aspiration**, **Goal**, **Project**, **Area**, **Resource** and **Inbox**, each in its own colour. The kind you chose has a filled disc and a frame. The others show their icon in the full line colour on a clear ground, inside a thin grey outline (since build 311; before, pale icons on pale discs). Under them is the name field with the cursor already in it, so a note is usually a name and Return, and one line says what the kind you chose is for.

Under that, everything else a note can be given as it is made, each choice with its small label. **Cancel** and **Save** are side by side at the top right of the window on the Mac, and **Save** is filled in the colour of the kind. **Escape** is Cancel, **Return** and **⌘S** are Save, and **Tab** moves from one text field to the next, as it does everywhere on the Mac. The choice chips are buttons, so Tab does not stop on each one. On the iPhone, **Cancel** is on the left of the bar and **Save** on the right. **Make this note** (from a link) has the same bar.

- **Aspiration** — the aspiration it serves is not asked for: an aspiration is the top of the chain. Only its name and its tags.
- **Goal** — a **Target** date and the aspiration it serves. Write the date as 2031-12-01 or pick it from the calendar. The two buttons carry the two icons: the star for an aspiration, the target for a goal with a date. The measure is not asked for here. Set it later with **Set a measure…** at the top of the goal.
- **Project** — the goal it delivers, and **In area…**: the area it is part of. The list also has a field to make a new area without leaving the screen: type the name and press **Add area**. A name already in use chooses that area.
- **Area** — the area it is part of, and the aspiration it serves.
- **Tags** — the whole list of tags you already have, with a tick on the ones you pick and a number beside each saying how much of your vault carries it, so a tag that means the same as one you already use but is spelled differently stands out. A field at the top makes a tag that does not exist yet. It is the same list the tag button in a note's header opens.
- **Template** — which template to start from, when that kind has more than one.

Only the choices that apply to the kind you have chosen are shown.

Two lists have a field at the top to make a new thing: **Serves…** (a goal or an aspiration) and **In area…** (an area). They work the same way. If a thing of that kind already has the name, it is chosen and nothing new is made. If the name cannot be used, the box says why in orange and your words stay in the field. For example, an aspiration and a goal with a date cannot have the same name, because both are kept in the **Goals** folder. And a goal that is Done, Missed or Dropped, and still in the **Goals** folder, is not in the list, so it cannot be chosen: the box tells you, and you use another name. A goal or an area that you put away with **Archive** is not looked at: its name can be used again, because the archived note is parked in the **Archive** folder.

**Which choice it starts on (build 299).** In a section with its own kind (Projects, Areas, Resources, Goals, Aspirations) the screen starts on that kind. On Today it starts on **Inbox**. Everywhere else (Inbox, Calendar, Search and so on) it starts on the choice you used last time, not always Project.

**Inbox** is the last button. It turns the lower half into the quick capture screen, so the same screen takes a project and a line you must not forget. See **Quick capture** below; since build 299 this is the only capture button in the Mac and iPad window (the tray is gone), and **⇧⌘N** still opens it directly. The menu bar capture and the iPhone's one **+** are as before.

### Renaming a note

Press the note's **name**, the big title at the top of the note. Or right-click the note in the list (long-press on the phone) › **Rename…**; on the iPhone it is also in the **…** menu at the top of the note. The file is renamed with it, and every note that pointed at the old name — `goal:`, `area:`, `parent:`, `related:` or a `[[wikilink]]` — is updated, so no link comes loose. The note's own `# Heading` follows when it still said the old name.

A name another note already uses is refused. A rename that only changes capitals ("website" to "Website") works since build 308; before, it was refused as a name already in use. In Apple Reminders the list follows on the next sync; the tasks keep their reminders. The Inbox and daily notes cannot be renamed.

### Archive and Deleted

- **Archive** moves a project, area, resource or goal to the Archive folder, marks it archived and stops syncing its tasks. On the Mac it is **Move to Archive** in the note's toolbar: the archive box with a small arrow going into it (since build 311 one drawing, as tall as the buttons beside it; before, an arrow stacked on a box). Since build 312 the word **Archive** is beside the drawing on the Mac; the iPad keeps the drawing alone. Use it for finished work. A note that is **Done**, **Missed** or **Dropped** keeps that word and the day it ended, so an archived project that was done still counts for its goal. Any other note is marked archived.
- **Delete** (⌘⌫, or right-click the note; long-press on the phone) moves a note to the **Deleted** section in the sidebar. If the note has open reminders in Apple Reminders, PARAGON asks once (since build 307): remove those reminders too, or keep them. Either way the repeating "missing or unreadable" warnings stop. **Put back** links the reminders to the note again. Nothing is lost: each entry has **Put back**, which returns it to the folder it came from (or beside it, if that name has been taken since), and **Delete for good**. **Empty** in the toolbar clears the lot.
- Deleted notes are kept for **30 days**, then cleared out on their own. They live in a hidden folder inside the vault, so the phone and the Mac behave the same way and nothing there syncs to Reminders. The Inbox note cannot be deleted.
- Only whole notes go here. A task you delete from a list is gone from the note straight away. A task deleted in the Inbox has an **Undo** for a short time; see **Undo for one-press changes**.

### Which aspiration an area serves

An area can say which aspiration it belongs to, and a project which goal it delivers: **Serves…** at the top of the note, or right-click it in the list. A project is offered the dated goals first, an area the aspirations first. The list is the same as on the **Goals** and **Aspirations** screens: only goals that are still running, in their order, and never the note itself. A goal the note already serves stays in the list even if it has ended, with its ending in brackets, such as (Done), so the tick is never lost. That is what makes Endurance the home of "an endurance athlete still racing at seventy" rather than leaving the aspiration floating on its own. An area is allowed to serve nothing — it is a standard you keep up, not a path to an outcome — so the review never asks about an area without one.

### Which area a project is in

Open a project and press **In area…** at the top. Pick an area, for example Health. The button then says **In Health**, in pink. A sub-area is listed after its own area, as **Health › Yoga**. **In no area** takes the project out again, and **Open Health** goes to the area.

- The same list is on the right-click menu of a project in the **Projects** list (press and hold on the iPhone) › **In area**.
- On the **New** screen, a new project has the same **In area…** button. Its list also has a field to make a new area without leaving the screen: type the name and press **Add area**. A name already in use chooses that area; no second one is made.
- It is kept in the note's settings block as `area: Health`. You can also write that line yourself.
- **Only this line puts a project in an area.** A `[[Health]]` link in the project's text, or Health in its `related:` line, links the two notes, but the project is not in Health because of it.
- If the line names an area that does not exist, for example after a spelling mistake, the button shows the name in grey, and its list says **No area is called …**. Pick the right area to fix it.
- If the area it names has been archived, the button says **(archived)** after the name, and its list says the area is archived and offers to open it.
- While notes are still coming from iCloud, the button is orange, and its list says the area may still be coming from iCloud instead of **No area is called …**. Wait for it to arrive before you change anything.
- On the **Map**, the project sits under the area you chose. The **Projects** list does not change: it stays grouped by aspiration.

### Find in a note

Since build 315. On the Mac press **⌘F**, or choose **Edit › Find in Note**. A find bar appears above the text: type a word, and use the arrows, Return, or **⌘G** and **⇧⌘G** to go to the next and the previous place. The bar shows how many places it found. On the iPhone open the note's **…** menu and choose **Find in note**. The find bar belongs to the text you edit, so a note in **Read** is turned to **Edit** first, and stays in **Edit**. To search all notes, use **Search** (**⇧⌘F** on the Mac).

Since build 316 the iPad has it too: the toolbar over an open note has a **Find in note** button, a magnifying glass, right after the pencil. It opens the same find bar as on the iPhone. On the iPhone, while the find bar is up, the top bar is solid, so the note's title no longer lies on top of the text.

### Share a note

Since build 315. Choose **Share…** (in the note's toolbar on the Mac and the iPad, between **Archive** and **Delete**; in the **…** menu on the iPhone) and pick one:

- **As PDF** makes A4 pages that look like **Read**, with tasks as boxes and dates like 2026-11-13. The pages are always light, also at night.
- **As plain text** is what you wrote, without the settings block at the top.
- **The file itself** is the note's own .md file.

PARAGON always hands over a copy, never the note in your vault, so nothing another app does can change your note. What you typed last is saved first. On the Mac, **Show in Finder** in the same menu shows where the note's file is.

## The Projects list

The **Projects** list groups your projects by what they work towards.

- **Under an aspiration.** A project that serves an aspiration, or serves a goal with a date
  that serves an aspiration, sits under that aspiration's heading. The heading is in the aspiration's gold, with
  the star and the count. The aspirations come in the same order as in **Aspirations**.
- **Not linked to an aspiration.** The project serves a goal, but that goal has no aspiration
  yet, or the `goal:` line names a goal that cannot be found (drawn with a dashed line). To fix
  the first, go to **Goals** and press **Give it an aspiration** under the goal. To fix the
  second, right-click the project (press and hold on the iPhone) › **Serves** and choose a goal.
- **Projects with no goal.** No `goal:` line at all. Right-click the project (press and hold
  on the iPhone) › **Serves** to give it one. This is not an error; the list only says so.
  Its heading is grey.
- **Done**, **Missed** and **Dropped** at the bottom, folded at first. An empty group is not
  shown. A project that says `status: archived` but is still in the Projects folder gets an
  **Archived** group at the very end.

A group's heading is a 17-point heading in the colour of what it is: the aspiration's gold,
grey for **Projects with no goal**. The count sits beside it, and there is no band behind it.
Press a heading to fold the group or to open it again.

Above the groups, one line speaks for the whole list: bars for all projects together, how many
tasks were finished in the last eight weeks and this week ("14 tasks finished in 8 weeks · 2
this week"), and "⚠ 1 needs a look" in orange when a project has a date that has passed or
a deadline after its goal's target date.

### What a row says

Each project is one row, like every other list in PARAGON. From left to right:

- The **mark**: the project's own icon, or a small green dot until you pick one. On
  **Automatic** the colour is green while the project is running or done, and grey when it is
  on hold, missed, dropped or archived. See **Your own icon** below.
- The **name**, on one line.
- One quiet **second line** in grey: how far it has come, then what it needs.
  - **0 of 2 · Next:** and the task to do next. That is the open task marked `#next`, or else
    the first open task in the note. When every task is ticked but a subtask under one of them
    is still open, it is that subtask. The Goals and Aspirations screens and **Plan** use the
    same rule. The `#next` itself is not shown there. A line you are waiting on somebody else
    for is never the next task, not even with `#next` on it (see **Waiting for** under **All
    actions**).
  - **All 15 finished · No next action** (or **Its one task is finished**): every task is
    ticked, but the project still says it is active. Either mark it done, or write its next
    task. The grey is on purpose; the weekly review is where this is asked with orange.
  - **Its one open task is waiting for someone · No next action** (or **All 3 open tasks are
    waiting for others**): every task that is still open is a line you are waiting on somebody
    else for. There is nothing there for you to do yourself, so no task is "next". It does
    not say "finished", because the project is not.
  - **No tasks yet**.
  - A project that has ended says the day instead, for example **Ended 2026-10-20 · 2 tasks
    finished** (see **Finished projects, newest first**).
- **Weekly bars**, at the right of the row: eight small green bars, the tasks finished in
  each of the last eight weeks, with this week on the right. A week is Monday to Sunday, as
  in the weekly notes. A task counts on the day in its `@done(…)` mark; subtasks count too.
  A project where no finished task carries a date shows no bars.
- A small **capsule** at the right edge when the project is **Paused**, **Done**, **Missed**
  or **Dropped**.

Everything else, the goal it serves, the deadline, the tags and the next task's date, is in
the project's facts panel, at the top of the note.

A heading shows an orange ⚠ and a number when a project in it has a date that has passed or
a deadline later than its goal's target date. "No next action" is never orange.

### Finished projects, newest first

- In **Done**, **Missed**, **Dropped** and **Archived**, the project that ended last is at the top.
- The order comes from the `ended:` line, the day you marked the project. Two projects that ended on the same day keep your own order.
- A project that ended before build 262 has no `ended:` line. Those sit at the bottom of their group, in your own order.
- Each row in these groups says the day it ended in its second line, for example **Ended 2026-10-20 · 2 tasks finished**. The year is always part of the date (since build 309). A project with no day shows no date there.
- The **Done** heading also says how many of the projects in it ended this year, for example **Done 6 · 4 this year**. When the two numbers would be the same, only the second is shown: **Done · 1 this year** (since build 309; before, "Done 1 · 1 this year"). "This year" means since 1 January. Only projects with an `ended:` day are counted. A project you have archived is no longer in the Projects list, so it is not counted. When none ended this year, nothing is shown.
- **Missed** and **Dropped** do not get this count. Only the order and the day.
- A finished row does not show a next task, so the day it ended is the only date on it.
- To change the day of a project in **Done**, **Missed** or **Dropped**, open the project and press the line under its header (see **How a note stands**). In **Archived** there is no such line: change the `ended:` line in the note's settings block.

### What you can do from the list

- **Click** a project to open it, as before.
- **Right-click** (press and hold on the iPhone): **Rename…**, **Serves**, **In area**, **Set active**,
  **Mark done**, **Mark missed**, **Mark dropped**, **Put on hold**, **Archive** and **Move to
  Trash…**. Only the lines that would change something are shown. A project you mark done
  moves into **Done** at the bottom of the list, where the projects are in the order they
  ended (see **Finished projects, newest first**).
- **Drag** a project up or down inside its group to arrange it by hand. A drag never moves a
  project into another group, and the projects in the other groups keep their places. The
  order is written into the note as `order:`, so the Mac and the iPhone agree. **Done**,
  **Missed** and **Dropped** cannot be arranged: they are in the order the projects ended.
- Drop a task from another list onto a project to move the task into it.
- **Search** in Projects shows the plain list of every project, as before. While a search is
  shown, projects cannot be dragged: the list then holds only the matches, so a new order could
  not be worked out for the rest.

The number beside **Projects** in the sidebar counts every project except those marked Done,
Missed, Dropped or archived. A project on hold is counted.

### Your own icon

Each project, area, goal, aspiration and resource can have its own icon. Open the note and press **Icon…** at the top, right after the kind of note (**Projects**, **Areas**, **Goal with a date**, **Aspiration** or **Resources**). On the Mac and the iPad, a goal or an aspiration opens on its page first, and there **Icon…** is in the heading of the facts panel, beside the words **Goal with a date** or **Aspiration**. On the iPhone, press a goal with a date or a resource to open it. For an aspiration, press and hold its row and choose **Open the note**. A picker opens. From the top:

- **Colour**: **Automatic** and fifteen colours. **Automatic** is the colour of the kind of note: green for a project, pink for an area, gold for a goal with a date (a deeper gold for an aspiration), blue for a resource. Each colour is a small tile with the icon on it, so you see how it will look.
- **Icon**: a field called **Find an icon**, then **No icon**, then the icons in groups. They are the 134 hand-drawn icons (**Hand-drawn · Travel**, **Hand-drawn · Outdoors** and so on). Since build 311 Apple's symbols are no longer offered (before, about 305 of them came after the hand-drawn ones). A note that already has an Apple symbol keeps it until you change it. The cross that clears the field is hand-drawn too.
- **Find an icon** searches the names and some words that are not shown. English and Swedish words both work: **bike** and **cykel** find the same icon.
- One press on a colour, an icon or **No icon** is a choice. It is written to the note at once and the picker closes. To change the colour and the icon, open the picker twice.
- **Cancel** closes the picker and writes nothing. So does the Escape key on the Mac, when the search field is empty. (While something is typed there, Escape empties the field first.)
- **No icon** takes the icon away. The colour you chose stays. On a project, an area or a resource the tile then shows the first letter of the name. On an aspiration or a goal with a date it shows the star or the target on your colour. Press **Automatic** to take the colour away as well.

Where the icon is shown:

- At the left of the project's row in the **Projects** list, and in front of each project on its area's page.
- At the left of an area's row in the **Areas** list, and in front of a sub-area's row on its parent's page.
- At the left of a resource's row in the **Resources** list. A resource with no icon keeps its thin blue line.
- On an aspiration or a goal with a date, **instead of the star or the target**: at the left of its row in the **Aspirations** and **Goals** lists, at the top of the aspiration's page, in front of each goal in an aspiration's chain, and on the goal rows in steps 3 and 4 of the **Weekly review**. A goal with no icon keeps its star or target. A goal with only a colour shows its star or target on that colour.
- In **Recent**, in the **Archive** and when you search inside a list, where the row's thin coloured line is replaced by the icon. A note you put in the **Archive** keeps the icon it had; you can no longer choose a new one for it.
- In the button at the top of the note itself. It says **Icon…** in a dashed grey capsule until something is chosen. Once something is chosen, the button is the tile itself, 24 points, with a thin ring in the note's colour just outside it. A screen reader says "Icon: Star" or "Icon: Target" for a goal that has only a colour.

The **Map**, the sidebar, the **Search** screen and the lists of linked notes keep the symbols for the kind of note (the flag, the four squares, the books, the star and the target). There the symbol tells you what kind of note it is, and an icon of your own would hide that.

**To go back to the star or the target:** press **Icon…** on the goal or the aspiration, then **No icon**. The star or the target comes back, drawn on your colour if you chose one. To lose the colour as well, open the picker again and press **Automatic**.

An icon replaces an emoji:

- A project that already has an emoji from an earlier version keeps showing that emoji. Nothing is changed in a note you do not touch.
- When you choose an icon, a colour or **No icon** for that project, the old `emoji:` line is removed from the note. The picker is the only button that gives a project a mark.
- Pressing **Automatic** when the colour is already **Automatic** changes nothing, so the old emoji stays.

The choice is kept in the note's settings block, for example `icon: hand:bike` and `icon-colour: blue`. With **Automatic** there is no `icon-colour` line. You can also write the lines yourself: `hand:` and the icon's key, or `symbol:` and the name of an Apple symbol, for example `symbol:figure.run`. A line PARAGON cannot read, for example an icon from a newer version, stays in the note and is shown as a dashed circle.

## An area's page

Open an area, for example Health. Above its text there is a part that shows what the area holds and whether work in it is moving. Press **Icon…** at the top of the note to give the area its own icon and colour; see **Your own icon**.

- **Standard**: at the very top, the first sentence of what "good" looks like in this area, for example "Three runs a week, one rest day, a yearly check-up." It shows two lines at most. When there is more, it ends with "…".
- Press **Standard…** and the whole text opens right there. Write it, then press **Save**. **Cancel** closes the box and writes nothing.
- When no standard is written, a dashed grey line says **No standard written yet**, and the button says **Write the standard…**.
- The question the Area template puts under **Standard**, "What does "good" look like in this area?", is not a standard. It counts as nothing written. The same goes for the question in your own Area template, if you have reworded it.
- The standard is kept in the note's text, under the heading `## Standard`. **Save** puts your words in place of the question. A note with no such heading gets one, above its first section. Writing it by hand in the note's text works as well.
- **Save** with an empty box takes the standard away, heading and all. When no standard was written, it changes nothing, so the template's question stays.
- If the standard was changed on your other device while the box was open, nothing is written. An orange line says what it is now, and your words stay in the box. Press **Save** again to use yours instead.
- **Weekly bars**: eight small pink bars, the tasks finished in each of the last eight weeks, in the area itself and in every project in it. Beside them, for example, "14 tasks finished in 8 weeks · 2 this week". A week is Monday to Sunday. When no finished task carries a date, no bars are shown. When the last finished task is older than eight weeks, the bars are left out too, and one grey line says when it was, for example "last task finished about 3 months ago".
- **Projects in this area**: every project whose **In area…** names this area, in the order you arranged them in the **Projects** list. The number beside the heading says how many.
- Each project shows its tile with its icon, its name and what it needs, in the same words as its row in the **Projects** list: **Next:** and the task to do next, **All 3 finished** and a grey **No next action**, **All 2 open tasks are waiting for others** and a grey **No next action**, or **No tasks yet**.
- "2 overdue" is red: open tasks in the project whose date has passed. The deadline is red once it has passed, and grey before that. A project on hold says **On hold**.
- Projects that are done, missed, dropped or archived are not listed. One line under the list says how many there are.
- **Press a project** to open it. To come back to the area, press ⌃⌘← on the Mac, or the back arrow at the top on the iPhone.
- **Right-click** a project (press and hold on the iPhone) for **Serves**, **In area**, **Set active**, **Mark done**, **Mark missed**, **Mark dropped** and **Put on hold** — the same lines as in the **Projects** list.
- **Add a project…**, beside the heading, makes a new project that is already in this area. Write the name and press **Add project** or Return. You stay on the area, and the new project is listed at once.
- If a project with that name is already there, the box says so in orange and keeps your words. **Cancel** closes the box and makes nothing.
- **Sub-areas**: under the projects, one row for each sub-area, like "Yoga · 1 project · 3 open". The open number counts the open tasks in the sub-area and in its projects. Press the row to open the sub-area.
- The projects of a sub-area are listed on the sub-area's own page, not on its parent's, so each project is shown in one place. Their finished tasks are in the sub-area's bars too.
- Press **Projects in this area** to fold the list away, and press it again to bring it back. The choice holds for every area. **Add a project…** stays beside the heading while the list is folded. The sub-areas fold away with the list; the orange iCloud line stays.
- On the Mac the list is never taller than a short box. When there are many projects, scroll inside it; the text of the note keeps its room below.
- Only the `area:` line puts a project here. A project that only has `[[Health]]` in its text is not listed. See **Which area a project is in**.
- While notes are still coming from iCloud, an orange line under the heading says that more projects may appear. It stays on screen when the list is folded.

## Sub-areas

**Making one.** Open the area note and click **Part of…** in the top row, then pick the area it belongs to. "Not part of another area" takes it back out. The same choices sit on the right-click menu of an area in the list (long-press on the phone), and **New › Area** has a **Part of** chip for making one from the start.

A sub-area is an ordinary area note with one extra line in its frontmatter, `parent: Health`. That means it behaves like any other area:

- Its own tasks and its own list in Apple Reminders.
- Its own box on the Map, drawn under the area it belongs to.
- A destination of its own in the Inbox "File it" column and in a task's **Move to** menu, where it reads "Health › Mobility".

In the Areas list a sub-area is indented under its area, and the chevron on the area folds its sub-areas away. Dragging to arrange works inside a family: a sub-area moves among its brothers and sisters, an area among the other areas. The note itself shows **Part of Health** at the top — click it to go there. The parent's own page lists its sub-areas under its projects, one row each; see **An area's page**.

Any area can be picked as the parent. Picking one that is already a sub-area lifts it up a level first, since areas nest one level only — asking for "Yoga under Mobility" means Mobility is the level above.

## Tasks

Tasks are ordinary list lines inside the notes:

- `- [ ] Book the hotel` is open
- `- [x] Book the hotel @done(2026-09-06)` is done
- `- [-] Cancelled` and `- [>] Moved to another day`
- `>2026-09-10` sets a date, `>2026-09-10T14:30` a date with a time of day. You do not have to type the time: the **At** line in the date window writes it (see **The date window and its At line**).
- `@deadline(2026-11-20)` sets a deadline: the day the task must be finished by (since build 316). See **A deadline besides the date**.
- `!`, `!!`, `!!!` set the priority
- `~45m`, `~1h` or `~1h30m` says how long the task takes. **Start the day** asks for it before it plans a task, adds up what you picked, and **Plan** makes each block that long. The small button on the right of a task row writes it for you. Since build 312 the dashed **How long?** button is shown only in **Start the day** and in the **Actions** column of **Plan**, where the time matters. Everywhere else (**Today**, **Actions**, the pages of projects and notes, **Close the day**, **Inbox**, **Weekly review**) a task shows its time only when it has one, such as **45 min**; to give it one there, right-click the task (hold it on the iPhone) and choose **How long**. Before build 312 **How long?** was on every task with no time, in every list. **Settings › Tasks › Use task times** hides all of it.
- `#tag` adds a tag
- an indented `- [ ]` under a task is a subtask
- `^t3cd432` at the end is the marker the app uses to match the task with its reminder. The editor hides it, so you will only see it in another editor. If it ever goes missing, the next sync puts it back.

While you type, the editor shows what the markdown means: headings grow, task boxes are coloured, a finished task is struck through, dates and tags stand out, and the symbols themselves fade. Nothing in the file changes; it stays plain markdown for NotePlan and every other editor.

### Swiping a task to tick it

Since build 304, swipe a task from the left edge, in any list, to tick it done (Today, All actions, Search, Inbox and the others). A full swipe ticks it at once. On the iPhone and iPad you feel the same gentle tap as when you tap the circle. On a task that is already ticked the swipe says **Not done** and opens it again. Cancelled tasks have no swipe. On the Mac it works with two fingers on the trackpad.

### Swiping a task to tomorrow

Since build 305, on the iPhone and iPad, swipe a task from the right edge to the left, in any task list, to move it to tomorrow. A full swipe moves it at once, and a message "Moved to tomorrow." appears at the bottom with an **Undo** button for about eight seconds (since build 306). **Undo** gives the task its old date back, or no date if it had none; it only does so if the task still has the date the swipe gave it. The task keeps its time of day (a call at 14:00 becomes a call at 14:00 tomorrow) and its repeat rule, and **Undo** never changes the time of day. Finished and cancelled tasks do not offer it. Swiping from the left still ticks a task done. Since build 306 the same right-edge swipe works on lines in the **Inbox**: a **Tomorrow** button with the sunrise mark, in the evening colour, and a full swipe moves the line at once, with the same message and **Undo**. Only open lines have it. On the Mac the right-click menu's date options are as before.

### Swiping a task to focus on it

Since build 316, the swipe from the right edge shows 2 buttons: **Tomorrow** and, beside it, **Focus**. **Focus** starts the focus timer on that task, like **Focus on this** in the menu. **Tomorrow** stays first, and a full swipe still means **Tomorrow**. On the Mac the same swipe works with two fingers on the trackpad. Also on the Mac, when the pointer is over a task's row, a small timer button shows at the right end of the row; a press starts the clock. **Focus** is not offered on a finished task, on a task in a Work note, or on a line in the **Inbox**.

### Ticking a task in the note's text

In Edit, the box of every task line can be pressed: tap it on the iPhone and iPad, click it on the Mac. It works exactly like ticking the task in a task list. The box becomes `[x]` and the line gets the done date, so Reminders sync and the Done list see it. A repeating task gets its next copy on the line below. Pressing a ticked or cancelled box opens the task again. Pressing anywhere else in the line only puts the cursor there. The box is drawn a little bolder, in the note's colour, so you can see it is a button.

On the iPhone and the iPad, a slim bar sits above the keyboard while you type in a note. Its checkbox button turns the line the cursor is on into a task, or a task back into an ordinary line; the other buttons are described next. On the Mac there is no bar: type `- [ ] ` as before, or click the box.

### Bold, italic, strikethrough and headings

The note file stays plain markdown, but while you write it looks finished: **bold** is bold, *italic* is italic, ~~struck through~~ text has a line through it and headings are big. The marker characters (`**`, `*`, `~~` and the `# ` of a heading) are hidden on every line except the one the cursor is on; on that line they show faintly so you can still edit them. On the iPhone and iPad, when you are not typing in the note, all markers are hidden.

On the iPhone and iPad the bar above the keyboard has these buttons for it:

- **B** makes the selected text, or the word the cursor is in, bold. Pressed again on bold text it takes the bold off.
- **I** does the same for italic.
- **S**, the letter S with a line through it, does the same for strikethrough. In the file it is `~~` around the text.
- With no word at the cursor, B, I and S put in an empty pair and leave the cursor between, ready to type.
- **H** steps the line: plain, big heading, middle heading, small heading, plain again.

At the end of the bar there are also a pencil button, for drawing a sketch, and a photo button, for adding a photo (see **Sketches and photos**).

On the Mac the Format menu has Bold (Command-B), Italic (Command-I), Strikethrough (Shift-Command-X) and Heading (Option-Command-H), with the same rules.

**Very large notes (since build 308).** While you type, only the lines you changed are restyled; the whole note is restyled 0.4 seconds after your last key. A note of several thousand lines stays quick to type in, and a short note behaves as before.

### Sketches and photos

**Pictures in the text.** A picture link in a note, to a file in your vault (`.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.heic` or `.gif`), shows as the picture itself, right where the line is, while you write. This is the same on the iPhone, iPad and Mac, and it covers sketches, photos and pictures added in Obsidian. You do not see the link text, but the file still holds the normal markdown link, so other apps show the picture too. The picture is as wide as the text at most, and at most 320 points tall, with rounded corners. A picture that iCloud has not sent yet shows a small grey box, **On its way from iCloud**.

**Pressing a picture.** A sketch on the iPhone or iPad opens the drawing page, so you can draw on it again; **Done** saves over it. A photo, or any picture on the Mac, opens bigger; press **Done** to close it (Escape on the Mac).

**Removing a picture.** Put the cursor right after the picture and press Backspace (Delete on the Mac). The picture and its link go from the note. The picture file stays in its folder.

**Sketches (iPhone and iPad).** Press the pencil button at the end of the bar above the keyboard. A drawing page opens with Apple's own tools: pen, marker, pencil, eraser, lasso, colours and ruler. Draw with Apple Pencil or with your finger. **Cancel** throws the sketch away. **Done** saves it. The sketch goes into the **Sketches** folder of your vault, as a picture (PNG, for example `Sketch 2026-11-11 114005.png`) and a drawing file (`.drawing`) with the same name, which lets you draw on it again later. A line like `![Sketch](../Sketches/Sketch%202026-11-11%20114005.png)` goes into the note where the cursor was. The Mac cannot draw, because it has no Pencil. A sketch opened to draw on has a **Copy words** button at the top, like a photo. PARAGON reads the handwriting in sketches too, so search finds it; after you draw on a sketch again, its new words are read straight away.

**Photos (iPhone and iPad).** Press the photo button, beside the pencil. Choose **Take photo** (the camera; the phone asks once if PARAGON may use it) or **Choose photo** (your photo library, no permission needed). The photo is saved as a JPEG, longest side 2048 pixels, in the vault's **Photos** folder, for example `Photo 2026-11-11 123005.jpg`. A line like `![Photo](../Photos/Photo%202026-11-11%20123005.jpg)` goes into the note where the cursor was, and the photo shows at once.

**Captions.** The alt text of a picture (the words in `![My caption](…)`) shows as a small grey line under the picture, on the iPhone, iPad and Mac. The default words **Sketch**, **Photo** and **Picture**, and empty alt text, show no caption. To set one, right-click (or Control-click) the picture on the Mac, or press and hold it on the iPhone and iPad, and choose **Caption…**. A small window has a text field, **Cancel** and **Save**. **Save** writes the words as the picture's alt text, so Obsidian and other markdown apps show the same caption. Clearing the field removes the caption.

**Copy words (iPhone, iPad and Mac).** **Copy words** puts the words PARAGON read in a picture on the clipboard, to paste anywhere. You find it in three places: a **Copy words** button at the top of the picture opened big; in a note's text, press and hold the picture (iPhone and iPad) or right-click it (Mac), where it sits in the same menu as **Caption...**, **Show words** and **Make tasks**; and in the **Pictures** gallery, press and hold (iPhone and iPad) or right-click (Mac) a thumbnail. If the words have not been read yet, PARAGON reads the picture right then. A picture with no words says **No words found in this picture**. A short note, **Words copied**, confirms it. The note and the picture file are not changed. **Select part:** a picture opened big has a **Select part** button at the bottom. Press it, then drag a box over just the words you want; when you let go, only the words inside the box are read and copied, and it says **Words copied** (or **No words in that part**). Press the button again (it reads **Stop selecting**) to leave without copying. It works for photos and pasted pictures; on the iPhone and iPad a sketch opens for drawing, so there use the **Copy words** button.

**Show words (iPhone, iPad and Mac).** Press and hold a picture in a note (right-click on the Mac) and choose **Show words**, in the same menu as **Caption...**, **Copy words** and **Make tasks**. The words PARAGON read in the picture are written into the note right under the picture, as a quote: each line starts with ">". They can be read, edited and searched like normal text. If the words have not been read yet, PARAGON reads the picture right then. A picture with no words says **No words found in this picture**, and nothing is written.

**Zooming a picture.** On a picture opened big, pinch with two fingers (or pinch on the Mac trackpad) to zoom in, up to 5 times. While zoomed, drag to move around. Double-tap to go back to normal size; at normal size a double-tap zooms in to 2.5 times. **Done** (Escape on the Mac) closes it. While you select part of a picture, the zoom goes back to normal.

**Make tasks (iPhone, iPad and Mac).** Press and hold a picture in a note (right-click on the Mac) and choose **Make tasks**, in the same menu as **Caption...**, **Copy words** and **Show words**. PARAGON reads the words in the picture and writes each line as an open task (a line starting with "- [ ]") straight under the picture in the note. Handy for a photo of a handwritten list or a whiteboard. The tasks are normal tasks: tick them, edit them, search them. If the words have not been read yet, PARAGON reads the picture right then. A picture with no words says **No words found in this picture**, and nothing is written.

**Words marked on a picture.** After you have searched for words (in **Search**), open a picture big (tap it in a note or in the **Pictures** gallery). The places on the picture where those words are get soft tinted boxes. The boxes stay on the words while you pinch to zoom and move the picture. If you have not searched, there are no boxes.

**Pictures from Finder (Mac).** Drag picture files (`.png`, `.jpg`, `.jpeg`, `.heic`, `.gif`) from Finder into a note. Each is copied into a **Pictures** folder in your notes folder (new, made when needed), with the same file name, or "name 2.jpg" if that name is taken. Its line goes where you dropped it. The original file stays where it was. Since build 318 the file name is made safe first (a name like `Scan (1).png` used to break the link), and a PDF can be dragged in the same way: it goes into the **Attachments** folder and shows as a row. See **Pictures and PDFs in a note**.

**Pasting a picture (Mac).** Copy a picture (a screenshot, or an image copied from Safari, Preview or Photos), put the cursor in a note's text and press Command-V (or **Edit › Paste**). The picture is saved as a PNG in the **Pictures** folder of your notes folder, named like `Pasted 2026-11-11 15.42.07.png` (a number is added if that name is taken), and its picture line goes where the cursor is. It shows at once, like any other picture. If you copied picture files in Finder, pasting copies them into **Pictures** too, like dragging them (see above). If the clipboard holds text, the text is pasted as text: text wins.

**Pasting a picture (iPhone and iPad).** Copy a picture, press and hold in a note's text and choose **Paste** (on an iPad keyboard, Command-V). The picture is saved as a JPEG, its longest side at most 2048 points, in the **Pictures** folder of your notes folder, named like `Pasted 2026-11-11 154207.jpg` (a number is added if that name is taken), and its picture line goes where the cursor is. If the clipboard holds text, the text is pasted as text. The Mac keeps saving PNG.

**The Pictures gallery.** A note with two or more pictures shows a **Pictures** row above its text, on the iPhone, iPad and Mac. It has a small square thumbnail (about 64 points) of every picture in the note, in the order they appear, and the number of pictures beside the heading. Press the heading to fold the row away or open it again; PARAGON remembers it. Press a thumbnail to open that picture bigger, as pressing the picture in the text does (press **Done** to close it, Escape on the Mac). A note with one picture, or none, shows no gallery.

Press and hold a thumbnail (iPhone and iPad) or right-click it (Mac) and choose **Copy words** to copy the words in that picture (see below).

**Searching the gallery.** The **Pictures** panel has a small **Search pictures** box. Type, and only the pictures whose words contain everything you typed are shown (capital letters do not matter). Clear the box to see all pictures again. If none match, it says **No picture has these words**.

**Sorting the gallery.** Drag a thumbnail onto another to change the order. On the Mac, drag with the mouse; on the iPhone and iPad, press, hold and drag. The pictures move in the note's text too: each picture takes the place of another, and the text around them stays where it was.

### Long press on a task's box

Long-press a task's box in the note text (right-click it on the Mac) for a small menu:

- **Cancel task**: the box becomes `[-]` and the line is struck through, like Cancel elsewhere in the app.
- **Move to tomorrow**: the due date becomes tomorrow and keeps its time of day, the same rule as Reschedule.

Undo takes either back.

### Return continues a list

Press Return on a task line and a new empty task starts below it with the same indent, like Apple Notes. Text after the cursor moves onto the new task. Press Return on an empty task line and the box goes away and the list ends. Plain bullets (`- `, `* ` and `+ `) work the same way, and the new line gets the same bullet. Numbered lists work too: Return on `1. milk` starts `2. ` on the next line with the same indent, and `1)` becomes `2)`. Return on an empty numbered line removes the number and ends the list. With the cursor before the box, Return works as before.

The task box under the note header shows the same tasks as a checklist. Ticking there edits the line in the file. Its header row has a **Hide finished** switch (also in Settings › Tasks) that leaves out what is done or cancelled; a finished task with open subtasks always stays visible, and nothing is removed from the file.

Right-click any task, anywhere in the app — long-press on the phone — for the task menu:

- **Reschedule**: Today, Tomorrow, Next Monday, In a week, Pick a date, Remove date. **Pick a date** opens the date window, where you can also give the task a time of day. The quick choices change the day only and **keep the time**: a call at 14:00 moved to tomorrow is a call at 14:00 tomorrow. **Remove date** takes the time off with it.
- **How long**: 3 min, 5 min, 10 min, 15 min, 30 min, 45 min, 1 h, 1 h 30, 2 h or 3 h, or **No time**. Only there while **Use task times** is on in Settings.
- **Waiting for…**: you handed this task to somebody else. A small window asks **Who?** and the line goes into the **Waiting for** list under **All actions**. On a line that is already waiting there is also **Not waiting any more**. See **Waiting for** under **All actions**.
- **Deadline** (since build 316): **Pick a date…**, **In a week**, and **Remove deadline** when the task has one. See **A deadline besides the date**.
- **Repeat**: every day, week, 2 weeks, month, 3 months or year. Since build 316 also **On weekdays…**, **Last day of the month**, **Other…** and **Count from the day I finish it**. When you tick a repeating task, the next one appears below it with the next date. In the file this is `@repeat(weekly)`. See **Repeat: the choices**.
- **Make this the next action** (in a project): the task gets a `#next` tag and a badge, and Today lists it under "Next actions". One per project. It is not offered on a line you are waiting on somebody else for.
- **Block time for this…**: opens **Plan** with the title filled in.
- **Rename…**: turns the line into a field. Type and press Return. The date, repeat, tags and subtasks stay, and so does the reminder it is linked to.
