- **Move to**: the Inbox, any active project or any active area. Subtasks move along. The reminder follows on the next sync. You can also drag the task onto another note in the middle column.

You can also **drag** a task: onto a note in the middle column or onto Inbox to move it, onto a day in the Calendar's month grid or week list to set its date, or onto a day in the weekly note.

### The date window and its At line

**Pick a date**, in the task menu and in the Inbox, opens the date window. It has a field for the date, a calendar, and under them a line called **At**: a small field for the time of day, four quick times (**09:00**, **12:00**, **14:00** and **16:00**) and a button, **No time of day**.

- Press a quick time, or write the time yourself as `14:00` (`9:30` is fine too). The field must be a time: if it is not, a red line says how to write it and **Set** cannot be used yet.
- **Set** is a filled button in purple, with white words. It writes the date and the time together. Return in either field does the same. On the iPhone, while the keyboard is up, a **Set** button also sits above the keys.
- **At** empty means no time of day: the task is due on that date, for the whole day. **No time of day** empties the field; press **Set** to keep the date without a time. It is not the **No time** in **How long**, which takes away how long a task takes.
- **No date** takes the date and the time off.
- The time is written on the task's line as `T14:00` after the date. A task row shows it beside the date, with a small clock.
- **Apple Reminders rings at that time**, for a task in a note that syncs to Reminders. After the next sync, a task you gave a time gets an alarm in Reminders at that time. Before, a task with only a date did not ring. A note marked Not synced, a Resource and a Goal never go to Reminders, so no alarm rings for their tasks.
- The word is **At**, not "Time", because a task's **time** already means how long it takes (`~45m`).

A task with a time is a fixed point in the day. **Plan** draws it at its hour (see **Plan** below), and **Start the day** keeps that hour free, as long as no note is still coming from iCloud: a task in a note that has not arrived yet cannot be kept free, and **Start the day** says so in its message. The Calendar section's day draws it at its hour too, as long as the task's own time. 

Since build 315 the same window, with its **At** line, opens wherever a task gets a date: on a task, in the Inbox, behind **Date…** in **Quick capture**, and behind **Day…** in **Close the day**. In Quick capture, **Set** writes the date and the time onto the line you are typing, like `>2026-11-14T14:00`. If the line already has a date, it is changed, not added a second time. In **Close the day**, the window opens on the task's own time, so you can keep it, change it or take it away. A project's deadline, a goal's target date and the day something ended are still a date only: they have no time of day. Before build 315, Quick capture's date window had no **At** line.

### A deadline besides the date

Since build 316 a task can have 2 days. Its **date** is the day you plan to do it. Its **Deadline** is the day it must be finished by. Before build 316 only a project had a deadline.

- **To set it**: right-click the task (hold it on the iPhone) › **Deadline** › **Pick a date…** or **In a week**. **Remove deadline** takes it off.
- **Or in the date window**: it has 3 pages, chosen at the top: **Date**, **Deadline** and **Repeat**. Press **Deadline**, pick the day and press **Save**. **Remove deadline** clears it. **Cancel** (Escape on the Mac) changes nothing. A deadline is a day only, with no time of day. Each page writes only its own thing.
- **On the row**: a small red flag and words: "7 days left", "1 day left", "Due today", "1 day late", "3 days late". The words are grey while there is time, and red on the day itself and after it. A finished task shows a grey flag and the date. The flag is on task rows and on lines in the **Inbox**.
- **Moving the task never moves the deadline.** **Reschedule**, the swipe to **Tomorrow** and dragging change the date only.
- **Late means past the deadline.** A task with a deadline does not show its date in red when that day has passed; the flag shows the red.
- **A repeating task** keeps the same number of days between its date and its deadline when you tick it. With only a deadline, the next task gets the next deadline.
- **In the file** it is `@deadline(2026-11-20)` on the task's line.
- **Apple Reminders** does not get the deadline. The reminder's date is the task's date, as before. A change in Reminders never removes the deadline.
- **Not changed**: **Overdue** on **Today**, the **Weekly review** and the widgets go by the task's date, not by the deadline.
- On build 315 and older the deadline is not lost, but it shows as the text "@deadline(2026-11-20)" in the task's title.

### Repeat: the choices

The **Repeat** menu has the 6 quick choices: every day, week, 2 weeks, month, 3 months or year. Since build 316 it has 4 more lines under them:

- **On weekdays…** opens the date window on its **Repeat** page, to choose the days.
- **Last day of the month** sets it with one press. It knows short months (28 or 29 February).
- **Other…** opens the **Repeat** page too, for any other number.
- **Count from the day I finish it** is shown when the task repeats every so many days, weeks, months or years. A tick shows when it is on.

The **Repeat** page of the date window has 4 choices: **Does not repeat**, **Every…**, **On weekdays** and **Last day of the month**.

- **Every…**: write a number and choose days, weeks, months or years, for example every 10 days. The chip **Count from the day I finish it** is here too.
- **On weekdays**: 7 chips, **Mon** to **Sun**, and the button **Monday to Friday**.
- One grey sentence says what the choice does. The buttons are **Cancel** and **Save**; on the Mac, Escape is **Cancel** and Return is **Save**. Nothing is written before **Save**.

The task's row says the rule in words: "Every Mon, Wed, Fri", "Every working day", "Last day of the month", "10 days after it is done".

**Count from the day I finish it**, an example: water the plants every 10 days. It was planned for the 1st and you did it on the 13th. The next one is on the 23rd. Counted from its date it would be the 21st.

**Use the new repeats only when both devices have build 316.** Build 315 does not know chosen weekdays, the last day of the month, or counting from the day you finish. If a device on build 315 changes such a task (ticks it or moves it), it writes the task without the repeat, and the repeat is lost. The 6 quick choices are safe on every build.

### Adding a task at the bottom of a note

The field says **Add a task…**. Type the words and press Return, or press the arrow.

Since build 316 you can write the date in plain words here, for example "Call the bank tomorrow 14:00". While date words are in the field, a dashed chip under it says what was read, like **Due tomorrow 14:00**. The **x** on the chip takes the date away and keeps the words as text. See **A date in plain words** under **Quick capture**. The field **Capture something…** at the top of the **Inbox** works the same way.

The **ⓘ** beside the field opens the list of what else you can write in it: a date, a date and a
time, a priority, a tag, a repeat. Up to build 141 that list was the grey text inside the field,
where it was too long to read on the phone and disappeared as soon as you typed.

Beside it: **Snippet**, a ready-made block of tasks from Templates › Snippets, and on the phone
the **pencil** that turns writing on and off.

### Undo for one-press changes

Three changes happen in one press, with no question first. Each can be put back for a short time.

- **Move every overdue task to today or tomorrow**: the two small buttons over **Overdue** on Today. The message at the bottom says how many tasks moved.
- **Swipe to tomorrow** (since build 306): the right-edge swipe on a task row or an Inbox line. The message says "Moved to tomorrow."
- **Delete** in the Inbox: the Delete key, or **Delete** in the line's menu. The message says which line was deleted.

While the message has an **Undo** button it stays for about eight seconds. The **Undo** button goes earlier if another message takes its place, and when you open or close a vault. One press on **Undo** is enough; a second press does nothing.

- After a move, **Undo** gives every task its own old date back, not one date for all of them. After a swipe to tomorrow it gives that task its old date, or no date if it had none. The time of day is never changed by a move, so **Undo** leaves it alone.
- After a delete, **Undo** puts the line back with its subtasks, in the same place. If the Inbox has changed since — a capture from your other device came in, for example — the line is added near where it was and nothing is written over. If the line is already there, the message says so and nothing is added. If iCloud is still bringing a newer copy of that note, **Undo** does not put it back: the message says the line stays deleted, because a write over an old copy could lose a newer capture.
- **Undo** only changes what is still as the press left it. A task you ticked off, gave another date or deleted in the meantime, or that another device changed, is left as it is.
- The message after **Undo** says how many were put back, and names the ones that were not, with the reason. For example: "5 of 6 tasks put back. “Send the invoice” was changed after the move, so it was left as it is." That message has no **Undo** of its own, so it goes away after the usual two and a half seconds.

There is no **Undo** on the buttons in **Close the day**, because that screen covers the message on the phone. A date set on one task, and a task deleted inside a note, have none either.

## The Inbox

Everything you capture lands here, and the screen is built for emptying it.

The middle column is the list of lines waiting to be sorted, with a capture field on top. Click a line to pick it, and four buttons appear: **Today**, **Tomorrow**, **Pick a date** and **File it**. The first three set the line's date, and the button for the date it already has is filled in and outlined in orange, so the four buttons also tell you where the line stands. **Today** and **Tomorrow** keep a time the line already has; **Pick a date** opens the date window with its **At** line, and the line then shows its time beside the date. On the Mac **File it** is a menu: move the line to a project or an area, turn it into a note, block time for it, take the date off again, or delete it. On the iPhone, since build 312, **File it** lists your projects, then your areas, at once: tap one and the line moves there. That is 3 taps (the line, **File it**, the project). Before it was 4, because the projects were one menu deeper, under **Move to**. With no project and no area it says "No project or area yet". On the iPhone the other choices are in the menu that opens when you hold the line: **Move to**, **Turn into a note**, **Block time for this…**, **Remove the date**, **Rename…** and **Delete**. **Delete** asks nothing first: the message at the bottom has an **Undo** button for about eight seconds (see **Undo for one-press changes**). Since build 306 you can also swipe an open line from the right edge to move it to tomorrow without picking it first: a **Tomorrow** button with the sunrise mark, in the evening colour; a full swipe does it at once, keeps the time of day, and shows "Moved to tomorrow." with an **Undo** button. To rename a line, right-click it on the Mac or hold it on the phone — the same menu opens there, with **Rename…** on it.

On the Mac the four buttons are in the right-hand column, under the line you are sorting and above the places it can go, so everything about one line is in one place. On the phone there is no right-hand column, so the buttons appear just under the line you tapped. On both, the line you picked keeps its ordinary colours and the other lines fade, so the one you are working on stands out.

### Notes in the Inbox (build 299)

A capture saved as **A note** into the Inbox is written into the Inbox note under a **Notes** heading. The Inbox screen shows them in a **Notes** part under the tasks, one row for each captured note. **Make task** turns a note into a task in the list above, to sort like any other line. The bin button deletes the note. **Inbox zero** shows only when there are no tasks and no notes.

### The keys

↑ and ↓ move down the list, **T** gives a line today's date, **M** tomorrow's, **D** ticks it off and **⌫** deletes it, with an **Undo** in the message that follows. Typing in the capture field never triggers them.

### Where it goes

The right-hand column shows the line you are on, the four buttons, and everywhere the line can go. Click a destination and the line moves there, and the next line is selected so you can keep going.

The destinations are grouped into **Projects** and **Areas**, with sub-areas indented under the area they belong to and a chevron to fold a family away. Since build 291 the **Projects** group and the **Areas** group each have a chevron by their heading too: press it to fold the group away, and again to open it. The app remembers which ones you folded. The grey box "Pick a line on the left..." is not shown until a line is picked.

## Reminders sync

The sync goes both ways. Click the sync ring at the foot of the sidebar and choose **Sync now** (or **File › Sync with Reminders**, ⇧⌘R), or let auto sync run at the interval set in Settings.

- Inbox tasks go to the Reminders list called Inbox.
- Each project and area gets a Reminders list with the note's name.
- Tasks in daily and weekly notes go to the Daily Notes list.
- Goals are never synced. They are direction, not to-dos.
- Completing, renaming, dating or deleting on either side carries over.
- If the same task changed in both places since the last sync, the note version wins and the sync report says so.
- Which side wins is a choice in **Settings › Reminders sync**. On the Mac and the iPad the row is **When both sides changed**, with **The note wins** and **The reminder wins**. On the iPhone, since build 316, the same row is **If both changed**, with **Note wins** and **Reminder wins**, so it fits on 1 line. The setting is the same one.
- During a restore from the outside copy, the sync line, the sync ring, the Mac menu bar item and the button in **Settings › Reminders sync** say **Restoring…** (since build 316; before, "Syncing…"). No sync runs until the restore has ended.
- **Renaming a list in Reminders** (since build 307) does not cancel its tasks. When a task's reminder is not in its list, PARAGON looks for it in all your lists, by its identity and its PARAGON mark, and follows it to the new list.
- **Lists are known by their own identity** (since build 308). Once a note and its Reminders list are linked, PARAGON remembers the list itself, not only its name. If two lists have the same name, PARAGON uses the one it is linked to and leaves the other alone; the sync report says "There are two Reminders lists called "Trip". PARAGON uses the one it is linked to and leaves the other alone." A new note whose name matches a Reminders list holding reminders PARAGON did not make (your own list "Trip", say) makes the sync stop and ask, with the alert **Sync stopped to ask you** and the buttons **Keep them apart**, **Do it** and **Cancel** (see **Keep them apart** below): "Reminders already has a list called “Trip” with 3 reminders that PARAGON did not make. This sync would join it to the note “Trip” and add those reminders to the note as tasks. If the note and the list are two different things, choose Keep them apart: PARAGON leaves both as they are and does not ask about this list again. Nothing was changed." **Cancel** changes nothing in the note or in your list, and the next sync asks again. After **Do it** the report says "The note "Trip" was joined to the Reminders list "Trip", which was already there." You are not asked for an empty list, the Inbox and daily lists, a list named in the note's `reminders-list:` line, or a list that is already linked.
- **No twin reminders** (since build 308). If two devices each made a reminder for the same task, the sync finds the two by their PARAGON mark, keeps the older one and removes the twin, and the sync report says ""Buy milk" was in Reminders twice; the extra one was removed." A tick on the removed twin moves to the one that stays. A twin with a different title is not removed. A task that has just arrived from your other device, with no reminder for it anywhere in Reminders, waits one sync before a reminder is made for it, so the other device's reminder can arrive first; the second sync makes it if there still is none.
- **The brake for mass changes** (since build 307). If one sync would cancel or delete more than 5 tasks or reminders, or more than a third of one list that has 3 or more tasks, it stops before it changes anything and shows what it would do, with **Do it** and **Cancel**. The timed automatic sync never does this on its own: it waits until you press **Sync now**. A list that fails to load is never read as "everything was deleted".
- **Keep them apart** (since build 315). When the sync stops to ask about a list with the same name as a note, you have three answers. **Do it** joins the two: the reminders come into the note as tasks. **Cancel** changes nothing, and the next sync asks again. **Keep them apart** is for when the note and the list are two different things: PARAGON remembers the list, leaves the list and the note exactly as they are, and does not ask about that list again. A short message says "The list “Trip” is kept apart", and the sync runs again by itself. The note's tasks do not go to Reminders while its list is kept apart. A list that is already joined to its note stays joined. Before build 315 the only way was to cancel and rename the note or the list.
- **Lists kept apart** (since build 315). The lists you keep apart are shown in **Settings**, in the group **Lists kept apart**, just under **Reminders sync**. **Connect again** beside a list forgets the choice; nothing is joined by that press, and the next sync asks you about the list once more. With no list kept apart, the group is not shown. The choice is kept in the vault, so your iPhone and your Mac share it. When you delete a list you kept apart, the device you made the choice on forgets it at the next sync, and a new list with the same name is asked about like any other.

**In the Mac menu bar.** Since build 303 a second PARAGON item sits beside the quick-capture one: the same clock ring as at the foot of the sidebar (green under an hour, yellow after that or while waiting for iCloud, full red after conflicts, grey and empty before any sync), with the short word beside it ("10:55", "Yesterday", "Syncing…"). Click it for a menu: the sync line (such as "Synced at 10:55", "Synced yesterday at 21:40" or, for an earlier day, "Synced 2026-11-09 at 21:40"), **Sync now**, **Show me what would change…**, **Last sync report…**, **Sync settings…** and **Show PARAGON**. Since build 304 it also shows the number of tasks due today right beside the ring, for example "3 · 11:20"; nothing is shown when no task is due, and over 99 it says "99+". The ring is drawn once per minute (in build 303 it redrew without pause and the Mac app would not open). To hide it, switch off **Show the sync ring in the menu bar** in Settings › Reminders sync (on by default).
- Subtasks become separate reminders named "Parent › Child". Tasks with a time get an alarm.

**When the notes are in a new place (since build 319).** Before every sync PARAGON looks for the marks of a copy in the vault folder. When the notes are used in another folder now, or are being copied into this one, no sync runs from it, and **Sync now** says "Reminders sync is paused: your notes are in another folder now." A mark that is still coming from iCloud pauses the sync too, until it can be read. The sync also rests while a copy runs on this device. See **PARAGON's own iCloud folder**.

Each device keeps its own sync bookkeeping, so the Mac and the iPhone can both sync the same vault. Since build 307 the device keeps its identity in a second safe place. If its bookkeeping is missing, the first sync takes over the ticks and edits from Reminders and does not undo them. If the bookkeeping file is there but cannot be read, the sync stops with a message and you try again in a moment; it never starts from zero. Since build 308 the sync also has its own access to the vault folder, separate from the screen's, so a sync in the background and your typing cannot trip over each other.

### Seeing what a sync will do

The toolbar buttons explain themselves when you hold the pointer over them: the coloured **+** is **New** in this section (⌘N); since build 299 the tray button is gone, because **New** has **Inbox** as one of its choices, and **Quick capture**, a thought into the Inbox, stays on ⇧⌘N; and the sync runs by itself every few minutes (set in Settings). Since build 292 there is no sync button in the toolbar: the sync ring at the foot of the sidebar (a pill until build 301) does it (see below), and **File › Sync with Reminders** (⇧⌘R) still works. The ring's tip says, for example, "Synced with Reminders at 15:05 - all fine". When the sync had notes for you, it says "... N things worth knowing, nothing to do (see Last sync report...)" instead of the old "Last sync: 8 warnings"; the word "warnings" became "worth knowing".

Since build 312 one click on the sync ring at the foot of the sidebar opens a small panel (on the iPhone and the iPad, one tap opens a menu): **Sync now**, **Show me what would change…**, **Last sync report…** (only when there is a report), a line, and **Sync settings…**. Before, a click synced at once, and the other choices opened only on a right-click or a hold, which nothing told you. So **Sync now** is 2 clicks now; before it was 1. The words at the ring say "Not synced" before any sync (before build 312: "Not synced yet"), then "Synced just now" (under 1 minute), "Synced 5 min ago", "Synced 3 h ago" (under 24 hours), then the date, "Synced 2026-11-09". The ring in the menu bar keeps its short words. **Show me what would change…** rehearses the entire sync on a copy of your vault and a copy of your reminders and shows the result. Nothing is touched. From that sheet you can press **Sync now** to do it for real. **Last sync report…** shows the same for the sync you ran.

## Aspirations and Goals

There are **two rows in the sidebar**, because there are two different things.

**Aspirations** says what you are becoming. An aspiration has no date, because you never tick one off. It is the A in PARAGON. Press **Aspiration** at the top of the **New** screen to make one.

**Goals** says what you will have done, and by when. A goal has a target date, a measure of what success looks like, since build 318 a number to follow if you want one (see **A goal with a number**), and the aspiration it serves, chosen in the **Serves aspiration** box. Press **Goal** at the top of the **New** screen.

A project or an area serves a goal with the `goal:` line. Press **Serves…** at the top of the project or area to choose the goal, or press **Add a project…** on the goal itself (see below). A goal note shows how many projects and areas serve it, open tasks, activity in the last 30 days, and flags such as "No project or area serves this", "Past its target date" or "Done".

Both are goal notes in your vault, so nothing was moved or rewritten. The line that tells them apart is `horizon:`, and it is the same line as before.

### Changing a goal's date and measure

The top of a goal says what it is: **Goal with a date** or **Aspiration**, as the heading of its facts panel. After the name and the status come its facts, each with a small grey label:

- **Serves…** — the aspiration a goal with a date serves. Press it to choose another one.
- **Set a target date…** — the day the goal should be reached by. When a date is set, the button shows it instead, for example **Target 2027-08-15**. Press it to change the date, or press **Clear** to take it away. Write the date as 2027-08-15 or pick it in the calendar.
- **Set a measure…** — one line that says how you will know the goal is reached, for example "All 440 km in 30 days". When a measure is set, the button shows it. Press it to change the words, write them, and press **Set** or Return. **Clear** takes the measure away.
- **This year** or **Long term** — how far ahead the goal reaches. This year is one or two years, Long term is three to ten. The button shows the one the goal has. Press it to see both, with a tick on the one the goal has now, and press the other to change it. It is kept as `horizon:` in the settings block, and a goal with no `horizon:` line counts as This year.

An aspiration has **Set a measure…** but no date button, because an aspiration has no date. It has no **This year** / **Long term** button either. An aspiration is not a longer goal, it is another kind of note, and changing it would move it to the other list.

If the measure was changed on your other device while the box was open, **Set** writes nothing the first time. The box stays open with your words in it, and an orange line says what the measure is now. Press **Set** again to use your words instead. **Clear** works the same way.

A new date changes the weekly review at once: "Past its target date" and "Due after its goal" are worked out from it.

On the Mac and the iPad, the goal's page in **Goals** has the same band (the title, "40 %" large and "of the way · Target 2027-08-15" under it) and the same facts panel as the note itself: the heading **Goal with a date** with **Icon…** beside it, then **Status**, **Serves**, **Horizon**, **Target**, **Progress** (the per cent), **Measure**, **Looked at**, **Tags** and **Reminders list:**, each a small grey label with its value under it, and a bordered pop-up or button where you can change it. **Horizon** is a labelled pop-up with **This year** and **Long term**. A notice such as **Target date passed** is a small orange capsule in the panel. An aspiration's page in **Aspirations** has the same panel, headed **Aspiration**; only the facts that fit are shown, so it has no **Target**, no **Horizon** and no **Progress**. On the iPhone, **Set a measure…** is also under an aspiration when you open it in **Aspirations**.

### A goal with a number

Since build 318. A goal with a date can carry a number beside its measure: where you started, where you want to be, and where you are now. An aspiration gets no number, because you never finish an aspiration. A goal without a number looks exactly as before.

- **Set a number…** is in the goal's header, beside **Set a measure…**. It opens a small window with 4 fields: **Start**, **Target**, **Now** and **Unit**. The unit is your own word (km, kg, kr, pages), 16 characters at most. The buttons are **Cancel** and **Save**; on the Mac, Return is **Save** and Escape is **Cancel**.
- An empty **Start** means 0. An empty **Now** means the same as **Start**. **Save** is off while **Target** is empty or a field does not hold a number.
- When the goal has a number, the button says **Edit number…**, and the window also has **Remove**. **Remove** asks first; the button to confirm says **Remove the number and its history**. It takes the number out of the note and nothing else.
- **The bar.** On the goal's page, under the header, a bar in the goal's colour shows the number, with words like "86.5 of 82 kg" and the per cent.
- **Update…** is the full-colour button at the right end of that line. It opens a small window with 1 number field, the unit beside it, **Cancel** and **Save**. The new value is saved with today's date. A second update on the same day replaces that day's value. On the iPhone the field opens the number pad.
- **Typing a number.** A comma and a dot both work ("86,5" and "86.5"). Spaces between thousands work ("100 000"). A minus sign works.
- **The per cent** says how far **Now** has come from **Start** to **Target**. Start 90, target 82, now 86 is 50 %. It never says "100 %" before the target is met (it says "99 %"), and never "0 %" when the number has moved the right way (it says "1 %").
- **Reached.** When the target is met or passed, the bar is full, the per cent says the real number (for example "113 %") and a small mark **Reached** stands beside it.
- **A target that goes down** works the same way: start 90 kg, target 82 kg. Moving the wrong way (92 kg) shows 0 % and an empty bar, never a minus number.
- **The curve.** Under the bar a small hand-drawn curve shows every update, the oldest on the left, one point for each update. The newest value has a dot and the target is a dashed line. Under it 1 quiet line says, for example, "From 90 kg · last update 2026-11-13 · 3 updates". With fewer than 2 values there is no curve, and the line ends with "press Update… when the number changes". The curve keeps up to 120 days of updates; over that the oldest go, but the very first value stays.
- **Where else it shows.** In the **Goals** list a goal's row gets a third line with the bar only and the value now ("86.5 kg"). In the **Weekly review**, the goals step shows the number's bar with "86.5 of 82 kg" and the per cent, beside the bar for the projects. It is not on the **Map** and not in the chain under an aspiration.
- **In the file.** The number is 5 plain lines in the goal note's settings block. You can change them by hand, in PARAGON or in Obsidian:

      number_start: 90
      number_target: 82
      number_current: 86.5
      number_unit: kg
      number_history: [2026-10-01 90, 2026-11-01 88, 2026-11-13 86.5]

- In the file a number has a dot for decimals and no spaces. On screen PARAGON uses your device's own way ("86,5" and "100 000" on a Swedish device).
- The per cent from the ticked tasks of the goal's projects is unchanged; the number has its own bar. Goals still never go to Reminders.

### Adding a project from its goal

A goal can make the projects that serve it, and an aspiration can make the goals that serve it. You stay on the page you are on.

- Open a goal. Under the projects that serve it there is **Add a project…**. Press it, write the name, and press **Add project** or Return.
- The new project appears in the list at once, and it already serves the goal: its `goal:` line names the goal. Press it to open it. Press **Add a project…** again for the next one.
- On an aspiration the button is **Add a goal…**, and the box has **Add goal**. The new goal serves the aspiration. It has no target date yet: open it and press **Set a target date…**.
- An aspiration makes a goal, never a project. The work then goes under that goal, which is the step that gives it a date.
- When nothing serves a goal yet, its note says "Nothing serves this goal yet" and shows the same button under it. On the Mac and the iPad, the goal's page in **Goals** lists its projects in a tinted panel headed **Projects**, with **Add a project…** at its foot; when no project serves it, the panel says "No project delivers this goal yet" in orange. An aspiration with nothing under it says "Nothing works towards this yet".
- If a project or goal with that name is already there, the box says so in orange and keeps your words. Change the name and press **Add project** or **Add goal** again.
- **Cancel** closes the box and makes nothing.

An area has **Add a project…** too, beside **Projects in this area** at the top of the area; see **An area's page**.

Where the button is: at the foot of a goal's own note, on the Mac and on the iPhone. On the Mac and the iPad it is also at the foot of the **Projects** panel on the goal's page in **Goals**, and under the goals on an aspiration's page in **Aspirations**. Inside an aspiration's chain, a goal with no project yet has **Add a project…** under the orange line that says no project delivers it. On the iPhone, **Add a goal…** is also under an aspiration when you open it in **Aspirations**.

### When you last looked at it

At the top of an aspiration, a goal with a date or an area, a grey button with an eye says when you last looked at it: **Looked at 12 days ago**, or **Not looked at yet**.

- Press it. A small box says how often that kind of note gets a look, and when the next look is due, for example "Goals with a date get a look every 3 months. The next look is due in 78 days."
- Press **I looked at it today** in the box. PARAGON writes `reviewed:` with today's date into the note's settings block, and the button says **Looked at today**.
- When the note already says today, **I looked at it today** is grey, because pressing it would change nothing.
- The same **I looked at it today** is on the right-click menu of a row in **Aspirations**, **Goals** and **Areas**. On the iPhone, press and hold the row.
- On the Mac and the iPad it is also in the facts panel of an aspiration's page in **Aspirations** and of a goal's page in **Goals**, as **Looked at**.
- It is the same date the **Weekly review** reads, so the note leaves **Due for a look** until it is due again. How often each kind gets a look is set in **Settings › Review rhythm**.
- A goal or an area that has ended, or is archived, has no button: it no longer comes up for a look.
- Projects do not have the button. They get a look every week, in step 5 of the **Weekly review**.

### How a note stands

Press the button in a note's header to say how it stands. A project or a goal can end in three
different ways, and the difference matters:

- **Done** — finished, and it did what it was for.
- **Missed** — it is over and it did not happen. A date passed, or you decided it had not worked.
- **Dropped** — you decided not to do it. Nothing failed; you called it off.

**Only Done counts towards a goal's per cent.** A missed or dropped project is left out of the sum
altogether: counting it as finished would be untrue, and counting it as nothing done would hold the
goal below full for ever.

**The day it ended is written down.** When you mark a project or a goal Done, Missed or Dropped,
PARAGON adds `ended:` with today's date to its settings block (frontmatter). The line goes at the
end of the block. A finished project then shows one grey line under its coloured band: **Done on
2026-10-21 · after 41 days · 17 tasks finished**. "After 41 days" counts from the `created:` line,
which every new note has had from the start; "17 tasks finished" counts the ticked top-level tasks.
A part PARAGON cannot know is left out.

- **Press the line to change the day.** Type it or pick it in the calendar.
- Changing from one ending to another (Done to Missed) **keeps the day**. **Set active** or **Put
  on hold** removes it, because the project is not over any more.
- **A project marked done before build 262 has no day.** PARAGON never guesses one: the line then
  says **Set the day it ended…** in a dashed grey frame, and you can press it if you remember.

Anything that has ended leaves the working lists — the weekly **Review**, the next actions, the
**Map**, and the list of notes you can move a task into. On the **Goals** screen it drops into a
closed group at the foot named after the ending.

The other two words are **Active** (you are working on it) and **On hold** (not now, but not given
up). The weekly **Review** offers the same choices when you right-click a project.

Older words still read as they always did: `achieved` and `completed` mean Done, and `paused` and
`someday` mean On hold. Nothing in your notes was rewritten.

### What is next for this goal?

When you mark a project **Done**, **Missed** or **Dropped**, PARAGON looks at the goal the project worked for. If nothing else is working on that goal, a short question comes up on the screen you are on. It does not matter whether you marked the project in its own note, in **Projects** or in the weekly **Review**. The question reads **What is next for this goal?**. Under it, one sentence says: Nothing is working on "(the goal's name)" now. It has up to three buttons:

- **Add a project…** opens **New** on **Project**. The goal is already chosen in the button that otherwise says **Serves a goal…**. Write the name and press **Save**.
- **Mark the goal done** is there only after **Done**. It marks the goal itself Done, with its own day, and a short message at the bottom of the window says so.
- **Not now** closes the question and changes nothing.

When the question comes, and when it does not:

- It comes only at the moment a project goes from working to over. Changing **Done** to **Missed** does not ask again. A project in the **Archive** does not ask.
- "Nothing is working" means no project and no goal with a date is still running under that goal. A project that is **On hold** is still the goal's project, so no question comes. An area does not count: an area keeps a standard, it does not deliver a goal.
- If the goal has itself ended or been archived, nothing is asked.
- While notes are still coming from iCloud, or iCloud is updating some, nothing is asked. PARAGON cannot know yet that nothing else works on the goal, and a wrong answer could lead you to mark the goal done. The goal's own page still has **Add a project…**.
- If you mark several projects one after another in the **Review**, the question comes once for each goal that is left with nothing.
- An aspiration is never finished. If a project that serves an aspiration directly ends and nothing else works towards it, the question reads **What is next for this aspiration?** and the buttons are **Add a goal…** and **Not now**. There is no **Mark the goal done** for an aspiration.
- Marking a goal Done with **Mark the goal done** does not start a second question about the aspiration above it.
- A reminder that you press while the question is on screen opens when you have answered it.
- After **Not now**, the goal's own page still has **Add a project…** (see **Adding a project from its goal**).

### The Aspirations screen

The **Aspirations** row lists your aspirations, one after another. Choose one and everything
working towards it appears beside it. On the Mac and the iPad that page begins with the
aspiration's band (title at 26 points in white) and its facts panel (see **Changing a goal's date and
measure**), then **The reason** (see below), then, in order:

- the **goals with a target date** that serve it, soonest first;
- the **projects** under each goal — finished ones struck through;
- the **next action** on each project. **No next action** in orange means there is nothing on
  that project you could start. A project where every open task is a line you are waiting on
  somebody else for is not orange: it shows a grey line, such as **All 3 open tasks are waiting
  for others**, as it does in the **Projects** list.

**Add a goal…** under the goals makes a new goal that serves the aspiration, and **Add a project…** under a goal with no project yet makes a project for it. You stay on the aspiration.

Each row is one title and one quiet second line, for example "1 goal · Not looked at yet ·
Health": how many goals serve it, **when you last looked at it** ("Looked at 12 days ago", or
"Not looked at yet"), and an area that serves it. When one of its goals needs attention, a small
orange capsule sits at the right of the row. An aspiration with nothing under it yet says
**Nothing serves this yet** in its second line.

Right-click a row (press and hold on the iPhone) for **Open the note** and **I looked at it today**.

**The reason** sits at the top of an aspiration, under its name. It is why this matters to you, in your own words, in the deep gold of an aspiration. It comes from the part of the note under `## Why`.

- Press **Reason…** and the whole text opens right there. Write it, then press **Save**. **Cancel** closes the box and writes nothing. Return does not save; press **Save**.
- The page shows the first four lines. When you wrote more, the box shows all of it.
- When no reason is written, a dashed grey line says **No reason written yet.**, and the button says **Write the reason…**.
- The Goal template puts a question under `## Why`: "What changes in my life when this is true?". The question is not a reason. It counts as nothing written. The same goes for the question in your own Goal template, if you have reworded it.
- **Save** puts your words in place of the question. A note with no `## Why` heading gets one, above its first other section. Writing it by hand in the note's text works as well.
- **Save** with an empty box takes the reason away, heading and all. When no reason was written, it changes nothing, so the template's question stays.
- If the reason was changed on your other device while the box was open, nothing is written. An orange line says what it is now, and your words stay in the box. Press **Save** again to use yours instead.
- While iCloud is still bringing a newer copy of the note, the button is grey. If no reason is shown yet, an orange line says why. Try again in a moment.
- On the iPhone the reason is the first thing inside an aspiration when you open it in the list.
- Only an aspiration shows a reason. A goal with a date does not show one yet, and neither do **Start the day** and the **Weekly review**. The text under `## Why` in a goal with a date stays in its note.

**There is no per cent on an aspiration, and that is deliberate.** You never finish one, so a
figure would be a promise the idea does not make. Each goal under it keeps its own bar, where
the number is true.

### The Goals screen

The **Goals** row lists **every goal that has a target date**, whether or not an aspiration
holds it. You do not have to choose anything first. Three groups, in this order:

- **Needs a look** — the goals the weekly review has something to say about: past their target
  date, nothing serving them, no next action. It is where to start, not a fault list.
- **By date** — the rest, soonest first.
- **Done**, **Missed** and **Dropped** — closed, with the count on the heading. Press a heading
  to look back.

A goal with no aspiration above it carries a **Give it an aspiration** button at the right of
its row. Right-click any
row (press and hold on the iPhone) for the same list, with **Open the note** and
**I looked at it today** above it.

Every link in the chain has its own icon: an **aspiration** is a **star** — the north star,
what you steer by and never tick off — and a **goal with a target
date** is a **target**, what you aim at and hit on a day. A **project** is a flag, a **task**
is a ring, and an **area** is the four squares. They are the same icons the sidebar uses: the
row named **Aspirations** carries the star, the row named **Goals** carries the target, and the
star is used for one thing only in the whole app — an aspiration. A note's own header shows
which of the two it is, with the icon and in words: **Aspiration** or **Goal with a date**.
If you give an aspiration or a goal an icon of your own (see **Your own icon**), your icon takes the place of the star or the target in the lists and on its page. The row you are in (**Aspirations** or **Goals**) and the words at the top of the note still say which of the two it is.

They also have their own colours: an **aspiration is a deeper gold** and a **goal with a date**
is the lighter gold the Goals list has always used.

A goal with no aspiration above it carries a **Give it an aspiration** button at the right of
its row, which opens the list of your aspirations. Nothing is wrong with such a goal — it is a
loose end, not an error — but the chain only works when each link points at the next.

Each row is one title and one quiet second line: its **per cent**, then its **target date and
how long is left**, for example "0 % · 2027-08-15 · in about 9 months" ("11 days over" once it
has passed). When it **needs attention**, a small orange capsule sits at the right of the row;
the checks are the same ones the weekly **Review** makes, so the two screens agree. The measure
is in the facts panel on the goal's page.

Press a row and, on the Mac and the iPad, the goal's page opens beside the list: its band (title at
26 points in white, per cent large) and its facts panel (see **Changing a goal's date and measure**), and the projects that
deliver it in a tinted panel headed **Projects**, with **Add a project…** at its foot.

A goal that has ended leaves the live lists and gathers at the foot under the name of its
ending — **Done**, **Missed** or **Dropped** — closed, with the count beside it. Press the
heading to look back. Inside an aspiration the same groups sit below the live goals.

A goal or aspiration also shows **what changed lately**: tasks finished in the last 30 days,
how many projects are finished, and when something last moved. When nothing can be counted the
line is not shown at all.

**All of them.** The button at the top right of the **Aspirations** screen opens every
aspiration at once, as one outline: each aspiration, the goals that serve it, the projects under those, and
the next action on each. Goals with no aspiration are at the foot, so nothing is missing. Press
it again for one at a time. On the iPhone the same button opens every row; tapping one row
closes the rest.

The **Note** button at the top of the column shows the goal's own text instead of the chain.
Press it again to go back. On the iPhone there is no second column, so an aspiration folds open
where it stands. Type in the search field and the ordinary note list comes back.

### How far a goal has come

Every goal with a target date shows a bar and a per cent, on its own page, in the Goals list and on its row in **Weekly review**. The figure is worked out from the projects under the goal. You never type it in.

Every project counts the same, whatever its size, so a project with forty small tasks does not drown one with three big ones.

- A project marked **done** counts as a whole one.
- A project still running counts as the share of its tasks that are ticked. Sub-tasks and cancelled tasks are left out, and so a project with no tasks yet counts as nothing done.
- The goal's figure is the average of its projects.

**Areas are not counted.** An area is something you keep up, not something that finishes, so counting one would hold its goal below full for ever.

**An aspiration has no bar of its own.** You never finish one, so there is nothing for a per cent to mean. Each goal under it has a bar, and that is where the figure belongs.

**No bar at all means there is nothing to measure yet** — no project under the goal. That is not the same as nought per cent, so the app draws nothing rather than an empty bar.

The per cent never says 100 until everything really is finished, and never says 0 once something has moved.

## Today, Calendar, daily and weekly notes

- **Today** shows the day's calendar events, one next action per active project (a line you are waiting on somebody else for is never one), overdue tasks, tasks due today, and undated tasks marked `!!` or more. The events are kept short here — one line each, with the place after the name — because they share the screen with everything else. Press the word **Calendar** in that heading to fold the events away; the number beside it (a small capsule in the heading's colour) still says how many there are, and pressing it again brings them back. **Next actions**, **Overdue** and **Due today** fold the same way (since build 291), and the app remembers which are folded. The big "0 due today" number is no longer in the purple band: the **Due today** list says it. Instead the band carries a small round white calendar button (since build 304): it opens today's note, or creates it while there is none (hold the pointer over it on the Mac to read **Today's note** or **Create today's note**). Tasks written in today's note still show under **From today's note**, but only when there are some. In a calendar row, the "open in Calendar" mark is a small round button tinted in the event's calendar colour. The day's date is in the band, in small white words, written like **Thursday 2026-11-12** (since build 309; before, "Thursday, 12 November"). On the Mac it has a line of its own under the title and the three round buttons, the whole width of the band, so it is never cut off. On the iPhone it is in one row beside the buttons. The **Overdue** heading carries two small buttons: a **sun** moves every overdue task to today, a **sunrise** moves them all to tomorrow. They are the same two pictures the Inbox uses for those dates. One press does the lot, and nothing is asked first. A short message says how many moved, and it has an **Undo** button for about eight seconds: each task gets back the date it had before (see **Undo for one-press changes**). Since build 304 the buttons are in one row: at the top are three small round buttons, the same size, in this order: today's note (a calendar), **Start the day** (an orange cup) and **Close the day** (a lilac moon): the note, the morning step and the evening step. Since build 312 each has one tiny word under it: **Note**, **Start** and **Close**. The pictures and the places are unchanged, and the band is about 13 points taller. Before, they were pictures only. On the Mac, hold the pointer over one and its full name appears. The band is one row shorter than in build 303, so the lists start higher.

- **Now and Then** on **Today**: on a day with a plan, two short lines under the date say where you are in it. The first starts with **Now** and names the plan block you are in, for example **Now 09:30 – 11:00 Write the offer**. On its right is a small orange button with a clock. Press it and the focus timer starts on that block, and the bar appears at the bottom, as usual. If exactly one open task has the same name as the block, the timer can tick that task off when you stop it (see **The focus timer**). While the focus timer runs on that block, a small dot is shown in place of the button: orange while the clock runs, grey while it is paused. The dot also shows when the clock runs on a task with the same name as the block, such as the task the block was made from. The second line is grey and starts with **Then**: the next block and when it starts, for example **Then 11:00 Call Anna**. It has no button. The lines change by themselves when a block starts or ends. When nothing is on now, only the **Then** line is shown. When the day has no plan, or the last block is over, nothing is shown, and **Today** is as short as before. Only plan blocks are shown here; your calendar events are under **Calendar** further down. On the Mac, hold the pointer over the clock button and its name appears.

- **The weekly review is late** (since build 315). When the last **Review done** is more than 7 days old, one line under the date on **Today** says so, for example **Last weekly review: 9 days ago**, in the Weekly review's colour. Tap it (click on the Mac) to open the **Weekly review**. A review done exactly a week ago is on time, so a review every Sunday never shows the line. Before the first **Review done** there is nothing to count from, and the line is not shown.
- **No weekly review yet** (since build 316). When PARAGON has been used on this device for 7 days or more, and **Review done** was never pressed, the same place on **Today** says **No weekly review yet**. A press opens the **Weekly review**. After the first **Review done** it is gone for good, and the line above takes over. It is not shown while notes are still coming from iCloud. At the first start of build 316, PARAGON notes your first day: today, or the day of the oldest backup inside the vault when that is earlier.

- **Start the day** shows what is overdue, what is due today, and your next actions. A line you are waiting on somebody else for is not a next action; it shows with the dated tasks once its follow-up date has come. At the top are three things you already know this morning:
  - **Last evening you wrote**: the line you wrote yesterday in **Close the day**, under **How was today?**. If you wrote nothing, this part is not shown. If yesterday's note is still coming from iCloud, an orange line says so. The line belongs to the day on which you pressed **Done**: if you close the day after midnight, it goes into the new day's note, and the next morning nothing is shown here.
  - **This week:** and the goal for the week, the same line as on **Today**. It is not shown when no goal is written. If this week's note is still coming from iCloud, an orange line says so. You write the goal on **Calendar › Week**.
  - **Today's calendar events**, one short line each, with the number in the heading. Press the heading to fold them away, and again to open them. The iPhone and the Mac each remember this on their own. If Calendar access is off, the message that says so is never folded away. When **Show the day's events in Today and in daily notes** is off in **Settings › Apple Calendar**, this part is not shown.
- Under those three things there is a field, **Add an action for today**, with a **+** beside it. On the Mac the cursor is already in it when the screen opens, so you can type at once. Type something that came to mind and press **+** (or Return). It goes to the Inbox, dated today, and appears in the list already picked. The field stays ready for the next one. If you type your own date, for example `>2026-10-02`, that date is kept, and the action is saved to the Inbox but not shown in today's list. On the iPhone, drag the list down to put the keyboard away.
- In **Start the day**, press **Pick** at the left of the two or three that matter most (the round circle after it marks the task as done, as everywhere else), then press the button at the bottom (it says **Plan 2 actions**, for example). Each one goes into today's plan as a block as long as the task's own time, or one hour when it has none, in the free time left today: after the time it is now, and never on top of a block you already have, a calendar event or another task that has a time of day (a call at 14:00 keeps its hour). A task in a note that is still coming from iCloud is not known yet, so its hour is not kept free; the message after the button then says so, and **Plan** shows the task once its note has arrived. **A picked task that has a time of day goes in at that time, if the hour is free**; if it is taken, or the time has already passed, the block goes into the first free time instead. Nothing is placed after midnight; if not all of them fit, a message says how many did. An action already in today's plan says **In the plan at** and the time in place of **Pick**. The blocks are ordinary plan blocks — move them or make them longer on **Plan**. With nothing picked, the button says **Done** and only closes.
- **Morning reminder.** In **Settings › Morning reminder**, turn on **Remind me to start the day** and choose a time (07:30 unless you change it). PARAGON then sends one notification every morning at that time: "Start the day. What matters most today? Pick it and put it in your plan." Press it and **Start the day** opens. On the iPhone the keyboard does not come up by itself, so you see the whole list first. It comes every day, at the weekend too, because your leisure tasks are in PARAGON as well. Turn the switch off and it stops.
- **Evening reminder.** In **Settings › Evening reminder**, turn on **Remind me to close the day** and choose a time (21:00 unless you change it). PARAGON then sends one notification a day at that time. Press it and **Close the day** opens. Turn the switch off and it stops.
- **The explanation in Start the day and Close the day (since build 312).** The paragraph of explanation is shown the first 3 times you open the screen. **Start the day** and **Close the day** each count for themselves. From the 4th time there is only a small hand-drawn info mark, called **What to do here**; press it to show the paragraph, and again to hide it. The one-line hints under the fields are unchanged. Before, both paragraphs were shown every day and pushed the tasks down the screen.
- **Both reminders work the same way.** The first time you turn one on, the iPhone or the Mac asks whether PARAGON may send notifications. That one answer is for both reminders and for the focus timer. Under the time, Settings says when the next reminder will come, for example "Next reminder: tomorrow at 07:30", or with the day when it is further away: "Next reminder: Saturday 2026-11-14 at 07:30". Times are always 24 hours. If no reminder can come, it says why in orange and where to change it. Each part has its own **Try it now**, which sends the same notification once, so you can try it now. Press that notification and the same screen opens as from the real one. Below the button it says what happened: when it was sent, and 15 seconds later whether the iPhone or the Mac delivered it. The iPhone and the Mac each have their own switches and times. A Focus mode, such as Do Not Disturb, can hold a notification back, and PARAGON cannot see that.
- **When you press a reminder and a screen is already open.** If it is the same screen, it stays as it is, with what you typed in it. If another screen is open, such as **New**, nothing is closed and nothing you typed is lost: **Start the day** or **Close the day** opens as soon as you close that screen. If the focus timer's question is open, or the question **What is next for this goal?** is on screen, it opens after you have answered it. On the Mac, if the main PARAGON window is closed, it opens when you next open the main window. The Settings, Help and **Plan** windows do not open it.
- **Close the day** is one page you work down and finish. From the top:
  - **Did not happen**: everything still open that was due today or earlier. Each has a
    **Tomorrow** button and a **Day…** button for another date, and **Move all to tomorrow** is
    under them. These are real tasks, so you can tick one off if you did it after all. Tasks
    with no date are not here, because a task that was never promised to today did not fail
    to happen.
  - **Tomorrow's calendar events**, with tomorrow's date, then **To do tomorrow**:
    everything dated tomorrow (including what you just moved there) and a few next actions. Press **First** at the left of the
    ones that should come first. That only marks them: in the morning, **Start the day** shows
    them first in its list, above **Overdue**, under **Marked first last evening**. No block is made. Press **First** again
    to take the mark off. The marks are kept in the vault, so a mark made on the iPhone is also
    there on the Mac.
  - **How was today?**: one line ("One line, if you feel like it"), kept in that day's daily
    note under a heading called **Looking back**. On the Mac the cursor starts in this field when
    the screen opens. It is ordinary text: nothing counts it and it never becomes a task. The
    next morning, **Start the day** shows it under **Last evening you wrote**, and step 7 of the
    **Weekly review**, which looks back at a whole week, shows it again, and so does that day's
    card in **Done**. Clear it and the heading goes too.
  - **What you did today**: how many tasks you finished, and what they went towards — an
    aspiration, a goal, an area, or the project itself — with how many for each. The names of
    the tasks are on the **Done** screen.
  - **Planned and worked**: a small table with three columns, **Task**, **Planned** and
    **Worked**, and **All of today** at the bottom. **Planned** is the length of the block in
    **Plan**. For a task you worked on without a block, it is the task's own time, such
    as **45 min**. **Worked** is what the focus timer wrote under **Worked** in today's note. A
    time worked that is longer than the time planned is orange and bold. A dash means there is
    nothing to show: no block and no task time, or the timer was not used on it. Blocks and work
    are put on one row by their name. A block made by **Start the day** has the same name as the
    work on that task, so they meet on one row. If you change the name of the block or of the
    task afterwards, also by adding a tag, they show on two rows, and **All of today** counts the
    planned time of both. If two tasks have the same name but different times, neither time is
    used. With **Use task times** off, a task's own time is not used here. A timer that is not
    stopped yet is not in the table: its time is added when you stop it, and a line under the
    table says so.
  **Done** saves the line; **Cancel** does not — but the tick boxes, **Tomorrow**, **Day…** and
  **First** save as soon as you press them.
- **All actions** lists every open task in the vault, grouped by its note. Since build 301 the boxes at the top are small, rounded chips on the Mac and the iPhone alike (**Search** got the same chips in build 302). Each row has a small grey capital name (**WHEN**, **MARK**, **TAGS**) instead of a big green heading; a chip that is off has a soft grey fill, a chip that is ticked is filled in the screen's green with white words and a small tick. The empty gap under the boxes is gone: the area is only as tall as its boxes. The chips narrow the list: **When** (Overdue, Today, Next 7 days, Later, No date), **Mark** (Next action, Important) and **Tags**. Boxes in the same row mean "any of these"; boxes in different rows are all required together. Since build 312 the top of the screen is one row: the switch **Actions 3 | Waiting for**, with the number of open actions inside it, and a button with the word **Filter**. **Filter** shows the boxes and folds them away; it is there only on the **Actions** half. Only while a box is ticked, a second line shows the ticked boxes, a count such as "2 of 5", and **Clear**, which unticks everything. So the list is never quietly narrowed. Before build 312 the filter was a picture with no name, and the line "Every open action" stood under the title; both are gone.
  - **Waiting for.** Since build 312 it is the right half of the switch **Actions | Waiting for**, on the iPhone and on the Mac. When lines are waiting, the half shows how many, for example **Waiting for 2**. On the iPhone the words of the chosen half are in the green of **Actions**. Before, it was a small hourglass button with no name beside the heading. While **Waiting for** is chosen, the column shows the lines you are waiting on other people for, one heading for each person, instead of the boxes: the boxes sort actions, and this list sorts people.
  - **What a waiting line is.** An open task with the tag `#waiting`, or one that starts with the words **Waiting for** (capitals do not matter). It looks like this in the file: `- [ ] Waiting for Anna: the quote >2026-11-06 #waiting @since(2026-11-01)`. Lines you wrote by hand are found too, even with no tag. The name is the words between **Waiting for** and the first colon; the thing is what follows. A line with no colon has no name. It is listed under **Someone**, with **No name given**. Anna and anna are one person. A ticked or cancelled line is not waiting any more, and neither is a line in a project that is done, missed, dropped or archived.
  - **The list.** First a count line, for example **4 things · 2 people**. Then one heading for each person, A to Z, with **Someone** last. Under a heading every line is an ordinary task row, with the note it lives in: tick it when the answer came, and the line is done. Under it a grey line says when to follow up and how long you have waited, for example **Follow up 2026-11-06 · waited 5 days** (before build 309: "Follow up Fri 6 Nov"). The follow-up date is the line's ordinary date. A line with no date says **No follow-up date**. When the date has passed it says **Follow up was 3 days ago**, in red. Inside a heading the lines go by follow-up date, then the longest wait.
  - **How long you have waited needs `@since`.** `@since(2026-11-01)` on the line is the day the waiting began. A task line has no date for when it was made, so without `@since` the line never says **waited**: PARAGON would have to guess. **Waiting for…** and the **Waiting for** snippet both write it. A snippets file you already have is not changed; add `@since({{date}})` to its **Waiting for** line by hand if you want it.
  - **Follow up next week**, under a line, moves its date one week on: from its date, or from today when the date has passed or there is none.
  - **Nothing waiting.** The list says **Nothing is waiting for anyone**, with one button (since build 315): **Make a line wait…**. It opens Quick capture with the words "Waiting for " already typed. Write the name, a colon and the thing, like "Waiting for Anna: the quote", and press **Save**. The line lands in the Inbox and shows in this list under Anna. While notes are still coming from iCloud it says that instead, and an orange line under the count says how many are missing: the list may be short until they arrive.
  - **Making a line wait.** Right-click a task (press and hold on the iPhone) and choose **Waiting for…**. A small window asks **Who?**. Type a name, or press one of the people you already wait for, and press **Add** (or Return). PARAGON writes `Waiting for Anna: the quote` with the tag `#waiting` and today as `@since`. A task with no date also gets a follow-up date one week from today; a task that has a date keeps it as the follow-up date. Choose **Waiting for…** on a line that is already waiting and the window starts on its person: a new name changes the name and keeps the day the waiting began. **Not waiting any more** takes the words, the tag and `@since` off and keeps the date. If nothing but the name follows **Waiting for**, the name becomes the task's words. The Inbox's own line menu has no such item; file the line first.
  - **A waiting line is never your next action.** It is left out of the next task on a project's box, of the next actions on **Today**, **Start the day** and **Plan**, and of the next task on the Goals and Aspirations screens, even when it carries `#next`. **Make this the next action** is not offered on a waiting line, so it cannot take `#next` off the real next action. A line that has a date is still in the lists by date, and that includes **Overdue** and **Due today** on **Today**, in **Start the day** and on **Plan**: when its follow-up date comes, chasing it is something to do. It is also in **All actions** and the Calendar. The **Next action** box under **Mark** means the `#next` tag and is unchanged.
- **Done** is a diary of the last 12 weeks: this week and the 11 weeks before it, each from Monday to Sunday. The newest day is at the top. The line at the top says how many tasks you finished this week and in the 12 weeks, for example **6 done this week · 212 in 12 weeks**. It is not shown until a finished task carries a day. Under it, **Last 12 weeks** shows one small square for each day: each column is one week, with Monday at the top and this week on the right, and the months above the columns, written like **2026-11**. The stronger the colour of a square, the more tasks were done that day; a grey square means nothing was ticked off. A day whose note is still coming from iCloud has an empty square with an orange dashed edge, and it says so: that day may not be empty, its note is just not here yet. The days still to come this week have no square, and nor do the days before you first ticked off a task with a day: for those the notes cannot say. Today's square has a frame around it. On the Mac, hold the pointer over a square to see its day and how many tasks were done. Press a square to go to that day in the list below; only a day that is listed can be pressed. Press **Last 12 weeks** to fold the squares away, and again to bring them back. The iPhone and the Mac each remember this on their own. Each day starts with a small card:
  - The day's name, for example **Today**, **Yesterday** or **Tuesday 2026-10-14**.
  - A grey line with how many tasks you finished and the time on the focus timer, for example **6 done · 2 h 40 min worked**. When the timer was not used that day, no time is shown.
  - The line you wrote that evening under **How was today?** in **Close the day**, beside a lilac moon.
  - Small labels for what the work went towards: an aspiration, a goal, an area, or the project itself, each with how many tasks. Tasks ticked in that day's own daily note share one label, **Daily note**. A weekly note, or an earlier day's note, keeps its own name, for example **Week 43 · 2026** for a weekly note or **Monday 2026-10-19** for a daily note.
  - Under the card are the tasks you finished that day. Press one to open the note it came from.
  - A day when you ticked nothing, but wrote a line in **Close the day** or used the focus timer, is listed too. Its grey line says **Nothing ticked off**.
  - A task counts on the day in its `@done(…)` stamp. A task ticked with no stamp has no day, so it is not listed here.
  - If a day's own note is still coming from iCloud, its card says **Still coming from iCloud** in orange, in place of the evening line. That line and the timer time are in the note, so they show when it arrives. On the iPhone an orange line at the top of **Done** also says that notes are still coming. When nothing is listed while notes are still coming, **Done** says so instead of saying there is nothing.
- **Calendar** lets you pick a day, week or month. The day view can show a month grid with week numbers and a dot under every day that holds something — green for tasks due, red if one is overdue, blue for calendar events, grey for a day that already has a note. Under the grid is the day itself: its events, what is due, what got done, and a button to open or create the daily note. The grid is off to begin with, because the day itself is what the screen is for: press the **calendar** button in the day's top row to show it, and press it again to put it away. The button is lit while the grid is showing, and the app remembers your choice. Move with the arrows, the Today button, or the left and right arrow keys. Drop a task on a day to give it that date. The day's heading is written like **Thursday 2026-11-12**. The grey line under it starts with the week, for example **Week 46 · 3 due · 2 events** or **Week 46 · nothing planned** (before build 309 it said "v. 46"). The heading of the month grid and of **Calendar › Month** is the month, written **2026-11**, and the seven letters over the days are always M T W T F S S.
- **Calendar › Week** is where you plan a week. At the very top is **Goal for this week**:
  one line that says what the week is about. When the arrows move to another week, the box
  says so: **Goal for next week**, **Goal for last week**, **Goal for week 45**. Press the box, write the line, and press Return
  or **Done**. An empty box is grey and dashed; a written goal is gold. Save an empty line to
  remove the goal. It is kept in the week's note as `focus: …`, and **Today** shows it in one
  line under the date. Below the goal is **Not planned yet**: every open task
  with no date yet, next actions first, drawn as dashed tiles. Under it is a row of seven small
  cells, one per day, each with the number of tasks due on it — today's cell is marked. Under
  the number is the total of their task times, for example **2 h 30**; a task with no time
  adds nothing. **Drag a
  tile onto a cell** and the task gets that day. You can drag onto a day's heading further down
  as well, and you can drag a task from one day to another the same way.
  The tray shows the first twelve and says how many more there are. It is not drawn at all when
  there is nothing to put in it. A task written in a daily note is never in the tray: it is already on that
  day by being there.
  The quickest way in is the **Week** button at the top of the **Plan** screen. On the iPhone, since build 312, that button shows the week inside the **Plan** tab, under the title **Plan the week** (see **Plan**).
  The heading over the seven days names the week and its first and last day, for example
  **Week 46 · 2026-11-09 – 2026-11-15**. Each day's heading is written like **Thursday 2026-11-12**,
  and the seven small cells say **Mon**, **Tue** and so on. A day with nothing on it says **Nothing planned**.
- A daily note shows the day's events and the tasks due that day above its own text. A weekly note shows all seven days as a plan: drop tasks onto a day, tick them off there. Each of the seven days is written like **Thursday 2026-11-12**.
- **The name of a daily or weekly note (since build 309).** In the top bar, in **Recent** and in every other list, a daily note is called by its day, for example **Thursday 2026-11-12**, and a weekly note by its week, **Week 46 · 2026**. Before, they showed the file's name, "20261112" and "2026-W46". The files keep those names. The heading PARAGON writes as the first line of a **new** daily note is unchanged, for example "# Thursday, 12 November 2026" (a new weekly note: "# Week 46, 9 Nov – 15 Nov 2026"). It is part of your note, so PARAGON does not rewrite it, in new notes or in old ones.
- **Reading a note on the Mac (since build 309).** While the cursor is not in the note, every "#" and "**" is hidden, on the first line too. So a daily note opens as "Thursday, 12 November 2026", not "# Thursday, 12 November 2026". Click into a line and the signs of that line show, so you can change them. The iPhone already did this.

## Apple Calendar and Time Blocks

The app reads events from the calendars you choose in Settings › Apple Calendar. Events appear in Today and in daily notes. Double-click an event, or use its arrow button, to open it in the Calendar app.

The Calendar section shows the day's schedule in the right-hand column: hours down the side (always 24 hours: 09:00, 10:00), events in place, your time blocks on top, a line for the time it is now, and a strip at the top for all-day events and tasks with no time. A task that has a time of day is drawn at its hour, as long as the task's own time (`~45m`), or half an hour when it has none. An event that began the day before is drawn from 00:00 on this day, and one that goes on past midnight is drawn down to 24:00, so nothing runs out of the day; the time written under its name is still the event's own. Press **+ Block time** or double-click an hour to reserve time; click a block to change or delete it; drag a task onto an hour to block that hour for it. The **Note** / **Schedule** switch swaps between the schedule and the daily note. On the Mac, **+ Block time** and the switch are in the toolbar at the top of the window; on the iPhone and the iPad they are at the top of the column.

### Plan

There is a second kind of block, and it is the opposite of a Time Block: it stays inside
PARAGON. It is what the **Plan** section shows. **Go › Plan** (⇧⌘P) on the
Mac opens the same thing in a window of its own, which can stay up while you work in a note.

On today, a slate blue line and dot cross both lanes at the time it is now, and move as the day goes on. It is the same line the Calendar section's day view draws. The colour is its own on purpose: every other colour in PARAGON means a kind of note or a warning, and the time of day is neither.

The screen has three parts:

- **Actions** in the middle column: what is due that day, then your next actions. Each row is a
  real task: tick the circle to finish it, right-click (long-press on the iPhone) for the task
  menu, and press **+** to make a block for it. Under each one is what it serves — the goal its
  note belongs to, in gold, or the area it sits in, in pink.
- **Calendar** on the right: what is already booked that day, read from Apple Calendar. An event that began the day before is drawn from 00:00 on this day, and one that goes on past midnight is drawn down to 24:00. This screen draws the hours from 06:00, so the box is drawn from 06:00 on, and the times written in it are the event's times on this day. An event that ends before 06:00 is not drawn on this screen.
- **Time blocks** beside it: your own blocks. They hang on the same hours as the calendar, so
  you can see whether a block lands inside something already booked. Two things at the same
  time stand side by side at half the width, so nothing is hidden behind anything else. **Drag an
  action from the Actions column onto an hour here** and it becomes a block of one hour at that
  hour, named after the task. Nothing is asked first: drag its edge to make it longer, or
  right-click it to remove it. The task keeps its own tick box in the Actions column — a block
  says when you mean to do the work, it does not do the work.

**A task with a time of day is drawn there too.** Give a task a time with the **At**
line in its date window, and on the day it is due **Plan** shows a small card in the **Time
blocks** lane at that hour, as long as the task's own time (`~45m`), or half an hour when it
has none. Two things at the same hour share the width, as everything here does. The card looks
different from a block on purpose: no orange, a thin border in the colour of the text and a small circle, because it
is a fixed point in your day and not a block of yours. It cannot be dragged or made longer.
Press it to open the task's note; tick the task in the **Actions** column. Only open tasks are
drawn. A task that ends before 06:00 is not drawn on this screen, but it is in the Actions list.
A task that starts before 06:00 and ends after it is drawn from 06:00 on, and its card still shows
its own times. When a block of yours has the task's name and lies over its hour, only
the block is drawn, so one task is not shown twice.

On the iPhone the parts are one page instead. At the top, above the hours, the actions are
shown again as small dashed tiles under **Actions**, and they stay there while the hours scroll
under them. **Scroll to the hour you want, hold a tile, and drag it onto that hour** in the
**Time blocks** lane. **Since build 312 you can also tap a tile.** A small menu opens, with the
action's name as its title: **Next free time**, **Pick a time…** and **Cancel**. **Next free time**
adds a block and says "Added to your plan at 14:15." (with the real time). The block is as long as
the task's own time, or 60 minutes when it has none. It starts at the time it is now, rounded up to
the quarter hour (at 06:00 on another day), and never lies on a block, a calendar event or a task
with a time of day. It looks for room until midnight. When there is no room, nothing is placed and
it says "No free time left on this day. Pick a time instead." **Pick a time…** opens the **New
block** sheet, the same one as **+ Block**, with the action's name, its length and the next free
start filled in. The hint above the tiles says "Tap one for a time, or hold and drag it onto an
hour." Up to six tiles are shown; the line beside the heading says how many more
there are. The full **Actions** list is still at the foot of the page, below the evening, for
ticking a task off, the task menu and the **+** button (which makes a one-hour block at the next
free hour). Tap a block to change its time. A short event or block — under about three quarters of an hour — is drawn on one line,
with its start time and its name.

The **Week** button shows the week, where you spread work across the seven days. On the Mac and the iPad it opens **Calendar › Week**. On the iPhone, since build 312, the week slides in inside the **Plan** tab: the title says **Plan the week**, the **Plan** tab stays lit, and the back arrow returns to your day.
