The copy is made once a day, after the daily copy, and with **Back up now**, and old ones are thinned out by the same rules as above. It holds the notes, the settings and also the pictures and sketches, and since build 318 the PDFs in **Attachments** too; the copies inside the vault have no pictures and no PDFs, on purpose. The section shows **Kept** ("on this Mac" or "in the folder “Dropbox”") and **Last made** (the date and time, like "2026-11-12 14:30", "Being made now…" while the first one is being made, or "Not yet"). A second copy that has never been made is made as soon as the vault is open, in the background; the first one copies every picture and can take a while. If the folder you chose cannot be reached, the copy is kept on this device instead and the section says "The folder you chose could not be reached, so this copy was kept on this Mac. Choose the folder again."; the backups inside the vault are made as always. There is no restore from the outside copy inside the app yet: take notes back from it by hand.

**Putting the outside copy back (since build 315).** Press **Restore from the outside copy…** in **Settings › Second copy, outside the vault**. PARAGON names the vault and shows the newest copy: when it was made, like 2026-11-13 14:05, where it is kept, and how many notes it holds. With more than 1 copy it lists them, newest first, each with its date, its number of notes, and "made just before a restore" for a copy a restore saved; the newest is chosen, and a press on another row chooses that one. Only copies of this vault are offered: each new outside copy says which vault it is of. A copy made before build 315 does not say it; it is shown with the words "made before build 315" and the names of its folders. The buttons are **Cancel** and **Restore**; on the Mac, Escape is **Cancel**. **Restore** puts its notes, pictures and sketches back into the vault, over the versions you have now. Before that, what you have now is saved twice: as a backup inside the vault, named "before restore", and as a fresh outside copy that holds your pictures. If these cannot be saved, nothing is restored. Nothing is ever deleted: what you made after the copy stays, and so does the copy. A note that is still coming from iCloud is never written over, and while notes are still coming from iCloud the restore does not start. A file of the copy that is not on this device is not put back and is named; the note in the vault stays as it was. No sync runs during a restore, and a restore does not start during a sync. With no outside copy yet, the question says "There is no outside copy yet." and has only **Cancel**. To undo a restore, use **Restore a copy** and choose the one marked "before restore". Before build 315 the outside copy could only be put back by hand, in Finder.

**After a restore, one line stays (since build 316).** Under the button **Restore from the outside copy…** one line says what the last restore did, for example **Restored 15 notes from 2026-11-13 08:57**. The day and time are the copy's. With pictures or other files too it says "Restored 15 notes and 4 other files from 2026-11-13 08:57"; if some files could not be written, it says how many. The line is still there after you close **Settings** and after you quit PARAGON, until the next restore. It is kept on each device by itself. While a restore runs, the same place says **Restoring…**, and so do the sync line and the sync ring. Before build 316 there was only a short message that went away after a few seconds, and it counted files, not notes.

**One folder per vault (since build 316).** Inside the folder **PARAGON Backups**, each vault has its own folder. Its name is the vault's name and the vault's identity (36 letters and numbers), for example "Notes 6F1C…". New outside copies go there. Clearing out old copies only touches copies that are surely this vault's own; a copy with no mark, and a copy of another vault, is never cleared out. Copies made by earlier builds stay where they are, straight in **PARAGON Backups**; they are still listed under **Restore from the outside copy…** and can still be restored. Nothing is moved. Before build 316 all copies of all vaults were in the one folder, and 2 vaults there could clear out each other's older copies.

**One vault, one identity (since build 316).** If the iPhone and the Mac each gave the vault an identity before iCloud had brought the first one across, both now keep the older one. The other one stays known as an older name of the same vault, so copies marked with it are still offered for a restore.

**Backups and the copy to PARAGON's own folder (since build 319).** Before PARAGON copies your notes to its own folder, or back, it makes an outside copy in the place of the second copy and checks it: every file in the copy must be the same as in the vault, nothing may be left out, and the number of notes must be the same. If the check fails, nothing is copied. The backup copy is listed under **Restore from the outside copy…** like the others. The files the copy writes for itself (`.ams-para/move/`, **PARAGON has moved these notes.txt**, and `.ams-para/Set aside by a move/`) are not part of any backup, inside or outside the vault, and a restore never writes them. The backups inside the vault and the deleted notes are copied with the vault. The outside copies stay in the same folder as before, because the vault keeps its identity.

Time Machine is a good third net on the Mac.

## Exact safety rules (build 307)

The precise rules behind the sync and the backups, for reference.

- **Mass-change brake.** Counted per sync: open tasks that would be cancelled plus open reminders that would be deleted. The brake stops the sync when the count is more than 5, or when it is more than one third of the tasks in one list that has 3 or more tasks. Nothing is changed before you answer. Buttons: **Do it** (the sync runs as planned) and **Cancel** (nothing changes). The timed automatic sync never runs a mass change; it waits for **Sync now**. A list that cannot be loaded counts as "not known", never as "all deleted".
- **Following a reminder.** A task is cancelled only when its reminder is found in no list. First the reminder is looked up by its own identity, then by its PARAGON mark across all lists. If found in another list, the task follows it.
- **Backup tiers.** The newest copy of all; the 5 newest before-sync copies; every other copy of today and yesterday; 1 per day for 14 days; 1 per week for 2 months (61 days). The day's and the week's copy is one that is not a before-sync copy when there is one. Clearing runs after a new copy or a restore, never on the copy being restored.
- **Restore.** The restored backup is protected until the restore is done. The result says how many notes were written; zero is reported as "nothing was put back".
- **Background copies.** The copy before a sync and **Back up now** run in the background. The sync starts when the copy is finished. The copy before a rename is still made in the foreground.
- **Sync memory.** Missing: the first sync takes over ticks and edits from Reminders (no note-wins on that first run). Unreadable: the sync stops with a message. The device identity is stored in two places.
- **Deleting a note with open reminders.** One question, **Remove reminders** or **Keep reminders**. Reminders that are already done always stay. Links are dropped either way, so no "missing or unreadable" warning is repeated. **Put back** links them again by their PARAGON mark.
- **Test copy of the Mac app.** Own name and own storage. Test mode refuses to run, and never erases settings, where a real notes folder is remembered.

## Exact safety rules (build 308)

The precise rules added in safety round 2, for reference.

- **Typing while the other device changes the note.** The editor remembers the version its text started from. On saving, if the note on disk is no longer that version, PARAGON compares the start, your text and the other text line by line. Changes to different lines, also neighbouring ones: both are kept in the note, with no message. Changes to the same line: your text is saved beside the note as "… (conflict date time).md", the note shows the other version, and a message says so. The sync's hidden mark on a task is not counted as a change to its line. A second copy in the same minute gets " 2" after the time; no copy is written over another. A "(conflict …)" copy is left out of the Reminders sync while it has that name. Nothing is ever written over a newer file.
- **Versions parked by iCloud.** Looked for after each re-read, for the notes that changed, and every 10 minutes for all notes. Each parked version is saved beside its note as "… (conflict date time).md", then marked as dealt with in iCloud, and you are told which notes are affected. A parked version that is the same as the note makes no copy. PARAGON does not merge or delete these for you.
- **Twin reminders.** Two reminders with the same PARAGON mark: the older one is kept, the twin is removed, a tick on the twin moves to the one kept, and the report says so. A twin with a different title is not removed. A task that arrived from another device, with no reminder for it anywhere, gets no new reminder in the sync it arrives in; it gets one in the next sync if none has turned up by then.
- **Reminders lists.** A linked list is found by its own identity first; its name is only used the first time. Of two lists with one name, the linked one is used and the other left alone. A list holding reminders PARAGON did not make is joined to a new note of the same name only after **Do it** in the alert **Sync stopped to ask you**; **Cancel** changes nothing. Not asked for empty lists, the Inbox and daily lists, lists named in `reminders-list:`, and lists already linked.
- **Outside backup.** A second copy outside the vault folder: on this device's own storage, or in a folder you choose (never one inside the vault). Made once a day after the daily copy and with **Back up now**, and at once when none has ever been made, with notes, settings, pictures and sketches, thinned out by the same rules as the copies inside the vault. The Settings section **Second copy, outside the vault** shows where it is kept and when it was last made. If the chosen folder cannot be reached, the copy is kept on this device and the section says so. A failed outside copy never stops the backup inside the vault. No restore from it inside the app in build 308; since build 315 there is one (see **Exact details (build 315)**).
- **Choosing a folder.** Looked at first, nothing written before you agree. An existing vault is opened with **Open it**, its note count shown; any other folder is set up only after **Set it up here**. A folder iCloud is still listing is waited for, not called empty. Starting the app and **Open … again** open directly.
- **Re-reading the vault.** Only notes whose file changed are read again. The check every 10 seconds, coming to the front and iCloud's notices read in the background. Task counts for the sidebar are worked out once per change of a note and kept.
- **File names.** A very long title gets a file name shortened to at most 180 bytes; the full title stays in the note's `title:` line and links find the note by it. Shortened names that would be the same get " 2", " 3". A rename that only changes capitals is allowed.
- **Sync and screen.** Each has its own access to the vault folder.
- **Very large notes.** While you type, only the lines you changed are restyled; the whole note is restyled 0.4 seconds after your last key.

## Exact details (build 315)

The precise rules and names behind round 8 of the premium review, for reference. The words in `code letters` are names in the app's code, in its files and in its tests. You do not need them to use PARAGON.

- **Keep them apart: the rule.** `ListsKeptApart` and `KeptApartList { id, name, device }` in `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Sync/ListsKeptApart.swift`. `device` is the device that made the choice. A list is kept apart when one of its own identities in Reminders is remembered. On another device than `device` the name also decides, when the remembered identity belongs to no list there (the same list can have another identity on the other device). On `device` itself only the identity counts. `droppingDeleted(deviceID:deviceListIDs:from:)` forgets this device's entries whose list is gone; `AppModel.forgetDeletedListsKeptApart` calls it after each finished sync, and never when Reminders gave no lists. `adding(id:name:device:to:)` never stores an identity twice and sorts by name; `removing(_:from:)` forgets one.
- **Keep them apart: where it is kept.** `VaultConfig.listsKeptApart`, the key `listsKeptApart` in `.ams-para/config.json` in the vault, beside `conflictPolicy`, `syncAreas` and `createMissingLists`. Both devices share it through the vault. An older file without the key reads as "no list kept apart". The file is read again when iCloud reports a change (`cloudFilesChanged`), at the start of each sync, and just before **Keep them apart** and **Connect again** (`Vault.reloadConfigIfChanged()`). Any other settings change takes `listsKeptApart` from the file as it is at that moment, so one device cannot erase a choice made on the other.
- **Keep them apart: in the sync.** `SyncEngine.sync`, step 2b-3. After the checks for "is this list ours", a list that is kept apart takes its note out of that sync whole: no link to a list, nothing written, no reminders made, none taken in, whatever the list holds, also when it is empty now. The order matters: a list that is already linked (by its remembered identity, by earlier links, or by a reminder with one of the note's marks) stays linked. `MassChangeSummary.ListJoin` has a new field `listID`. The timed, quiet sync never shows the question, as before.
- **Keep them apart: on screen.** `AppModel.keepApart(_:)` and `AppModel.connectAgain(_:)` in `SyncSafetyDialogs.swift`. The button is shown only when the question is about joining a list. After the press: the message "The list “X” is kept apart" (or "2 lists are kept apart"), then `syncNow()` again, without a yes to any mass change. Identifiers: `sync.mass.keepApart` (beside `sync.mass.doIt` and `sync.mass.cancel`), `settings.keptApart`, `settings.keptApart.connect.<list name>`. Test: `RoundEightATests.testAListKeptApartIsNeverJoinedAndNeverAskedAbout`.
- **Restore from the outside copy: the order.** `Vault.restoreOutsideCopy(_:in:now:)` in `VaultBackup.swift`. (1) A backup inside the vault, named "before restore". (2) A new outside copy in the folder `PARAGON Backups`, named "before restore"; none is made when the newest outside copy already equals the vault. If (1) or (2) fails, the restore stops before any file is written. (3) Every file of the copy is copied back, except `signature.txt`, `skipped.txt` and `paragon-copy-of.json`. Each file is first copied beside its place under a name starting `.paragon-restore-`, and only the finished copy takes the place (`Vault.putBack`, `replaceItemAt`); the vault file is never removed first. A file of the copy that is not on this device (`CloudFiles.presence == .away`, or `CloudFiles.isDataless` for Dropbox) counts as failed before anything is touched. The same holds for a restore of a backup inside the vault. A picture the vault already holds unchanged (same size and date) is skipped and not counted. A file that is still an iCloud placeholder, in the copy or in the vault, is never written over and is named in the result. Outside copies are not cleared out by a restore.
- **Restore from the outside copy: whose copy.** The vault has a lasting identity in `.ams-para/vault-id.txt`, made with the first outside copy of build 315 (`Vault.identity(creating:)`). Each new outside copy holds `paragon-copy-of.json` with that identity and the vault folder's name. `Vault.outsideCopiesForRestore(in:)` returns the copies with this vault's identity and the copies with no such file (`OutsideCopy.unmarkedFolders` then holds their top folder names). A copy with another vault's identity, or with a mark that cannot be read, is never offered. Tests: `RoundEightATests.testOnlyThisVaultsOutsideCopiesAreOfferedForRestore`, `testAFileThatCannotBeCopiedLeavesTheVaultFileUnchanged`.
- **Restore from the outside copy: on screen.** `AppModel.lookAtOutsideCopy()`, `cancelOutsideRestore()`, `restoreOutsideCopy()`; `chooseOutsideCopy(_:)`; the state is `OutsideRestoreChoice` with `.looking`, `.none`, `.copies` and `.restoring`, the vault's name and the chosen copy. The copies come from the place in use (the folder you chose, else this device), newest first, the newest chosen. The list is shown with more than 1 copy, or when a copy has no mark; it is at most 150 points high and scrolls. Looking and restoring run in the background. A restore is refused while a sync runs, and holds `isSyncing` until it ends, also when it fails, so no sync or re-read starts meanwhile. Refused, with the usual message, while notes are still coming from iCloud. The sheet is `OutsideRestoreView` in `SettingsView.swift`: 420 points wide on the Mac, 340 points high on the iPhone (half or full height when the list is shown). Identifiers: `settings.outsideBackup.restore`, `outsideRestore.sheet`, `outsideRestore.headline`, `outsideRestore.cancel`, `outsideRestore.confirm`, `outsideRestore.copy.<n>` (rows, from 0). Test: `RoundEightATests.testRestoringTheOutsideCopyPutsNotesAndPicturesBackAndSavesTheVaultFirst`. In the screen tests the place is a throwaway folder, so real copies are never read.
- **The version.** **Settings › About**, first row, `LabeledContent("Version", value: AppVersion.text)`, identifier `settings.about.version`. The foot of the sidebar (`sidebar.version`) and the foot of **Browse** are unchanged. Test: `RoundEightATests.testTheBuildNumberIsTheNewestWhatsNewEntry` reads `BuildStamp.number` in `App/Paragon/AppModel.swift` and the first "## Build N" heading of `Docs/VersionHistory.md`. The two must be the same number, and the file must contain "1.0 (N)".
- **What's new: the name.** `HelpView.Page.title` is "What's new" for `.versionHistory`. Its stored value stays "Version history", because an open Help window is remembered by it. The file is still `Docs/VersionHistory.md`; `HelpDocument(fileName:title:)` shows the title **What's new** in place of the file's first heading. Mac **Settings › Help** uses `HelpWindowButton(page:label:)`. The item in the Help menu is **What's New**. Identifiers: `settings.howItWorks` (now on the Mac too) and `settings.whatsNew`.
- **Review done.** `WeeklyReviewDone` in `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/WeeklyReviewDone.swift`. The key `review-done`, with a day like 2026-11-13, in the settings block of the weekly note of the week the button was pressed in. `NoteIndex.lastWeeklyReview()` is the newest such day over all weekly notes. `isLate(last:today:)` is true only when there is a last day and it is more than 7 days ago (`lateAfterDays = 7`); no day at all is never late; a day in the future counts as 0 days. `lateLine` gives "Last weekly review: N days ago". `AppModel.markWeeklyReviewDone()` makes the weekly note if it is missing and does nothing when the day is already today. Identifiers: `review.done`, `review.done.last`, `today.reviewLate`. Tests: `WeeklyReviewDoneTests` (2), and the screen test `testTheReviewEndsWithReviewDone`.
- **Weekly review reminder.** `DayReminder.weeklyReview`. Notification names `paragon.weeklyReview` and, for **Try it now**, `paragon.weeklyReview.test`. Kept on each device by itself, under `weeklyReviewReminderOn` (off at first), `weeklyReviewReminderMinutes` (1020, which is 17:00) and `weeklyReviewReminderWeekday` (1 to 7, where 1 is Sunday; 1 at first). One notification that repeats each week, on that weekday, hour and minute. Its title is "Weekly review"; its text is "Time to walk through the week: the Inbox, what is overdue, your projects and areas." A press opens the **Weekly review** as a place, not as a sheet; a sheet that is open is left alone. Identifiers: `settings.weeklyReviewReminder`, `settings.weeklyReviewReminder.day`, `settings.weeklyReviewReminder.test`.
- **Empty screens.** `EmptyStateView` has `hint` (label size, soft grey, identifier `empty.hint`, not shown on a warning page) and `actionIdentifier` (`empty.action` when none is given). `AppModel.capturePreset` holds the words **Quick capture** opens with; it is read once, then cleared. Button identifiers: `waiting.empty.start`, `inbox.empty.capture`, `actions.empty.capture`, `map.empty.new`, `calendar.empty.today`, `calendar.empty.startNote`, `templates.empty.new`; the others use `empty.action`.
- **The date window with At.** **Quick capture** uses `DateTimeChoiceView`; **Set** calls `CaptureReading.line(_:dated:at:)`, which changes a date mark `>YYYY-MM-DD` or `>YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM` that is already on the line, or adds one at the end. **Close the day › Day…** opens on the task's own time: the same time moves the task as before, a changed time sets date and time together. Still a date only: a project's deadline, a goal's target date, the day something ended, and the **Target** chip in **New**. Identifiers are the window's own: `date.field`, `date.time`, `date.time.09:00` and the other quick times, `date.time.clear`, `date.set`, `date.cancel`. Test: `CaptureReadingTests.testTheDateWindowWritesDateAndTimeAndReplacesAnOlderDate`.
- **Widget links.** On the scheme `amspara`, with no host: `amspara:///list/today`, `amspara:///list/overdue`, `amspara:///list/inbox`, `amspara:///list/review`. A line that opens a note keeps `amspara://<note title>`, unchanged since build 174. `WidgetList`, `WidgetSnapshot.link(toList:)`, `WidgetSnapshot.list(for:)` and `WidgetSnapshot.firstWaitingList` (order: due today, overdue, Inbox, for a look; all zero gives **Today**) are in `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/WidgetSnapshot.swift`. On the medium and large sizes each line is its own link; the small sizes have one link for the whole widget. `AppModel.handle(url:)` looks for a list link after the link to **Plan** and before the capture link: today and overdue open **Today**, inbox the **Inbox**, review the **Weekly review**. With no vault open, nothing happens. The file the widgets read is unchanged, so an old widget beside a new app still works.
- **iPhone: opening a section.** `openSection` chooses the tab that owns the section (**Today**, **Plan**, **Actions**, **Inbox**) and puts that tab back on its first screen. Every other section is pushed on **Browse**, as before. A request made before the tabs are on screen (the app started by a widget) is answered when they appear.
- **Find in a note.** Mac: **Edit** menu, **Find in Note** ⌘F, **Find Next** ⌘G, **Find Previous** ⇧⌘G, all off while no note is open; Apple's find bar on the note's text (`usesFindBar`, searching as you type); `NoteFind.run(.show/.next/.previous)`. iPhone: Apple's find bar on the text (`isFindInteractionEnabled`), without replace; menu item `note.find` in the **…** menu `note.more`. `AppModel.findInNoteRequests` carries ⌘F from the menu to the open note. The change from **Read** to **Edit** is the stored `editorMode`, so the note stays in **Edit**.
- **Go menu keys.** `SidebarGroups.numbered`: **Inbox**, **Today**, **Plan**, **All actions**, **Aspirations**, **Projects**, **Weekly review**, on ⌘1 to ⌘7.
- **The capture key.** `CaptureHotKey` (`off`, `controlOptionSpace`, `controlShiftSpace`, `controlOptionCommandSpace`), kept on each Mac under `captureHotKey`; missing or not known means off. Not in the vault. `App/Paragon/CaptureKey.swift`, Mac only: the key is claimed at launch only when it is not off, and again on every change in **Settings**. The panel floats, does not bring PARAGON to the front, is 380 points wide and centred, and closes 1.2 seconds after a save, on Escape, or on the key again. Identifiers: `settings.captureKey`, and `settings.captureKey.refused` for the line shown when macOS did not give the key.
- **Share.** Menu `note.share` with `note.share.pdf`, `note.share.text`, `note.share.file` and, on the Mac, `note.showInFinder`. `NoteShare.fileName(title:ext:)` turns `/`, `:` and `\` into `-`, and an empty name into "Note". `NoteShare.plainText(of:)` leaves out the settings block and the hidden task marks. The copy is written to the temporary folder `PARAGON share`, as `<Title>.pdf`, `<Title>.txt` or the note's own file name. What you typed and did not save yet is saved first. The PDF is A4 (595 × 842 points) with margins of 48 points, always light; the note's name is on top unless the note starts with it as a heading; a page break falls between blocks. **Show in Finder** selects the real file in the vault. Tests: `Round8CTests` (4), and the Mac screen test `testAnOpenNoteOffersShare`.

## Exact details (build 316)

- **After the designer's look (build 316).** Task row facts: 5 points between a mark and its words, 12 points between facts, 2 points between the two fact lines; whole facts wrap, only the project's name shortens; about 88 points with two fact lines, 68 with one. A task's date window on the iPhone: 730 points on all pages, Apple's segmented control **Date · Deadline · Repeat**, **Save** on all pages, **Remove deadline**, the colour of the task's note. Choices 32 points tall with 15-point words. **Every…**: number 1 to 999 with a stepper, unit menu (day, week, month, year), switch **Count from the day I finish it** (`RepeatEvery` in Core). The six quick repeat choices are in the task menu under **Repeat**, not in the window. The deadline's mark: a red pennant on a pole on a finish line (24-point grid, 1.9 stroke, round ends, no fill). Date chip under a field: 28 points tall, x 10 points in a 28 by 28 touch area, the screen's colour. Main buttons never pale: **Save** on the New sheet says "Give it a name first."; a date that cannot be read says "Write the date as 2031-12-01." Inbox keyboard: Return on the empty field, a drag of the list or a press on a line closes it. Calendar › Day: **Create the daily note** and **Open the daily note** open the note on the iPhone. Tests: `RoundNinePolishTests`.

The precise rules and names behind round 9 of the premium review, for reference. The words in `code letters` are names in the app's code, in its files and in its tests. You do not need them to use PARAGON.

**Task dates**

- **A date in plain words: the rule.** `PlainDate` in `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Markdown/PlainDate.swift`: `PlainDate.read(_:today:calendar:)` finds the first date in a line; `PlainDate.line(_:understoodOn:ignoring:)` gives the line as it is saved, with the words replaced by the mark `>YYYY-MM-DD` or `>YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM`. English words only, capitals or small letters. Weeks start on Monday. Only the first date in a line is read.
- **A date in plain words: what is read.** `today`, `tomorrow`, `day after tomorrow` (also with "the" in front). A weekday with `on`, `by`, `due` or `this` in front, or alone as the last words of the line: the next such day after today, never today itself. `next Friday`: the Friday of the week after this one (on Friday 2026-11-13 both "Friday" and "next Friday" give 2026-11-20; on a Monday "Friday" is this week's and "next Friday" is next week's). Short weekday names (mon, tue, tues, wed, thu, thur, thurs, fri, sat, sun) only with on, by, due, this or next in front, or with a time after. `in 3 days`, `in two weeks`, `in a month`, `in 2 years`: a number from 1 to 999, or a, an, one to twelve. `next week`: its Monday. `next month`: its first day. `13 Dec`, `13th December`, `Dec 13`, `December 13th, 2026`: without a year, the next time that day comes, and today counts. `2026-12-13` written without the `>`. The words "on", "by" and "due" in front of a date belong to it and leave the title with it.
- **A date in plain words: the time.** A time may follow the date: `14:00`, `14.00`, `9:30 am`, `2pm`, `2 pm`, `noon`, `at 14`, `at 14:00`.
- **A date in plain words: fixes after the review (build 316).** A bare "at 1" to "at 6" is 13:00 to 18:00. A dot time (`14.00`) counts only when no word follows. A time that does not exist (45.00, 25:00) keeps the day without a time and leaves the number in the text. Dates inside `@deadline(…)`, `@since(…)`, `@done(…)` and `@repeat(…)` are never read as the task's date.
- **Repeats and deadlines beside a build 315 device (build 316).** A task with one of the new repeats gets its `^id` in the same write, so build 315's id pass does not rewrite the line. A `@repeat(…)` value PARAGON cannot read is kept word for word (`TaskItem.unreadRepeat`) until you choose a repeat or **Does not repeat**. A `@deadline(…)` already in a reminder's title is kept there (refreshed to the task's deadline) and is never shown in the task title; PARAGON never adds the mark to a reminder title that has none. Tests: `Round9FixTests`.
- **A date in plain words: never guessed.** A line that already has a `>` date mark: the mark wins and no words are read. A weekday alone in the middle of a sentence ("Prepare Friday meeting"): a weekday alone counts only as the last words of the line; a tag, `!`, `~45m`, `@repeat(…)` and one full stop may follow it. A short weekday alone ("Enjoy the sun"). A time without a day ("Call at 14:00"). Words in quotation marks, in a link, in `[[a note link]]`, in a web address or in `code`. "tomorrow's" and "today's". A day that does not exist (31 Feb). Number dates such as 13/12.
- **A date in plain words: on screen.** In **Quick capture** the date chip is the one that was there before (`capture.dueChip`); it shows the time since build 316 and gets the **x** (`readChip.remove`) only while the date comes from words. Under **Capture something…** in the **Inbox** and under **Add a task…** in a note it is `PlainDateChip` (`plainDate.chip`), shown only while date words are in the field. The chip's words are `PlainDateChip.title`: "Due today", "Due tomorrow", "Was due yesterday", else "Due " and the weekday and date; a time is added after it. The **x** remembers the words taken away; they stay plain text only while those very words are in the field. With **A note** chosen in **Quick capture** nothing is read. **Date…** first writes words already read as their mark, so the picked date replaces them. Not read: the **+** in **Start the day**, the share sheet, the link `amspara://capture`, and lines typed in a note's text. The **ⓘ** lists show "tomorrow 14:00 — a date in words" and "next Friday — also: in 3 days, 13 Dec". Tests: `TaskDatesRound9Tests`.
- **Deadline: in the file.** `@deadline(YYYY-MM-DD)` on the task's line, for example `- [ ] Send the form >2026-11-17 @deadline(2026-11-20) ^t3fa2c1`. It is written after the date, `@repeat(…)` and `@since(…)`, and before `@done(…)` and the `^t` mark. A day only, no time of day. `TaskItem.deadline`, `TaskParser.deadlineRegex`; the editor dims it like the other marks.
- **Deadline: the words.** `TaskDeadline.words(until:from:)`: "N days left", "1 day left", "Due today", "1 day late", "N days late". `TaskDeadline.isPressing`: true on the deadline's day and after it; the words are then red. The flag is always red on an open task. A finished task: a grey flag and the date. The row view is `DeadlineLabel`, on task rows (`TaskRow`), on **Inbox** lines and on the Inbox's chosen line. A task with a deadline does not draw its date in red when the date has passed.
- **Deadline: setting it.** Task menu **Deadline**: **Pick a date…** (opens the date window on its **Deadline** page), **In a week** (today and 7 days), **Remove deadline** (only when there is one). The date window `TaskDateWindow` has 3 pages, `TaskWindowTab`: **Date**, **Deadline**, **Repeat**; identifiers `task.window.date`, `task.window.deadline`, `task.window.repeat`. It opens on **Date** unless the menu asked for another page. The **Deadline** page has no **At** line; **Set** writes, **No deadline** clears (shown only when there is one), **Cancel** changes nothing. Its grey line says: "The day it must be finished by. It stays where it is when you move the task to another day." `AppModel.setTaskDeadline(_:_:)`.
- **Deadline: with a repeat.** `TaskItem.nextOccurrence`: the next task keeps the number of days between date and deadline (never less than 0). A task with a deadline and no date gets the next deadline and still no date.
- **Deadline: Reminders and older builds.** The deadline is not sent to Apple Reminders, like the task's length `~45m`. The sync's comparison of the two sides leaves it out, so setting or clearing it starts no sync. `@deadline(2026-12-01)` typed into a reminder's title is read as a deadline when it comes back. Build 315 and older do not know the mark and leave it in the task's words, so it shows as text in the title and in the reminder's title; it is not lost. **Overdue** on **Today**, the **Weekly review** and the widgets go by the task's date.
- **Repeat: in the file.** One word in `@repeat(…)`. As before: `daily`, `weekly`, `monthly`, `yearly`, `2w`, `3m`, `10d`, `every 2 weeks`. New in build 316: `mon,wed,fri` (any of mon tue wed thu fri sat sun, in week order; full names are read too); `weekdays` for Monday to Friday (`workdays` is read too); all 7 days are written as `daily`; `monthend` (`month-end`, `eom` and `last day of month` are read too); and `10d after done`, `weekly after done`: any every-N rule followed by ` after done`.
- **Repeat: the next date.** `RepeatRule.next(after:)`. Chosen weekdays: the next chosen weekday after the task's date, and after the day it was ticked when that is later. Last day of the month: the last day of the task's month when its date is before it, else the last day of the next month. After done: counted from the day the task was ticked, whatever date it had. Chosen weekdays and the last day of the month cannot be joined with "after done". Ticking in Apple Reminders uses the same rule.
- **Repeat: the words on a row.** `RepeatRule.label`: "Every Mon, Wed, Fri", "Every working day", "Last day of the month", "10 days after it is done".
- **Repeat: on screen.** Task menu **Repeat**: the 6 quick choices, then **On weekdays…** (it shows the chosen days when there are some, like "Every Mon, Wed, Fri…"), **Last day of the month**, **Other…** (it shows the rule's own words when it is none of the 6, like "Every 10 days…"), and **Count from the day I finish it** for the every-N rules only. A quick choice keeps **Count from the day I finish it** as it was. The **Repeat** page is `RepeatChoiceView`: **Does not repeat**, **Every…**, **On weekdays**, **Last day of the month**; at least 1 weekday stays chosen; 300 points wide on the Mac. Identifiers: `repeat.kind.*`, `repeat.day.1` to `repeat.day.7`, `repeat.count`, `repeat.afterDone`, `repeat.save`, `repeat.cancel`.
- **Repeat: older builds.** The old values read and write exactly as before (tested). A build before 316 does not know `mon,wed,fri`, `weekdays`, `monthend` or `… after done`. When such a build writes the task's line again (a tick, a move), it leaves the repeat out. Use the new repeats only when both devices have build 316.

**Backups**

- **The restore line.** `RestoreResult.line(stamp:)` in `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/VaultBackup.swift`; `stamp` is the copy's day and time. "Restored N notes from STAMP". With other files (pictures, settings): "Restored 15 notes and 4 other files from STAMP". No notes: "Restored 3 files from STAMP". Some not written: "Restored 2 of 3 files from STAMP. 1 could not be written." Nothing: "Nothing was put back from STAMP. Your notes are as they were." The restore failed before anything was written: "The copy of STAMP was not restored. Nothing was changed." `RestoreResult.notes` counts the written files that end in `.md`. Kept on each device under `lastOutsideRestoreLine` and `lastOutsideRestoreVault` (the vault folder's path; the line is shown only for that vault). Identifiers: `settings.outsideBackup.restored`, `settings.outsideBackup.restoring`. Test: `RoundNineCTests.testTheRestoreLineNamesNotesAndTheCopysDay`.
- **Restoring….** `AppModel.isRestoring` is true from the yes in the restore sheet until the restore has ended, also when it failed. The sync ring's short word is "Restoring"; the sync line, the Mac menu bar item's first line and the button in **Settings › Reminders sync** say "Restoring…".
- **One folder per vault.** Inside `PARAGON Backups`, a sub-folder named `<vault folder name> <identity>` (`Vault.outsideSubfolderName(for:)`). A folder belongs to a vault when its name is the identity, or ends in a space and the identity, so a renamed vault keeps its folder. Only an identity shaped like PARAGON's own (36 letters and numbers, a UUID) gets a sub-folder. `VaultBackup.subfolder` is empty for a copy inside the vault and for a copy in the old place. Read (`Vault.outsideCopies(in:)`): every copy straight in `PARAGON Backups` (the old place) and the copies in this vault's own sub-folders, one per identity the vault has had. Another vault's sub-folder is never opened.
- **Offered for a restore.** As in build 315: copies marked as this vault's, now also with an older identity of it, and copies with no mark (shown with their top folders). A copy marked as another vault's is never offered.
- **Clearing out.** `Vault.pruneOutsideCopies(in:now:protecting:)` runs the tiers of build 307 over this vault's own marked copies only: copies whose `paragon-copy-of.json` can be read on this device and names one of this vault's identities, in the sub-folders and in the old place. Never cleared out: a copy with no mark, a copy of another vault, a copy whose mark is not on this device, and anything else in the folder. "Nothing changed since the last copy" compares with the newest copy that is surely this vault's. A vault whose identity cannot be read or written at that moment still gets its copy: in the old place, with no mark, and nothing is cleared out that time. A device on build 315 does not see the sub-folders, so it cannot clear them out; it still clears out the old place as before. Test: `RoundNineCTests.testTwoVaultsInOneFolderNeverThinOutEachOthersCopiesAndOldCopiesStayFindable`.
- **The older identity wins.** `VaultIDs` in `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/VaultIDs.swift`. The folder `.ams-para/vault-ids` holds one file per identity, named `<yyyyMMdd'T'HHmmss'Z'>~<identity>.txt`, in UTC. The name says everything, so a file still in iCloud counts, and a file iCloud renamed ("… 2.txt") counts too. `VaultIDs.winner(of:)`: the oldest day wins; equal days: the identity that sorts first; an identity noted twice counts with its oldest day. An identity from build 315 has no day and is noted with 2026-11-13 00:00 UTC, the same on every device. `.ams-para/vault-id.txt` stays one line, so build 315 can still read it; it is written again when it holds a younger identity. Identities are noted when an outside copy is made, also the ones in versions of the file that iCloud set aside. While `vault-id.txt` is still in iCloud, no identity is used or made. An identity typed by hand (not a UUID) is kept as it is, with no sub-folder and no file. Limit: an identity lost before build 316 cannot be found again; copies marked with it stay on disk but are not offered. Tests: `RoundNineCTests.testTheOlderIdentityWinsAndTiesGoToTheOneThatSortsFirst`, `testBothDevicesAdoptTheOlderIdentityAndCopiesMarkedWithTheOtherStillCount`, `testAnIdentityFromBuild315IsKeptAndNoted`.

**The weekly review**

- **No weekly review yet.** `WeeklyReviewDone.isNoneYetDue(last:firstUse:today:)`: no review day at all, and the first day of use is 7 or more days ago. With no first day known: never. The first day is kept on each device under `firstUseDay`, a day like 2026-11-13, noted at the first start of build 316: today, or the day of the oldest backup inside the vault when that is earlier. Not shown while notes are still coming from iCloud. The words are `WeeklyReviewDone.noneYetLine`, "No weekly review yet"; identifier `today.reviewNoneYet`. Test: `RoundNineCTests.testNoWeeklyReviewYetShowsAWeekAfterFirstUseAndNeverAfterAReview`.
- **Weekly review reminder.** Unchanged: **On** (the weekday) and **At** (the time), shown while **Remind me to do the weekly review** is on; Sunday 17:00 and off at first.
- **The steps.** On the iPhone the parts of the list use Apple's compact spacing. The tick of a finished step sits at the right end of the heading's line on every device. A heading takes up to 2 lines.

**Capture, focus and find**

- **Record my own key….** `RecordedKey` in `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Model/RecordedKey.swift`: the key's number, the keys held with it, and the key's name (the letter on your keyboard, or Space, Return, Tab, Delete, Esc, the arrows, F1 to F12). Kept on each Mac under `captureHotKeyOwn`; not in the vault. `CaptureHotKey.custom` is the new choice (`captureHotKey` holds "custom"); `CaptureHotKey.fixedChoices` is **Off** and the 3 fixed keys. The rule `RecordedKey.isAllowed`: ⌘ or ⌃ must be held; ⌥ alone, ⌥⇧ alone and Shift alone do not count. The every-app keys (the ⌘ letters Q, W, H, M, C, V, X, Z, A, S, N, O, P, F, T and comma, ⌘Tab, ⇧⌘Tab, ⌘Space, ⌘Return, ⌘Delete, ⌘Esc, ⇧⌘Z, ⌃C, ⌥⌘Esc) are refused, not warned about (`RecordedKey.refusalLine`). The line for a refused key: "Hold ⌘, ⌃ or ⌥ together with the key. A key by itself would stop you typing it in every app." A warning, and **Save** still works, for: ⌘Space, ⌥⌘Space, ⌃Space, ⌃⌥Space, ⌃⌘Space, ⌘Tab, ⇧⌘Tab, ⌥⌘Esc, ⌥⌘D, ⌃⌘Q, ⌃⌘F, ⇧⌘3, ⇧⌘4, ⇧⌘5, ⌃ with an arrow, ⇧⌘N (PARAGON's own quick capture), and ⌘ with Q, W, H, M, C, V, X, Z, A, S, N, O, P, F, T or comma. The window `RecordKeySheet` is 380 points wide. It reads key presses only while it is open and only inside PARAGON; no permission is needed. While it is open PARAGON lets go of the capture key in use, and takes the chosen one again when the window closes. **Save** with nothing recorded says "Press the keys first." Identifiers: `settings.captureKey.record`, `recordKey.sheet`, `recordKey.keys`, `recordKey.refused`, `recordKey.warning`, `recordKey.cancel`, `recordKey.save`. Test: `RoundNineCTests.testARecordedKeyNeedsAModifierAndWarnsAboutKeysMacOSOwns`.
- **Focus by swipe and on the Mac row.** `TaskRow` in `NoteEditorView.swift`. The swipe from the right has **Tomorrow** first and **Focus** second; a full swipe is **Tomorrow**. The Mac's timer button is shown while the pointer is over the row, only where the time button is not, keeps its place when not seen (the row does not jump), and is hidden while renaming. Both are offered under the same conditions as **Focus on this** in the menu: a timer bar can be seen there, the task is open, and it is not in a Work note (`AppModel.canFocus`). Identifiers: `task.swipeFocus.<title>`, `task.focus.<title>`. **Inbox** lines have neither.
- **Find in note.** iPad: a button in the wide toolbar over a note, after the pencil and before Archive, Share and Delete; identifier `note.find`, the same as the iPhone's menu item. A note in **Read** is turned to **Edit** first, as before. iPhone: while the find bar is up, the top bar has its solid background.

**Widgets**

- **One list.** `App/ParagonWidgets/OneListWidget.swift`, kind `ParagonOneList`, small size only. The choice **List** in **Edit Widget** is **Today**, **Overdue**, **Inbox** or **Review**; **Today** at first. It reads the same shared file as the other widgets; the numbers are due today, overdue, in the Inbox and due for a look. The link is `amspara:///list/today`, `/overdue`, `/inbox` or `/review`, the links of build 315. The words at zero: "Nothing due today", "Nothing overdue", "The Inbox is empty", "Nothing for a look". A zero is grey. The widgets of earlier builds are unchanged.

**Settings and help**

- **Dates in help headings.** `HelpDates.plain(_:)` in Core: "D Month YYYY" with an English month name becomes YYYY-MM-DD; "7-8 September 2026" becomes "2026-09-07 to 2026-09-08"; a heading with no date stays as it is. It is used for every `##` heading of **How it works**, **What's new** and **Words in PARAGON**. Only what is shown changes: the file `Docs/VersionHistory.md` still says "## Build 316 · 13 November 2026". On the iPhone and the iPad the 3 help pages use the small title in the bar.
- **If both changed.** On the iPhone the row in **Settings › Reminders sync** is **If both changed** with **Note wins** and **Reminder wins**. On the Mac and the iPad it is **When both sides changed** with **The note wins** and **The reminder wins**. One setting, `conflictPolicy`.
- **About.** **Version**, then the row **Details**, closed at first. Inside: **Device id**, with the id under it on 1 line in small fixed-width letters (it shrinks to fit, to 60 % at most, and can be copied), and the sentence about the `.ams-para` folder and the task marks `^t…`.
- **The vault's folder.** `VaultPlaceWords` in Core. The name is the last part of the path. The place is "in iCloud Drive" when the path holds "/Mobile Documents/"; "in Dropbox" when it holds "/Dropbox" (capitals or not); else "on this Mac", "on this iPhone" or "on this iPad". The row reads "Name · place". **Show in Finder** is on the Mac only, while a vault is open; it opens Finder with the vault folder chosen. The full path is the row's hover tip.
- **Mac menus.** **File › Search Everywhere** and **Go › Plan** have no dots. With dots, because they open a sheet or a window: **New…**, **Quick Capture…**, **Show me what would change…**, **Last sync report…**, **Sync settings…**.
- **The sync ring's tip (Mac).** "The sync with Apple Reminders. Press for Sync now and the other choices; ⇧⌘R syncs at once." The rest of the tip is unchanged.

**Colours by day**

- **Orange words.** `Theme.attention.ink`: #B35300 by day (5.1 to 1 on white, 4.6 to 1 on the page #F3F5F8), #FFA23A at night. Before: #FF9500, 2.2 to 1 on white. Small words in an orange capsule: #9F4B08 (`capsuleWords`, the day colour #D4640A a quarter deeper). Orange icons, the capsule's own fill and orange borders are as before. About 50 places.
- **The band by day.** `ShadeRule.bandDayStart`: the part's day colour, made 5 % deeper at a time until white words read 4.5 to 1. Three change: **Plan** #C05E0C to #B6590B, the blue of the line for now #4B7F9E to #477996, the morning orange #D4640A to #B65609. The other 10 keep their colour. `ShadeRule.bandDayEnd`: the start 18 % deeper, for example **Today** and **Calendar** #6B4EA8 to #58408A, **Inbox** #5E7D12 to #4D660F, **Projects** #2E7D5B to #26664B. White words read 4.7 to 8.0 to 1 at the start and 6.4 to 10.1 to 1 at the end (before: 2.9 to 3.5 to 1 at the far end). The small words on a band are full white (before, white at 85 %). The night band is unchanged.
- **Cards.** `ShadeRule.tintShare(night:phone:)`: 0.13 at night, 0.12 by day on the iPhone, 0.07 by day on the Mac and the iPad. iPhone cards by day: **Settings** and **Today** #EDEAF5 (1.09 to 1 against the page #F3F5F8; before #F5F3F9, 1.01 to 1), **Inbox** #ECEFE3, **Projects** #E6EFEB, **Areas** #F8E8EF, **Resources** #E6EEF6, **Goals** #F2EEE2. Soft grey words on the new card read 5.0 to 1. It holds for list cards and for the tinted panels, such as the facts panel of a note, on the iPhone.
- **The iPhone's fields.** **Add a task…**, **Search for a word** and **Capture something…**: fill #FFFFFF by day and #1E2228 at night; a line of 1 point, #E2E6EB by day and #2E333A at night; corners of 8 points; 10 points of room left and right and 7 above and below. The Mac and the iPad keep Apple's rounded field.

**Lists and screens**

- **Add in the Inbox.** Always full colour. Pressed with an empty field it puts the cursor in the field and adds nothing.
- **Save on the iPhone's sheets.** The filled button in the part's colour; when it cannot act, a pale fill with the word in the part's colour. iPhone and iPad.
- **Daily note on the iPhone.** No title in the top bar. The band says the date. The Mac and the iPad keep the title.
- **Bands and the line under them (iPhone).** **Inbox**: the line "… to sort" is not drawn. **Plan**: when the day shown is today, the line under the day's name does not count blocks; it counts events, and is not drawn with no events. On another day it is as before ("3 blocks · 1 event").
- **Plan.** The iPhone's band counts the blocks of today's plan, the ones the **Plan** tab draws. Messages at the foot of the iPhone sit 66 points up (16 before; 128 while a focus timer runs, as before). **Not planned yet** leaves out an action that has a block on any day of the week shown: a block's name is the action's plan name, capitals and extra spaces ignored (`NowAndNext.isPlanned`).
- **One note, one tab (iPhone).** Each tab remembers the note on top of its own stack. A tab that comes to the front opens the chosen note only when no other tab is showing it.
- **Lines of text.** **Projects** list, second line: up to 2 lines. **Not planned yet** tiles: the note's name gives way before the action's words. Snippets list: a preview of 3 lines. **Map** box titles: 2 lines. **Calendar** day heading: 1 line, shrinking to 75 %.
- **Map.** The actions under a box share one line, to the first of them. No empty slot between **Arrange** and **Export** on the iPhone. A map smaller than its page starts at the top, centred left to right.
- **Mac Projects.** A group heading of 1 line: 4 points above and below. A name on 2 lines: 8 points.
- **Mac Search.** The area of the tick boxes is as tall as the boxes. While Search is open the sidebar shows a **Search** row under **Look back**, as the chosen row.
- **Small things.** **New** sheet tiles: 5 points left and right of the word; the word shrinks to 65 % at most. Fold arrows: 16 points in **Projects**, 14 points in **Aspirations** and the **Goals** list.
- **The build number.** `BuildStamp.number` is 316; **Settings › About** says 1.0 (316).

## Exact details (build 317)

- **After the designer's look (build 317).** `Marks.byName` has `play.fill`, `pause.fill` and `stop.fill` (the stop square `M7 7h10v10h-10Z`), 16 points in a 28-point round button on the focus bar. **Today's actions**, medium: `WidgetRows.plan(size:count:)` — 1 or 2 tasks with their second row, 3 or 4 tasks with one row each, "and N more" at the right end of the foot; small (3 lines) and large (7 lines, two rows each) as before. The medium and large heading has Today's sun, 15 points. Lock Screen wide shape: `LockGlance.heading` says "3 due, 1 overdue" when both numbers are there; the two task lines are one text step smaller. **Settings › Siri and Shortcuts**: the names are **Add to Inbox**, **Due Today**, **Open Today**, **Start the Day**, **Close the Day**, **Start Focus**; on the iPhone the phrase stands under the action. Tests: `WidgetTickTests`.

The precise rules and names behind build 317, for reference. The words in `code letters` are names in the app's code, in its files and in its tests. You do not need them to use PARAGON.

**Siri and Shortcuts**

- **Where the code is.** `App/Paragon/Intents/`: `ParagonIntents.swift` (the 6 actions), `ParagonShortcuts.swift` (the phrases and tiles), `FocusTaskEntity.swift` (the task list of **Start Focus**), `ShortcutBridge.swift` (how an action reaches the app). What an action says is decided in `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/ShortcutAnswers.swift`.
- **One model, one writer.** `AppModel.shared` is the one model for the windows and for the actions. An action runs inside the app; the system starts the app in the background when it is not running. Nothing in `App/Paragon/Intents/` opens a file.
- **The actions.** `AddToInboxIntent` ("Add to Inbox"), `DueTodayIntent` ("What Is Due Today?", tile "Due Today"), `OpenTodayIntent` ("Open Today"), `StartTheDayIntent` ("Start the Day"), `CloseTheDayIntent` ("Close the Day"), `StartFocusIntent` ("Start Focus"). The 3 that open PARAGON have `openAppWhenRun` true; the other 3 have it false.
- **The phrases.** 5 for Add to Inbox, 4 for What is due today, 3 each for Open Today, Start the day and Close the day, 4 for Start focus: 22 in all, as listed in **Siri and Shortcuts**. Each holds the app's name.
- **The tiles.** `shortcutTileColor` is purple. Apple's symbols: `tray.and.arrow.down`, `checklist`, `sun.max`, `cup.and.saucer.fill`, `moon.fill`, `timer`.
- **Add to Inbox: fields.** **Text** (needed; Siri asks "What do you want to add?") and **Day** (a date, may be empty).
- **Add to Inbox: the line.** `ShortcutCapture.item(text:day:)`: line breaks become spaces, spaces are collapsed. Empty words: nothing is written and Siri asks again. **Day** adds `>YYYY-MM-DD` only when the words carry no date; a typed date wins. The target is always the Inbox, as a task (`- [ ] …`).
- **Add to Inbox: the writing.** `AppModel.captureFromShortcut` calls `AppModel.capture`, the path of **Quick capture**: unsaved typing in the open note is written first, the line is added, the Inbox is saved. It counts in "caught today".
- **Add to Inbox: kept for later.** With no vault open, or the Inbox still coming from iCloud, or a failed write, the words go into `capture-outbox.jsonl` in the App Group folder (the file the share extension uses) and are filed the next time PARAGON sees the vault.
- **Add to Inbox: the answers.** `ShortcutCapture.answer(for:filed:)`: "Added to your Inbox." · "Added to your Inbox, for 2026-11-14." · "Added to your Inbox, for 2026-11-14 14:00." · kept: "Saved. It goes into your Inbox the next time PARAGON opens your notes." · kept with a date: "Saved, for 2026-11-14. It goes into your Inbox the next time PARAGON opens your notes."
- **Add to Inbox: neither written nor kept.** When the line is not in the Inbox and not in the outbox, the action throws `ShortcutProblem.notSaved`: "PARAGON could not save this, so it was not added. Nothing was lost from your notes. Please try again." It never says "Added" then.
- **What is due today: the tasks.** `DueTodayAnswer`: open tasks dated today (`due`) and the number dated before today (`overdueCount`), from `index.openTasks(dueOnOrBefore:)`, in the order of **Today**. Before answering, unsaved typing is written and the notes are read again (only changed notes), unless a sync is running.
- **What is due today: the sentence.** `DueTodayAnswer.spoken`. At most 3 names are said (`spokenLimit`); the rest is "and N more". A time is said as "at 14:00". `#tags` are left out (`spokenTitle`). Overdue: "One more is overdue." / "N more are overdue."; with nothing due today "One thing is overdue." / "N things are overdue.". Notes still coming: "One note is still coming from iCloud, so this may not be everything." / "N notes are still coming from iCloud, so this may not be everything."
- **What is due today: the card.** `DueTodaySnippet`: at most 5 tasks (`shownLimit`); the grey line is `footnote`, "and N more · N overdue". The action returns the task names as a list of text.
- **Open Today, Start the Day, Close the Day.** With no vault: `ShortcutProblem.noVault`, "PARAGON has no notes folder open. Open PARAGON and choose your folder first." The day's screens go through `AppModel.openDayStep`: when another sheet, the stop question or the "what next" question is open, the screen waits (`openWhenFree`). On the Mac `showMainWindowForShortcut` brings the main window to the front.
- **Start Focus: the list.** `AppModel.focusSuggestions`: tasks due today or earlier in Today's order, then each project's next action, at most 30. `focusMatches`: open tasks whose words hold the text, at most 30. Never a task in a work note.
- **Start Focus: the pick.** `FocusPick.pick(plan:dueToday:)`: the block running now, else the first task due today, else the next block, else nothing ("Nothing is planned or due today. Choose a task to focus on.").
- **Start Focus: the answers.** "Focus started on X, N minutes planned." (minutes only when planned) · "The clock is already running on X." · `taskGone`: "That task is no longer open in PARAGON." · `busy`: "PARAGON is asking about the clock you stopped. Answer that first." · when the app refuses to start a clock, its own words are said (`cannotFocus`).
- **Start Focus and the Lock Screen.** On iOS `StartFocusIntent` is a `LiveActivityIntent`, so the timer shows on the Lock Screen at once, also when PARAGON is in the background.
- **Settings.** `SiriShortcutsSection` in `App/Paragon/SystemSearch/SystemSearchViews.swift`, heading "Siri and Shortcuts", 6 rows, the button with the identifier `settings.shortcuts.open` (iPhone and iPad: Apple's `ShortcutsLink`; Mac: **Open Shortcuts**, which opens `shortcuts://`).

**System search**

- **Where the code is.** `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/SystemSearch.swift` (the rules), `App/Paragon/SystemSearch/SystemSearchIndexer.swift`, `SystemSearchThumbnails.swift`, `SystemSearchViews.swift`.
- **What is in it.** `SystemSearchRule.entries(for:)`: every note of the vault, also archived ones. Left out: notes in the Work folder, conflict copies, deleted notes, templates, snippets.
- **A result.** Title: the name the app shows. Part word: Project, Area, Resource, Goal, Aspiration, Daily note, Weekly note, Inbox, Archive. A goal with no end is an Aspiration. The line under the title is the part word, " · ", and the first words.
- **The first words.** The note's lines without headings, empty lines, picture lines, code fences, rules and comments; without task boxes and hidden task marks (`^t…`); `[[Health]]` reads "Health", `[the bank](https://…)` reads "the bank"; `**`, `__`, `==` and backticks are dropped. Lines are joined with " · ". At most 300 characters (`snippetLimit`), cut at a whole word, with "…".
- **Found by.** Title, part word, first words, and tags. The keywords are the tags, the part word and "PARAGON".
- **The tiles.** One picture per part, the day colour, the same by day and by night.
- **Kept current.** 2 seconds after the last change to the notes, the notes whose result would look different are handed over and the notes that are gone are taken out. A rename is "old one out, new one in".
- **Built from nothing.** When the build number changes, another vault is opened, the switch is turned on, **Build the list again** is pressed, or the memory file is gone.
- **Still coming from iCloud.** While any note is still coming, nothing is taken out.
- **Switch off or vault closed.** PARAGON's part of the system search is emptied. A build that is running stops, and what it handed over in that moment is taken out again.
- **The memory file.** `paragon-system-search.json` in the app's Caches folder: which build and vault the list was made for, and one short mark per note. Indexing uses the notes the app holds in memory; it reads and writes no file in the vault.
- **Names.** Setting `showNotesInSystemSearch` (on when never set). Apple's group name `com.schabbauer.amspara.notes`. A result's identity is `note:` and the note's path in the vault. Results never expire.
- **A result that is gone.** "PARAGON could not find that note. It may have been moved or deleted."
- **Test runs.** The test vault is never put into the system search of the machine, unless `PARAGON_SYSTEM_SEARCH_TEST` is 1.
- **Settings.** `SystemSearchSection`, heading "System search": the switch `settings.systemSearch.switch`, the row **Notes in it** `settings.systemSearch.count` ("None", "No vault open", "Building…", "Not built yet", "1204", "1204, updating…"), the button **Build the list again** `settings.systemSearch.rebuild`. The grey line on the Mac: "With this on, Spotlight (⌘Space) on this Mac finds your notes by title, first words and tags, and a press on a result opens the note in PARAGON. The list stays on this device. Work notes are never in it." On the iPhone and the iPad the place is "the search on this device (swipe down on the Home Screen)".

**A tick on the widget**

- **Where the code is.** `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/WidgetTicks.swift` (`WidgetTick`, `WidgetTickQueue`), `App/ParagonWidgets/WidgetTickIntent.swift` (`TickWidgetTaskIntent`, `ActionLine`), `App/Paragon/WidgetTickApply.swift` (`AppModel.applyWidgetTicks`). Tests: `WidgetTickTests`.
- **The file.** `widget-ticks.jsonl` in the App Group folder (`group.com.schabbauer.amspara`; on the Mac `D24ENP83QQ.group.com.schabbauer.amspara`), beside `widget-snapshot.json`. One line per press, added at the end, never rewritten in place.
- **A line.** `key` (the widget line: note path, `#`, line number), `notePath`, `taskID` (the task's `^t` id, may be missing), `title` (the task's words exactly as in the note), `due` (may be missing), `at` (the time), `ticked` (true; false means the tick was taken back).
- **The last press counts.** A tick and then a take-back on the same line leave nothing waiting.
- **When the app writes.** `applyWidgetTicks` runs at launch, when PARAGON comes to the front, and in the 10-second look while it is open. It first writes what is being typed.
- **Finding the task.** By its `^t` id among the open tasks of that note. Else by exact words and date, when exactly 1 open task matches. A task with that id that is already done: dropped. 2 tasks that cannot be told apart: dropped.
- **Writing the tick.** Through `AppModel.toggle`, the path of every tick in the app. If the note changed on disk at that moment, the app reads it again and tries 1 more time; if that fails too, the tick is dropped.
- **Waiting.** The tick goes back into the file when its note is missing while notes are still coming from iCloud, or when the note is here as an older copy while iCloud holds a newer one (`notesBeingUpdated`).
- **The message.** "Ticked on the widget: X" for 1, "N tasks ticked on the widget." for more. Only for ticks that are really in the note. Then the widget summary is written at once.
- **The summary file.** Each item of `widget-snapshot.json` has 4 new fields that may be missing: `notePath`, `taskID`, `rawTitle`, `due`. A line without `notePath` (written by an older build) shows no tick circle.
- **The widget.** Tick circles on the medium and large **Today's actions** only. A waiting tick is drawn ticked, grey and struck through, and is left out of the Lock Screen widget's number and lines.
- **The Mac test copy.** It has no App Group, so the tick circle does nothing there.

**The Lock Screen widget Due today**

- **Where the code is.** `App/ParagonWidgets/LockScreenWidgets.swift`; the words are `LockGlance` in `WidgetTicks.swift`.
- **Names.** Widget kind `ParagonLock`, name "Due today", description "How much is due today, and your next two actions." 3 shapes: round, wide, and the line above the clock. iPhone and iPad only.
- **The words.** "N due today" · "N due today, M overdue" · "M overdue" · "Nothing due today". The round number is due today plus overdue; at zero a drawn tick.
- **No file, or a file from another day.** "Open PARAGON"; the round shape shows the sun mark.
- **The wide shape.** The first 2 lines of **Today's actions**; with none, "Nothing due, nothing waiting".
- **A tap** opens **Today**.

**Today's plan on the iPhone**

- The iPhone's and iPad's widget list now holds: **Today's actions**, **What is waiting**, **One list**, **Today's plan**, **Due today** (Lock Screen), and the focus timer's Lock Screen display. The Mac's list is unchanged.
- `AppModel.hasPlanWidget` is true on every device. The iPhone's `widget-snapshot.json` now holds `plan`, and `events` when Calendar access is given and events are shown. It never asks for Calendar access.
- On a day with no blocks the iPhone says **Tap to plan the day**; the Mac says **Click to plan the day**.
- Tinted and clear widgets: the block's name and the plan blocks are marked `widgetAccentable`.
- The line **Today's plan** in **Settings › Widget** is still on the Mac only.

**The focus timer on the Lock Screen and in the Dynamic Island**

- **Where the code is.** `App/Shared/FocusActivityShared.swift` (in the app and in the widgets), `App/Paragon/FocusLiveActivity.swift`, `App/ParagonWidgets/FocusLiveActivityWidget.swift`, `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/FocusGlance.swift`.
- **It never counts.** It holds dates (`FocusGlance`: `isPaused`, `awaitingAnswer`, `workedSeconds`, `countsFrom`, `plannedSeconds`) and the system draws the clock and the bar. The app sends an update only on pause, go on and stop. No push messages are used.
- **The small words.** "of 45 min" · "of 45 min · paused" · "paused" · "stopped". Under a stopped clock: "Open PARAGON to save the time".
- **No end of its own.** The clock counts on past the planned time. It ends only when the timer ends.
- **One rule.** `FocusLiveActivity.sync` makes the Lock Screen display match `AppModel.focus`. It runs on every change of the timer, at launch, when PARAGON comes to the front, and when the stop question opens or closes. Every display that is not the timer now running is ended at once. So none is left behind and there are never 2.
- **Starting.** A display can be started while PARAGON is in front, or by **Start Focus**. If it was swiped away, it comes back the next time PARAGON comes to the front while the timer runs.
- **The buttons.** `PauseFocusIntent` ("Pause the focus timer"), `ResumeFocusIntent` ("Go on with the focus timer"), `StopFocusIntent` ("Stop the focus timer"; it opens PARAGON). They call `pauseFocus`, `resumeFocus`, `stopFocus` in the app. They are not shown in the **Shortcuts** app. They also work when the iPhone had closed PARAGON completely.
- **Switched off.** With Live Activities off for PARAGON in the iPhone's Settings, nothing is shown and nothing is asked.
- **Settings of the app.** `NSSupportsLiveActivities` is true in the app's Info.plist.
- **Test runs.** Nothing is put on the Lock Screen under `-paragon-test-vault`, unless `-paragon-live-activity` is also given.
- **The look.** White words on black at 62 %; the plan's orange for the dot, the bar and the main button; hand-drawn play and pause marks; the stop square is drawn in the same line.

**The build number.** `BuildStamp.number` is 317; **Settings › About** says 1.0 (317).

## Exact details (build 318)

- **After the designer's look (build 318).** `GoalProgressBar` is one drawn bar everywhere and fills in 0.35 seconds; per cent is written "65 %". On the iPhone the number words stand on their own line above the bar and are never cut; **Update…** is 32 points high. The number window's hint: "The target may be lower than the start."; its button mark is `chart.line`. A PDF row in a note being edited is 52 points high. Identifiers: `goal.number.line`, `goal.number.update`.

The precise rules and names behind build 318, for reference. The words in `code letters` are names in the app's code, in its files and in its tests. You do not need them to use PARAGON.

**Sharing pictures and PDFs in**

- **Where the code is.** `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/SharedFiles.swift` (names, folders, link lines, filing), `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/Capture.swift` (the outbox), `App/ParagonShare/ShareViewController.swift` (the share sheet, iPhone, iPad and Mac), `App/Paragon/Views/Attachments.swift` (how a picture or a PDF is shown).
- **Kinds kept.** Pictures `png`, `jpg`, `jpeg`, `heic`, `gif`, and `pdf` (`SharedFiles.accepts`). Another picture kind is kept as a JPEG: longest side 3000 pixels, quality 0.9, made 1 at a time (`jpegLock`). Any other file is left out, with the red line "“name” was left out: PARAGON keeps pictures and PDFs."
- **Limit.** 20 files in 1 share (`SharedFiles.maxItems`). More are left out, with the line "PARAGON takes 20 files at a time. N more were left out."
- **Share sheet: when PARAGON is offered.** In `App/Config/ParagonShare/Info.plist`: `NSExtensionActivationSupportsImageWithMaxCount` 20 and `NSExtensionActivationSupportsFileWithMaxCount` 20; text, web address and web page as before.
- **Share sheet: on screen.** Field **Text**, or **A few words (optional)** when files are shared (`share.words`). **Save to**: **Inbox** (the default) or **Today's note** (`share.target`). Each file row (`share.file`): a small picture, the name, "Picture · size" or "PDF · size". The switch **As note, not task** is hidden with files. **Cancel** and **Save**; on the Mac Escape and Return.
- **Folders.** Pictures into `Pictures/`. PDFs into `Attachments/` (`SharedFiles.attachmentsFolder`), new in build 318, made when needed.
- **File names.** `SharedFiles.safeName`: the last part of the name the other app gives. Taken out: `/ \ : * ? " < > | [ ] ( ) # ^ %`, control characters and line breaks. No dot and no space at either end. At most 80 characters before the ending. The ending in small letters. A file with no usable name, or with only a random identifier as name, is called `Shared 2026-11-13 101500.png` (date and time of the share).
- **Never over an existing file.** `SharedFiles.freeName`: a taken name gets ` 2`, ` 3` and so on (`IMG_0412 2.heic`). Capital and small letters count as the same name. A file that iCloud has not sent yet (`.Name.icloud`) counts as taken (`SharedFiles.takenNames`). The copy itself refuses to write over a file and then tries the next name, 5 tries.
- **The line in the note.** `SharedFiles.link(toFile:fromNote:words:)`. Picture: `- ![Picture](Pictures/IMG_0412.heic)`; with words `- ![Receipt Clas Ohlson](Pictures/IMG_0412.heic)`. PDF: `- [Invoice.pdf](Attachments/Invoice.pdf)`; with words `- [Pay before Friday](Attachments/Invoice.pdf)`. The path is as seen from the note; in a note inside a folder it starts with `../`. A space is written `%20`.
- **The words.** On the first file only. `[`, `]` and line breaks are taken out of them. When a web link was shared with the files, the words and the link are a line of their own first, and the file lines carry no words.
- **Where the lines go.** Under the note's `## Notes` heading, made when missing. A share with files is always note lines, never a task.
- **How a share travels.** The share sheet never writes into the vault. It copies each file, by file and never through memory, into the App Group folder: `capture-files/<item id>/<n>/<name>` (`SharedFiles.stagingFolder`, `SharedFiles.stage`). It adds 1 line to `capture-outbox.jsonl` with the new, optional field `files`. Old lines without `files` still read.
- **Filing.** `Vault.fileShared(_:into:filesRoot:)`, when PARAGON comes to the front and at start: copy into the vault, write the lines, save the note, and only then remove the waiting copies. If the note cannot be saved, the copies just made are removed and the item stays whole in the outbox.
- **iCloud is slow.** If the Inbox or today's note is still coming from iCloud, nothing is copied and the item keeps waiting. Message: "N filed, M kept for later".
- **Nothing lost when the app stops.** `CaptureOutbox.drain(filing:)`: the taken lines stay in `capture-outbox.<id>.jsonl` beside the outbox. After each item that file is written again without it; it is removed when empty. A file of that name left by a stopped run is read first by the next run. At worst the 1 item being filed at the moment of the stop is filed twice. `AppModel.drainOutbox` uses it for each outbox.
- **Never filed twice.** The waiting folder `capture-files/<item id>` is made before the outbox line and removed only after the note is saved. An item with `files` whose folder is gone was filed already and is skipped. **Save** in the sheet writes 1 line only (`ShareModel.saved`); **Cancel** after a **Save** removes nothing.
- **Clearing up.** **Cancel** removes that share's copies. `SharedFiles.sweep`: copies that no outbox line names are removed when they are 1 day old (`SharedFiles.orphanAge`, 24 hours). The files of every item kept in this run are never cleared.
- **Shared folder missing.** "PARAGON’s shared folder is not available. Open PARAGON once and try again." **Save** is off.
- **Inbox rows.** `InboxNoteWords`: a picture as a small square, a PDF with first page, name and page count. No **Make task** (`inbox.note.makeTask`) on such a row. The bin removes the line; the file stays.

**Share on the Mac**

- **One target, not two.** `ParagonShare` builds for iPhone, iPad and Mac (`project.yml`: `supportedDestinations: [iOS, macOS]`). Same bundle identifier `com.schabbauer.AMSPara.Share`, same `Info.plist`. `Tools/ship.sh` is unchanged.
- **Entitlements.** `App/Config/ParagonShare/ParagonShare-macOS.entitlements`: app sandbox, App Group `D24ENP83QQ.group.com.schabbauer.amspara`, `files.user-selected.read-only`. `ParagonShare-macOS-Debug.entitlements` has no App Group, so a Debug build on the Mac says the shared folder is not available. Only the TestFlight build can share on the Mac.
- **Two outboxes on the Mac.** `AppModel.outboxURLs`: the old one in Application Support, and `capture-outbox.jsonl` in the App Group folder. Both are emptied the same way.

**Pictures and PDFs in a note**

- **Read.** `MarkdownPreview`: a line that is 1 picture or 1 PDF of the vault (`SharedFiles.attachment(inLine:notePath:)`) is drawn by `AttachmentBlock`. Picture: at most 420 × 260 points, caption under it (`attachment.picture`). PDF: a row with the first page, the name and the pages (`attachment.pdf`).
- **Edit.** `SharedFiles.pdfMatches`: `[words](Attachments/x.pdf)` and `![…](x.pdf)` are drawn as a row, 52 points high, 200 to 340 points wide. Pictures in Edit are unchanged.
- **Row words.** Title: the link's words, or the file name without `.pdf`. Second line: "PDF · 1 page", "PDF · N pages", or "PDF" when the count cannot be read (`SharedFiles.pagesWords`); then " · file name" when the title is your own words.
- **Not here.** "On its way from iCloud" (the download is asked for); a press says "“name” is still on its way from iCloud. Try again in a moment." Missing: "This file is not in the vault"; a press says "“name” is not in the vault any more."
- **Quick Look.** It opens a copy in the temporary folder `AttachmentPreview`, emptied at each open.
- **Mac drop and paste.** A PDF file dropped on a note or pasted as a file is copied into `Attachments/` under its safe, free name; the link is written at that place. Dropped pictures use `SharedFiles.safeName` too.
- **Not built.** Printing and **As PDF** show a picture or PDF line as words and file name. A PDF row has no picture menu.
- **Backups.** `VaultBackup.pictureExtensions` gained `pdf`: the outside copy holds PDFs, and reuses an unchanged PDF from the last copy. The backups inside the vault hold notes only.

**A goal with a number**

- **Where the code is.** `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Model/GoalNumber.swift`, `App/Paragon/Views/GoalNumberViews.swift`.
- **Keys.** `number_start`, `number_target`, `number_current`, `number_unit`, `number_history`, in the goal note's settings block. All optional.
- **When a goal has a number.** When `number_target` reads as a number. Missing start: the first history value, else 0. Missing current: the newest history value, else the start.
- **Per cent.** (current − start) ÷ (target − start), for targets that go up and that go down. Below 0: shown as 0 %. Above 100: shown as it is (113 %); the bar stays full. Never 100 % before the target is met (99 %). Never 0 % once it moved the right way (1 %). Start = target: no per cent, unless current = target (100 %). Held at 1 000 000 000 % at most.
- **Reached.** Current ≥ target for a target that goes up; current ≤ target for one that goes down. The mark says **Reached**.
- **History.** 1 settings line, a list of `YYYY-MM-DD value`, oldest first, 1 value for each day (the same day replaces). At most 120 days (`GoalNumber.historyLimit`); over that the oldest go and the very first entry stays. Items that do not read as "date value" are left out of the curve. A history written by another editor as a list over several lines reads the same; PARAGON writes it back as 1 line.
- **Set a number….** Writes all lines and the first history entry (today's date, the value of **Now**). Empty **Start** = 0; empty **Now** = the start. **Save** is off while **Target** is empty or a field is not a number.
- **Edit number….** Keeps the history. A changed **Now** is added with today's date. Changing only start, target or unit adds nothing to the history. The fields are filled by `GoalNumber.typed(_:locale:)`: no sign between thousands, the device's decimal sign ("100000", "86,5").
- **Update….** `GoalNumber.updating(_:on:day:)`: the value with today's date; the same value on the same day writes nothing.
- **Remove.** Takes out the 5 lines and nothing else. Question: "Remove the number from this goal?"; button **Remove the number and its history**.
- **Numbers in the file.** A dot for decimals, no spaces, at most 2 decimals, no zeros at the end (`86.5`, `100000`). No dot at the end of a very long number.
- **Numbers on screen.** The device's own format ("100 000", "86,5" in Sweden).
- **Numbers typed.** `GoalNumber.parse`: comma or dot, spaces or `'` between thousands, a minus sign. "1.250,75" and "1,250.75" both read as 1250.75. On the iPhone the number pad; the keyboard with a minus sign when a value is below 0.
- **Unit.** 1 line, at most 16 characters. An empty unit removes the `number_unit` line.
- **Saving.** An edit still waiting in the note editor is saved first; then the number is written to the note as it is on file.
- **Older builds.** Build 317 and before do not know the 5 keys and keep them, unchanged and in place, when they rewrite the note (test `testAnOlderBuildKeepsTheNumberLinesWhenItRewritesTheNote`).
- **The curve.** `GoalNumber.curve`: 1 point for each update, spaced evenly, not by calendar distance. The start is drawn first when the history does not begin there. A `number_current` changed by hand is drawn as the last point. Fewer than 2 points: no curve.
- **Where it shows.** Goal header: `GoalNumberChip` (**Set a number…** / **Edit number…**). Goal page: `GoalNumberPanel` (bar 160 points wide, words, per cent, **Reached**, **Update…**, curve, the line under it). **Goals** list row: the bar only, 72 points wide, and the value. **Weekly review**, goals step: `GoalNumberLine`. Not on the **Map**, not in a chain, not for an aspiration.
- **Identifiers.** `goal.number` (header button), `goal.number.panel`, `goal.number.line`, `goal.number.update`, `number.field`, `number.start`, `number.target`, `number.now`, `number.unit`, `number.save`, `number.cancel`, `number.remove`.
- **Not built.** No "on course" or "behind" against the target date.

**Movement**

- **Seconds.** `MotionTiming` in `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Model/MotionTiming.swift`: `tick` 0.20, `settle` 0.25, `fold` 0.20, `arrive` 0.25, `page` 0.20, `toast` 0.20, `fill` 0.35, `press` 0.15, `fade` 0.15. Limits: `shortest` 0.15, `longest` 0.35. `spin` 1.0 is one turn of the sync ring, the one number outside the limits.
- **Names.** `Motion` in `App/Paragon/Views/Motion.swift`. `tick`, `fold`, `arrive`, `page`, `fill`, `scroll`: ease-out. `settle`: a spring without bounce. `press`: snappy. `spin`: linear, repeating. `toast` and `fade`: ease-out, and kept with Reduce Motion.
- **Transitions.** `Motion.rise` (up from the foot, with a fade) and `Motion.grow` (from 60 % to 100 % size, with a fade). Both are a plain fade with Reduce Motion.
- **Reduce Motion.** Read in 1 place, `Motion.isReduced` (`UIAccessibility.isReduceMotionEnabled` on the iPhone and the iPad, `NSWorkspace.accessibilityDisplayShouldReduceMotion` on the Mac). Every moving name is then nothing, so the screen changes at once.
- **The tick.** `TickMark` in `Motion.swift`: the open ring and the filled circle lie over each other and fade across; the filled one grows from 60 % to 100 %. Same 15-point drawing, same 22-point press area, same identifier `task.tick.<title>`.
- **Rows.** `AppModel.saveSettling(_:)` and `reloadSettling()` put the ordinary save and reload inside `Motion.settle`. Used by `toggle`, `setDueDate`, `setDue`, `makeNextAction`, `clearNextAction`, `addTask`, `addSubtask`, `deleteTask`, `moveTasks`, `moveTask`, `capture`. The write comes first and is not delayed.
