- **A second press.** `Note.holdsDone(_:)`: by the task's `^t` identifier, else by line and title. `AppModel.toggle` asks it before ticking, so a row that is fading away cannot be ticked again. Un-ticking is unchanged.
- **Folds.** `FoldButton` puts its change inside `Motion.fold`. A fold remembered in the settings (Today's groups, the Inbox's groups, an area's projects, **Last 12 weeks**, the Weekly review's steps, Search's boxes, **Filter**, the ended goals, the month grid, **Pictures**, **Hide finished**) also carries `.animation(Motion.fold, value:)` on its list.
- **Not animated.** The Mac sidebar, the groups of the **Projects** list, **Templates**, the **Note** button's editor, text while typing, the line through a ticked task.
- **The test of the rule.** `MotionRulesTests`: every duration in `MotionTiming.bounded` is within 0.15 to 0.35 seconds and `fold` is 0.2; and in `App/**/*.swift` every line with `withAnimation` or `.animation(` names `Motion.`, and no file but `Motion.swift` names a curve.

**Tests.** `SharedFilesTests` (5), `GoalNumberTests` (7), `MotionRulesTests` (2), and `testATickedTaskIsKnownFromAStaleCopy` in `NoteTests.swift`.

**The build number.** `BuildStamp.number` is 318; **Settings › About** says 1.0 (318).

## Exact details (build 319)

The precise rules and names behind build 319, **PARAGON's own iCloud folder**, for reference. Build 320 changed the words from "move" to "copy": the words in quotes and in bold below are the new ones. Where this chapter still says "a move", it means the copy; the code and some file names still use that word. The words in `code letters` are names in the app's code, in its files and in its tests. You do not need them to use PARAGON.

**The place**

- **Where the code is.** `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/VaultMove.swift` (the rules of a move), `Core/Sources/ParagonCore/Vault/VaultDevices.swift` (the list of devices), `App/Paragon/OwnFolder.swift` (finding the folder, switching), `App/Paragon/Views/OwnFolderViews.swift` (the pages, the bar, the rows in Settings).
- **The folder.** The iCloud container `iCloud.com.schabbauer.AMSPara` (`VaultMove.containerID`). The vault is its `Documents` folder. Files and Finder show it as **PARAGON** in iCloud Drive; PARAGON writes "iCloud Drive › PARAGON" (`VaultMove.ownFolderWords`). It is always looked for off the main thread.
- **Reachable.** PARAGON's folder counts as reachable when the device is signed in to iCloud and iCloud gives the app its container. While it is not, **Settings › Vault** does not show **Copy to PARAGON's own folder…**.
- **Kept on each device** (not in the vault): `vaultInOwnFolder` (this device opens PARAGON's folder), `lastVaultInOwnFolder`, `previousVaultBookmark` and `previousVaultName` (the folder before the copy), `oldFolderChangesSeen` (the list you answered **Not now** to).
- **First build.** `VaultMove.firstBuild` is 319.

**The files a move writes**

- In the folder the notes go to: `.ams-para/move/arriving.json` (the mark "being filled"), `.ams-para/move/complete.json` (the mark "complete"), `.ams-para/move/manifest.json` (the list of every file with its size and fingerprint), and later `.ams-para/move/seen-<device id>.json` (what that device last saw in the old folder).
- In the folder the notes leave: `PARAGON has moved these notes.txt`, at the top of the folder (the mark "moved"). It holds plain sentences and then the same data as the other marks. Its text: "PARAGON copied these notes to PARAGON's own folder (iCloud Drive › PARAGON) on 2026-11-14. The copy was made on the device “Martin's Mac”." "This folder is the old folder. PARAGON does not use it any more and has not changed it." "Keep it until you are sure that everything is in the new place." "Please leave this file here: it tells PARAGON on your other devices where the notes are now." "The lines below are for PARAGON. Please do not change them." The name of the file is not changed in build 320, because devices with build 319 look for exactly this name.
- Set aside: `.ams-para/Set aside by a move/<yyyy-MM-dd HHmmss>/<path>`. A name that is taken there gets " 2", " 3".
- In every vault, from the first start of build 319: `.ams-para/devices/<device id>.json` with `deviceID`, `name`, `build`, `kind` ("iPhone", "iPad" or "Mac") and `seenAt`. Written when it is not there, when the build, the name or the kind changed, or when the last one is 1 day old. A file still in iCloud is not written over.
- **What each mark holds** (`MoveStamp`): `format` 1, `moveID`, `generation` (the move number: 1 for the first move, 2 for a move back, and so on), `vaultID`, `at`, `deviceName`, `deviceID`, `toOwnFolder`, `placeName`, `build`. The mark "complete" also holds `fileCount`, `byteCount`, `noteCount` and `manifestDigest` (one fingerprint over path, size and fingerprint of every file). Fingerprints are SHA-256.

**What a folder is** (`VaultMove.standing`)

- The mark with the highest move number decides.
- `plain`: no mark. `vault`: the mark "complete" is highest. `halfMade`: a mark "being filled" with a higher number than any mark "complete" beside it. `movedAway`: a mark "moved" with a higher number than the mark "complete". `unclear`: a mark is there but its content is still in iCloud; nothing is decided.
- Equal numbers: "complete" wins over "moved".
- `openVault` refuses a `halfMade` folder, whoever asks: "Martin's Mac started copying notes into this folder on 2026-11-14 and has not finished. The notes are still in their old folder."
- A folder chosen by hand with **Choose folder…** that is PARAGON's own is treated as PARAGON's own.

**The devices** (`VaultDevices`)

- The list holds every device with a file in `.ams-para/devices/`, and every device known only by its Reminders sync file that synced in the last 60 days (`recentDays`). A device of the second kind is shown as "Another device: has not opened PARAGON 319 or newer yet, last synced 2026-11-10" and is not ready.
- A ready device is shown as "name: PARAGON 319, this device" or "name: PARAGON 319, seen 2026-11-14". On the Mac the name is the Mac's own name; on the iPhone and the iPad it is "iPhone" or "iPad".
- Not ready first, then by name. `VaultDevices.allReady` must hold for **Copy my notes** to be offered, and it is asked again when the copy starts (`startMove`). There is no way round it.

**The order of a move** (`AppModel.startMove`, then `VaultMove.run`)

1. A sync is running: "A sync is running. Try again when it has finished." Otherwise the text in the editor is written and the sync is held.
2. Copying to PARAGON's folder: the devices are checked again.
3. Going back: the changes waiting in the old folder are brought over first; a failure there stops it.
4. Every file is read, which brings it down from iCloud. A file still in iCloud is waited for, 20 seconds each. A file of which the device holds an older copy than iCloud is asked for and looked at every 0.25 seconds; if the newer one does not come in time, the file counts as not here. If a file does not come, the move stops and names it.
5. Versions of notes that iCloud had set aside are saved as "(conflict …)" copies, by the existing rule.
6. An outside copy with the reason "before move" is made in the place of the second copy and checked (`checkNewestOutsideCopy`): every file in it has the same bytes as the vault's, nothing was left out, the number of notes is equal. Otherwise "The backup copy could not be confirmed, so nothing was copied." followed by the reason.
7. The place the notes go to (`checkDestination`): it must be empty, or carry marks of this same vault. A complete vault of this vault there: refused, "Your notes are already in that place: …". A mark "being filled" by another device that is less than 1 day old (86 400 seconds): refused, "… is copying your notes too. …". Files that PARAGON did not put there: refused. A mark not readable yet: "iCloud has not finished listing the new place. Try again in a minute."
8. Room on the device: the bytes to copy and 20 MB more.
9. `arriving.json` is written. PARAGON waits until iCloud has it, 2 minutes at most, then checks that the mark is still its own (`checkOnlyOurMove`).
10. Copy. Each file is read 1 time; the bytes whose fingerprint is taken are the bytes that are written. The write goes through iCloud's file coordination, and the file keeps its dates.
11. Check: both folders are listed again and compared by number of files, size and fingerprint. Files that changed are copied again, 3 rounds at most; then "The copy could not be checked because these files kept changing: …".
12. Wait for iCloud: every file must be taken. Looked at every 2 seconds, 20 minutes at most (`uploadPatience`). iCloud full: "iCloud is full, so the copy could not be sent to your other devices. …". Too slow: "iCloud has not taken 12 files yet (no connection, or iCloud is busy). …".
13. Both folders are listed 1 more time. Differences are copied and sent too, 3 passes at most.
14. `checkOnlyOurMove` again. From here the move is not stopped any more ("Finishing…").
15. `manifest.json` and `complete.json` are written. An old mark "moved" in that folder is removed.
16. The text file is written into the folder the notes left. If it cannot be written, the move still counts; the next device that follows writes it.
17. The app opens the new folder, remembers the old one as "previous", and the sync goes on.

- **What is copied.** Every ordinary file under the vault folder, hidden ones too: notes, pictures, PDFs, sketches, templates, settings (`config.json`), the vault's identity, the Reminders memory of every device (`sync-state-<device>.json`), the backups inside the vault and the deleted notes. Left out: the move's own files, `.DS_Store`, files whose name starts with `._`, and Finder's `Icon` file.
- **What stops a move.** A link or another thing that is not an ordinary file: "The folder holds something PARAGON cannot copy safely: … Nothing was copied." The new place inside the old one, or the other way round.
- **Stop.** The button is on the pages **Copying your notes** and **Going back to the old folder** until the step "Finishing…". After a stop or a failure the folder the notes are in is not changed and not marked; the other folder is `halfMade`. The next try by the same device, in the same direction, goes on under the same move number and `moveID`; files that are there and equal are not copied again. The copied files are not removed by themselves.
- **Only 1 device.** `checkOnlyOurMove` fails when `.ams-para/move` holds a second file whose name starts with "arriving", when `arriving.json` cannot be read or carries another `moveID`, or when `complete.json` carries another `moveID` with the same or a higher move number.
- **Progress words** (`MoveProgress.words`): "Reading your notes…", "Reading your notes: 212 files…", "Copying 87 of 412 files…", "Comparing copies and originals: 300 of 824 files…", "Sending to iCloud: 87 of 412 files…", "Finishing…". Before the first one: "Getting ready…".

**Following a move on another device**

- A device knows that its notes are in a new place when its own folder carries the mark "moved", or when PARAGON's folder holds a complete vault with one of this vault's ids and a higher move number than its own folder shows.
- The device opens the new place only when the mark "complete" can be read, `manifest.json` has the fingerprint the mark names, and every listed file outside `.ams-para/Backups` and `.ams-para/Deleted` is there and downloaded (`PlaceLook.isComplete`).
- **Listed files that are not there.** **Open the new folder** is offered all the same when nothing is still arriving, at most a tenth of the listed files are not there (`mayOpenDespiteMissing`: missing × 10 ≤ expected), and 4 looks 30 seconds apart agree. The page names up to 8 of them. On the welcome screen: 12 looks 5 seconds apart, and only with `config.json` listed; the caution then reads "2 files from the list made when the notes were copied are not here. They may have been deleted or renamed since."
- **Open the new folder** (`followMove`): the new place is looked at again; `bringOver` from the folder the device leaves; the mark "moved" is written there if it is missing; the app switches. Not complete yet: the page **Not yet** with "The notes are not complete in the new place on this device yet. Nothing has changed."
- The new place cannot be reached: "PARAGON cannot reach iCloud Drive on this iPhone. Check that iCloud Drive is on for PARAGON." After a move back, on a device that does not know the old folder: "PARAGON cannot find that folder on this iPhone. Choose it in Settings › Vault › Choose folder…".
- **A move back that never finished.** A device in the old folder shows the bar "… is copying your notes back into this folder. Reminders sync is paused until that is done.", with no button. If PARAGON's folder still holds the complete vault, the bar is "Your notes are in a new place: iCloud Drive › PARAGON." with **Open…** instead.

**Reminders sync**

- `syncNow` looks at the marks first (`refreshOwnFolder`), every time. Standing `movedAway`, `halfMade` or `unclear`, or a complete vault of this vault in PARAGON's folder with a higher move number: the sync rests, with "Reminders sync is paused: your notes are in another folder now." on a sync you asked for.
- The same look runs every 30 seconds while a vault is open.
- During a move, a move back, **Open the new folder** and **Bring them over**, the sync is held as during a sync.

**Bring over** (`VaultMove.bringOver`)

- Compared with `seen-<device id>.json`, or the first time with `manifest.json`.
- The old folder's file equals the list: nothing. Not in the new folder: copied. In the new folder with the same bytes: nothing.
- The new folder's file equals the list, and the old folder's file is under `.ams-para/` or its date is later than the date of the mark "complete": the new one is first copied to `.ams-para/Set aside by a move/<yyyy-MM-dd HHmmss>/<path>`, then replaced.
- Anything else for a file of yours: a conflict copy "<name> (conflict yyyy-MM-dd HHmm).md" beside the note; " 2", " 3" when the name is taken; never 2 copies with the same bytes. For PARAGON's own files under `.ams-para/`, the new folder's stays.
- Deleted in the old folder: reported only.
- `.ams-para/Backups` of the old folder is not looked at. The old folder is only read.
- What the device saw is remembered only when no file failed; a file that failed is offered again.
- The result lines: "2 files new in the old folder: copied.", "1 file changed in the old folder: brought over.", "1 file changed in both places: both versions kept, the old folder's with “(conflict …)” in its name.", "1 file deleted in the old folder: still here, nothing was deleted.", "2 files could not be brought over and are still in the old folder: …".

**Looking at the old folder**

- Only devices in PARAGON's folder that still know the old one. When the vault opens, and then 1 time an hour at most (3600 seconds). It never waits for iCloud: a file that is not down yet is asked for, and the look is tried again at the next 30-second look.
- Only your own files, outside `.ams-para`, raise the bar. The page lists 12 at most, then "and N more".
- **Not now** stores the list; the bar comes back when the list is a different one.

**Go back**

- Shown while the device is in PARAGON's folder, knows the old folder, and can reach it.
- `bringOver` from the old folder first; then the same move with `toOwnFolder` false and the next move number.
- In the old folder: equal files stay; files that differ and files that are no longer in the vault are moved to `Set aside by a move`. Its mark "moved" is removed when the mark "complete" is written.
- Afterwards the device forgets the "previous" folder.

**Start, and the welcome screen**

- `vaultInOwnFolder` is set: the folder is looked for off the main thread, with "Opening your notes…". It is opened only when its standing is `vault` or `movedAway` and `config.json` is listed. Otherwise: "Your notes are in iCloud Drive › PARAGON, and iCloud has not brought them to this iPhone yet. PARAGON is waiting and opens them when they are here." PARAGON then looks every 5 seconds.
- iCloud cannot be reached: "PARAGON cannot reach its folder in iCloud Drive on this iPhone. Check that you are signed in to iCloud and that iCloud Drive is on for PARAGON."
- No folder known: PARAGON looks into its own folder every 5 seconds, writing nothing, and shows what is there: **I found your notes here — use them?** with **Use them** and **Choose a different folder…**; or the line that the notes are still arriving; or "Martin's Mac started copying the notes into iCloud Drive › PARAGON on 2026-11-14 and has not finished. Finish it on that device; the notes are still in their old folder."
- No vault is ever set up afresh in PARAGON's folder at a start.
- **Siri and Shortcuts.** `AppModel.vaultOnceOpen()` waits while the folder is being opened, 40 times 250 milliseconds at most (10 seconds), before an action answers that there is no vault.

**Backups**

- `Vault.backedUpPaths` and `Vault.restore` skip every path for which `VaultMove.isMoveOwn` holds.

**Page titles**

- **Copy your notes to PARAGON's own folder**, **Copying your notes**, **Your notes are in PARAGON's own folder**, **Your notes were not copied**, **Nothing was copied**, **The copy is complete, but PARAGON could not switch yet**.
- **Your notes are in a new place**, **Opening your notes in their new place**, **Your notes are open in their new place**, **Not yet**.
- "3 files were changed in the old folder since the copy" (the title of the page behind **Look…**), **Bringing the changes over**, **The changes are here**, **Nothing was brought over**.
- **Go back to the old folder**, **Going back to the old folder**, **PARAGON is back in the old folder**, **PARAGON did not go back**.
- Buttons: **Cancel**, **Copy my notes**, **Check again**, **Stop**, **OK**, **Done**, **Not now**, **Open the new folder**, **Bring them over**, **Go back**; on the bar **More…**, **Open…**, **Look…**. On the Mac, Escape is the left button and Return is the coloured one.

**Identifiers**

- The page: `ownFolder.page`, `ownFolder.title`, `ownFolder.cancel`, `ownFolder.confirm`.
- The bar: `ownFolder.banner`, `ownFolder.banner.button`.
- **Settings › Vault**: `settings.vault.move`, `settings.vault.moved`, `settings.vault.show`, `settings.vault.goBack`.
- The welcome screen: `ownFolder.found`, `ownFolder.found.use`.

**For trying without iCloud** (debug builds only, never in TestFlight): `-paragon-own-folder <path>` uses an ordinary folder as PARAGON's own; `-paragon-own-step offer`, `move` or `back` opens that step by itself.

**Not changed.** The share extension and the widgets do not open the vault and know nothing of a move. The debug Mac app has no iCloud right, so it never offers the copy unless `-paragon-own-folder` is given.

**Tests.** `VaultMoveTests` (16): a move copies and checks every file and leaves the old folder as it was; a stopped move is never a vault and can be run again; a note changed during the copy; iCloud full, no room, a folder with other files; a second device opens only a complete folder; changes in the old folder; going back sets aside and never deletes; devices not on this build hold the offer back; the backup check; never into a vault in use; 2 devices at the same time; a note changed during the wait for iCloud; odd names, empty files and links; an older copy never replaces a newer note; many listed files not there means still arriving; backups and restore leave the marks alone.

**The build number.** `BuildStamp.number` is 319; **Settings › About** says 1.0 (319).

## Exact details (build 320)

The precise list behind build 320, **Words only: copy, not move**, for reference. Build 320 changes words. No rule, no check, no order and no button place is changed. The words in `code letters` are names in the app's code. You do not need them to use PARAGON.

**Settings › Vault**

- Button: **Move to PARAGON's own folder…** → **Copy to PARAGON's own folder…**
- Grey line: "PARAGON can keep the notes in a folder of its own in iCloud Drive and open it by itself on every device. Nothing happens until you say so." → "PARAGON can copy the notes into a folder of its own in iCloud Drive and open it by itself on every device. Your old folder stays as it is. Nothing happens until you say so."
- Button on a device that has not followed yet: **Your notes have moved…** → **Your notes are in a new place…**

**The offer page**

- Title: **Move your notes to PARAGON's own folder** → **Copy your notes to PARAGON's own folder**
- New first line under **What happens**: "PARAGON copies your notes, checks every file, and then uses the copy. Your old folder stays as it is."
- "Reminders sync is paused during the move and goes on afterwards." → "Reminders sync is paused while the notes are copied and goes on afterwards."
- "Move on one device only. On your other devices PARAGON then shows “Your notes have moved”: press Open… there." → "Copy on one device only. On your other devices PARAGON then shows “Your notes are in a new place”: press Open… there."
- Main button: **Move my notes** → **Copy my notes**
- Warning: "Another device is moving the notes" → "Another device is copying the notes"
- Warning: "… So PARAGON does not move the notes yet." → "… So PARAGON does not copy the notes yet."
- Warning: "… started a move on 2026-11-14 and has not finished it. Finish the move on that device, or try again tomorrow." → "… started copying on 2026-11-14 and has not finished it. Finish it on that device, or try again tomorrow."

**While it works, and the end**

- Title: **Moving your notes** → **Copying your notes**
- Title: **Your notes were not moved** → **Your notes were not copied**
- Title: **Nothing was moved** → **Nothing was copied**
- "… so nothing was moved." → "… so nothing was copied." (4 messages: a device not ready, no place for the backup copy, the backup copy not confirmed, the vault's id still coming from iCloud)
- On the last page: "On your other devices PARAGON shows “Your notes have moved”. …" → "On your other devices PARAGON shows “Your notes are in a new place”. …"

**On your other devices**

- Bar: "Your notes have moved to iCloud Drive › PARAGON." → "Your notes are in a new place: iCloud Drive › PARAGON."
- Bar: "Your notes have moved. Waiting for iCloud: 87 of 125 files." → "Your notes are in a new place. Waiting for iCloud: 87 of 125 files."
- Bar: "Your notes have moved. PARAGON cannot reach the new place on this iPhone." → "Your notes are in a new place. PARAGON cannot reach it on this iPhone."
- Bar: "… is moving your notes back into this folder. …" → "… is copying your notes back into this folder. …"
- Bar: "3 files were changed in the old folder after the move." → "3 files were changed in the old folder since the copy." The title of the page behind **Look…** changes in the same way.
- Page title: **Your notes have moved** → **Your notes are in a new place**
- "They are now in iCloud Drive › PARAGON. The move was made on 2026-11-14, on the device “MacBook Pro”." → "On 2026-11-14 “MacBook Pro” copied them to iCloud Drive › PARAGON."
- "What you changed on this iPhone since the move comes along." → "What you changed on this iPhone since then comes along."
- "2 files from the list made at the move are not there. You have probably deleted or renamed them after the move:" → "2 files from the list made when the notes were copied are not there. You have probably deleted or renamed them since then:"
- "Nothing had changed on this iPhone since the move." → "Nothing had changed on this iPhone since the copy."
- **Sync now**: "Reminders sync is paused: your notes have moved to another folder." → "Reminders sync is paused: your notes are in another folder now."

**Go back to the old folder**

- "A file in the old folder that is replaced, or that you have deleted since the move, is set aside there, not deleted." → "… since the copy, …"
- "They follow: PARAGON shows “Your notes have moved” there. Press Open… on each." → "They follow: PARAGON shows “Your notes are in a new place” there. Press Open… on each."

**The welcome screen, and opening a folder**

- "2 files from the list made at the move are not here. …" → "2 files from the list made when the notes were copied are not here. …"
- "A move into iCloud Drive › PARAGON was started on 2026-11-14 by Martin's Mac and has not finished. …" → "Martin's Mac started copying the notes into iCloud Drive › PARAGON on 2026-11-14 and has not finished. …"
- "A move into this folder was started on 2026-11-14 by Martin's Mac and has not finished. …" → "Martin's Mac started copying notes into this folder on 2026-11-14 and has not finished. …"

**Messages when something stops the copy** (`MoveError`)

- "The move was stopped. Your notes are where they were." → "The copy was stopped. Your notes are where they were."
- "… Nothing was moved." → "… Nothing was copied." (3 messages: something PARAGON cannot copy safely, other files in the new place, not enough room)
- "The move could not be finished: …" → "The copy could not be finished: …"
- "The list of files made at the move is not on this device yet." → "The list of files made when the notes were copied is not on this device yet."
- "Your notes are already in that place: … moved them. … PARAGON will soon show “Your notes have moved” on this device." → "Your notes are already in that place: … copied them. … PARAGON will soon show “Your notes are in a new place” on this device."
- "… is moving your notes too. Two devices must not move them at the same time, so this move stopped. …" → "… is copying your notes too. Two devices must not copy them at the same time, so this copy stopped. …"

**The text file in the old folder**

- Inside the file: "PARAGON moved these notes to … The move was made on the device …" → "PARAGON copied these notes to … The copy was made on the device …"; "This folder is the old copy." → "This folder is the old folder."; "… where the notes went." → "… where the notes are now."
- The lines for PARAGON at the end of the file are the same as before. PARAGON reads only those.

**Not changed, and why**

- The name of that text file is still **PARAGON has moved these notes.txt**. Devices with build 319 look for exactly this name.
- The hidden folder `.ams-para/Set aside by a move/` keeps its name, so the page after **Go back** still says “Set aside by a move”.
- The backup copy made before the notes are copied is still listed with the reason "before move". The reason is part of the backup folder's name.
- "A note comes here with Move to Archive", "Moved to tomorrow." and the other words about moving a task or a note are not about this, and are as before.
- **Go back to the old folder…**, **Go back**, **Bring them over**, **Open the new folder**, **Show in Files**, **Show in Finder**, **Check again**, **Stop**, **Done** and **Not now** are as before.
- `VaultMove.firstBuild` is still 319: a device with build 319 or build 320 is ready.
- No name in the code, no identifier and no stored key is changed. In the code the work is still called a move.

**Tests.** `VaultMoveTests` reads the new first 2 sentences of the text file. No other test changed.

**The build number.** `BuildStamp.number` is 320; **Settings › About** says 1.0 (320).

## On the iPhone

**Reading or writing.** A note opens ready to write in. The **pencil** button at the bottom of the note (in the toolbar on the Mac) turns writing on and off. When the pencil is lit and its frame is solid, you are writing. When it is grey with a thin solid frame, you are reading: the note is shown rendered — headings, bold, tappable links — and there is no cursor. If a note ever seems to refuse your typing, look at that button.


Sync with Reminders from the phone with the button in the top right of every screen (Today, Inbox and the others), or Settings › Reminders sync › Sync now. Since build 300 the button has a clock ring round it that shows how fresh the last sync is: full just after a sync, emptying over two hours, green for the first hour and yellow after that (and yellow while a sync waits for iCloud), a full red ring when the last sync had conflicts, a grey empty ring when there has never been a sync; while syncing it turns and the ring is full. Since build 312 the small words under it say "Not synced" before any sync, then "Synced just now" (under 1 minute), "Synced 5 min ago", "Synced 3 h ago" (under 24 hours), then the date, "Synced 2026-11-09". "Syncing", "iCloud" and "1 conflict" or "2 conflicts" are as before. Before build 312 the words were only a time, such as "10:05", or "Not synced yet". Since build 312 one tap opens the menu: **Sync now**, **Show me what would change…**, **Last sync report…** (only when there is a report), a line, and **Sync settings…** (opens Settings at Reminders sync). No hold is needed. Before, a tap synced at once and the other choices needed a hold; so **Sync now** is 2 taps now, and was 1. Since build 301 the phone also gives a gentle tap you can feel when a sync finishes: a soft tap when all is well, a warning buzz when it ended with conflicts. The first sync is when iOS asks for permission to use Reminders. Since build 303 the phone also gives a soft tap you can feel when you tick a task done, in any list and on a box in a note; unticking gives nothing, and the Mac gives nothing.

On the phone, a note's own actions — Rename, Archive and Delete — are behind the **⋯** button at the top right of the note (three small rings since build 311; before, three specks). On the Mac they are buttons in the toolbar; since build 312 **Move to Archive** has the word **Archive** beside its picture. The pencil is not in there: it is always on screen.

On the phone, **Today**, **Plan**, **Actions**, **Inbox** and **Browse** open with a large title. It becomes a small title when you scroll. Since build 310 every list screen also opens with a slim band in its part's colour (see **How PARAGON looks**). The tab bar icons are hand-drawn marks: **Actions** is a ticked list (since build 311; before, an empty ring, which now means an open task only), **Browse** is a folder (before, four squares, which **Areas** keeps), and **Plan** is an upright day line with two blocks on it (before, a calendar page with a tiny clock). The tab you are on has the colour of its screen: purple for **Today**, **Plan** and **Browse**, green for **Actions**, leaf green for **Inbox** (since build 310; before, Apple's blue on every tab). The number on the **Inbox** tab is white on leaf green, not red, because red means overdue or danger. The phone shows five tabs: **Today**, **Plan**, **Actions**, **Inbox** and **Browse**. **Plan** is the day on an hour ruler and **Actions** is All actions. **Browse** holds everything else: Aspirations, Goals, Projects, Areas, Resources, Archive, Calendar, Weekly review, Map, Done, Deleted, and Settings with Help. Since build 312 **Browse** opens with a search field (**Search notes**) at the very top and, under it, one line with the notes you opened last, up to 5; the **Search** row and the **Recent** row lower down are gone (see **Finding things again**). A gear at the top right of **Browse** opens **Settings**; the **Settings and Help** row at the foot of **Browse** is still there too. On the iPhone **Settings** opens with **Help** first (**How it works**, **Words in PARAGON**, **What's new**; the last was called "Version history" before build 315), then **Vault** and the rest as before. The version, PARAGON 1.0 (315), is at the foot of **Browse**, and since build 315 also in **Settings › About**; a long press on it shows the **Work** section. On the Mac the same long press is on the **Goals and PARA** heading in the sidebar (named **PARA** before build 314). Since build 312 **Today**, **Actions** and **Inbox** have a round **+** button at the bottom right, just above the tab bar, in the leaf green of the **Inbox**. It opens **New** with **Inbox** chosen, for a quick line into the Inbox. Before, these tabs had a small **+** at the top left. **Plan** keeps its own small **+**. Tap a note to open it and swipe from the left edge to go back. Long-press a task for the task menu. The share sheet in other apps has a PARAGON entry that sends text or a link to the Inbox. Since build 315 a widget or a link that opens **Today** or the **Inbox** changes to that tab and shows the tab's first screen; before, the iPhone stayed on the tab you were on. The **Weekly review** and the other screens that are not a tab open on **Browse**, as before.

## Moving between the tabs

Press a tab in the bar at the bottom to move to it: Today, Plan, Actions, Inbox, Browse. The
**Inbox** tab shows how many lines are waiting in the Inbox.

You cannot swipe sideways between the tabs any more. The swipe made the app crash on the
iPhone, so since build 232 the tabs work the same way as in Apple's own apps.

A drag from the very **left edge** still means *go back*, as it does everywhere in iOS.

**Actions** has its own filter at the top: **All**, **With a date**, **No date** and **Next
actions**.

## The Mac window

Since build 314 (premium review round 7, Mac only; the iPhone is unchanged).

**The sidebar has four groups.**
- **Do**: **Inbox**, **Today**, **Plan**, **All actions**.
- **Goals and PARA**: **Aspirations**, **Goals**, **Projects**, **Areas**, **Resources**, **Archive**. A long press on this heading shows **Work**, as before.
- **Look back**: **Calendar**, **Weekly review**, **Map**, **Recent**, **Done**, **Deleted**.
- **Tools**: **Templates**, **Snippets**, **Tags**.

Before build 314, **All actions** sat next to **Recent** and **Done**; it is now under **Do**. Click any group heading to fold the group shut, and again to open it; a small arrow shows which. PARAGON remembers what you folded. **Tools** starts folded the first time. There is no **Search** row on the Mac any more: the search field in the toolbar replaces it. The iPad keeps the row.

**Go menu shortcuts.** **⌘1** **Inbox**, **⌘2** **Today**, **⌘3** **Plan**, **⌘4** **All actions**, **⌘5** **Aspirations**, and since build 315 **⌘6** **Projects** and **⌘7** **Weekly review**. **⇧⌘P** (**Plan**) and **⇧⌘M** (**Map**) still work.

**Settings › Help** (since build 315) has **How it works**, **Words in PARAGON** and **What's new**, on the iPhone and on the Mac. On the Mac each row opens the Help window on that page; the **Help** menu has the same three. Before build 315 the Mac had only **Words in PARAGON** there. **What's new** lists every version, newest first; before build 315 it was called "Version history".

**A list uses the whole window until you open a note.** These screens are only a list: **Today**, **Weekly review**, **All actions**, **Search**, **Done**, **Deleted**, **Recent**, **Tags**, **Projects**, **Areas**, **Resources**, **Archive** and **Work**. On the Mac they are as wide as the window. Open a note and the list narrows to a column, with the note beside it. Close the note and the list is full width again: press the **Close** button (a small cross) in the note's toolbar, or choose **Go › Close Note** (**⌃⌘↑**, beside Back and Forward on ⌃⌘← and ⌃⌘→). **Inbox**, **Calendar**, **Plan**, **Templates**, **Snippets**, **Aspirations** and **Goals** keep their two parts side by side, because the right side has real content there. Before build 314 the list-only screens were a narrow column with an empty **New…** panel beside it.

**One search field.** It is in the toolbar on every screen. Type in it and **Search** opens with your words; **⇧⌘F** jumps to it. The small **Search notes** field at the top of each Mac list is gone.

## On the iPad

The iPhone app is also an iPad app. Install it from TestFlight on the iPad in the same way.

The iPad shows the Mac's three columns, not the iPhone's tabs: the sidebar, the list, and the note. The build 314 changes to the Mac window (four sidebar groups, full-width lists, one toolbar search field) are for the Mac only. The iPad keeps its sidebar **Search** row and its list fields.

- **In landscape** (wider than tall), all three columns fit on the screen, side by side. On the iPad mini the note column is about as wide as an iPhone screen.
- **In portrait** (taller than wide), iPadOS decides how the columns are shown. PARAGON is made for the iPad in landscape.

Since build 316 the toolbar over an open note has a **Find in note** button (a magnifying glass, right after the pencil). See **Find in a note**.

## Widgets

Four widgets on the Mac. On the iPhone and the iPad, four for the Home Screen and one for the Lock Screen (since build 317; before, three, and none for the Lock Screen). Since build 317 **Today's plan** is on the iPhone and the iPad too, and you can tick a task on **Today's actions**.

**On the Mac**: click the date and time at the right of the menu bar to open Notification Centre, scroll to the bottom and click **Edit Widgets**. Search for **PARAGON** and drag one in. You can also right-click the desktop and choose **Edit Widgets**, which puts the widget on the desktop itself.

**The first time after an update, PARAGON may not be in that list.** macOS only learns about a new widget once the app it came with has run. Open PARAGON, quit it with ⌘Q, open it again, and look in **Edit Widgets** once more. If it is still missing, restart the Mac: macOS keeps its own list of known widgets and that list is stubborn about new ones.

**On the iPhone**: press and hold the Home Screen, press **+** at the top, search for **PARAGON**, and pick one.

**Today's actions** is the same list the planner shows: everything due today or earlier, then your next actions. Each line shows the note it lives in and, under it, **what it serves** — the goal in gold, or the area in pink. It comes in three sizes; the small one shows three lines, the medium four, the large seven.

**Ticking a task on Today's actions** (since build 317; iPhone, iPad and Mac). In the medium and the large size each line starts with a tick circle, in the colour of the task's kind of note.

- Press the circle and the line is ticked at once: the circle is ticked, and the words turn grey with a line through them.
- Press the rest of the line and its note opens, as before.
- Press a ticked circle again and the tick is taken back. This works until PARAGON has written the tick.
- The small size has no tick circle. The whole small widget opens **Today**.
- **The widget does not write into your notes.** It leaves a short line in a small file that the widget and PARAGON share. PARAGON itself writes the tick into the note: when it starts, when it comes to the front, and every 10 seconds while it is open.
- So on the Mac, with PARAGON open, the tick is in the note within about 10 seconds. On the iPhone, with PARAGON closed, the tick waits until you next open PARAGON. Until then the widget shows the task as ticked, but the note, Apple Reminders and your other device still have it open.
- When the tick is written, the line leaves the widget, and PARAGON says "Ticked on the widget: Book the hotel", or "3 tasks ticked on the widget."
- It is written the same way as a tick in the app: what you are typing is saved first, a repeating task gets its next copy, and Reminders follows at the next sync.
- A tick only lands on the task you pressed. If that task is gone, already done, or cannot be told apart from another task with the same words and date, the tick is dropped and nothing else is ticked. The task then shows as open again on the widget.
- If the note is still coming from iCloud, or iCloud holds a newer copy of it, the tick waits.
- After an update from an older build, the tick circles show once you have opened PARAGON one time.

**What is waiting** is four numbers: due today, overdue, waiting in the Inbox, and due for a look. A zero is grey and a number that wants you is in its own colour, so the one to read finds you first. The Inbox number is leaf green (since build 310; before, amber).

**One list** (since build 316) is a small widget for one list. It shows the list's name and mark, one big number, and a few words, like "3 due today" or "Nothing overdue". A press opens that list. Choose the list with the widget's own **Edit Widget** (hold the widget on the iPhone, right-click it on the Mac): **Today**, **Overdue**, **Inbox** or **Review**. It starts as **Today**. You can place one for each list. A zero is grey, as on **What is waiting**; the words at zero are "Nothing due today", "Nothing overdue", "The Inbox is empty" and "Nothing for a look". **Today** and **Overdue** open **Today**, **Inbox** opens the **Inbox**, and **Review** opens the **Weekly review**. It comes in the small size only.

**Today's plan** shows your plan for today. Since build 317 it is on the iPhone and the iPad as well as on the Mac (before, on the Mac only). It comes in three sizes. At the top it says **Today's plan**. Under that, in big orange letters, is the plan block you are in now, or else the next one, for example **Swim**. Under the name, a few words say when it is: **Now · 20 min left**, **Now · until 11:00**, **in 25 min** or **at 14:00**. Under that, in grey, comes the block after it, like **Then 11:00 Write the report**.

- The big line is made from your plan blocks only. It names the same block as the **Now** and **Then** lines on **Today**. Your calendar events are never in it.
- The medium widget has a small ruler of the next three hours on its right, and the large one has a ruler of six hours below the words. The small widget has the words only.
- The ruler has two lanes, like **Plan**: **Calendar** on the left and **Time blocks** on the right. A plan block is an orange box with an edge. A calendar event is a purple outline with no fill. When your desktop is not in front, macOS draws widgets in grey, and then the shape still tells them apart.
- The thin blue-grey line with a dot is the time right now.
- The words and the line move by themselves, also when PARAGON is closed. The widget has a new picture ready for every minute of the next hour, and then for every quarter hour. macOS decides when it really draws them. If it is late, the line and **at 14:00** stay right, and **in 25 min** can be up to a quarter of an hour old.
- A block that you also put into Apple Calendar shows in both lanes, as it does in **Plan**. The big line uses the block only, so it never shows it twice. Two events at the same time are drawn on top of each other in the small ruler.
- On a day with no blocks it says **No blocks planned today** and **Click to plan the day** (on the iPhone and the iPad: **Tap to plan the day**). After the last block it says **Nothing more planned today**.
- Click it and PARAGON opens on **Plan**, on today. If PARAGON already has its window open, the click uses that window and brings it to the front. It does not open a second window.
- If you add, move or remove a block in **Plan**, or an event changes in Apple Calendar, the widget follows after a few seconds.
- The Calendar lane needs **Show the day's events in Today and in daily notes** to be on in Settings, and Calendar access to be allowed. If not, the lane says **Calendar not shown**, on the medium widget inside the lane and on the large one above it. The widget never asks for Calendar access: that question belongs to PARAGON itself.
- On an iPhone that shows its widgets tinted or clear, the block's name and the plan blocks take the tint and the calendar's outlines stay plain. So the 2 lanes can still be told apart.

**Due today** (since build 317) is a widget for the Lock Screen of the iPhone and the iPad. It comes in 3 shapes.

- Round: the number of open tasks due today or earlier, with the word "due" under it. At zero it shows a drawn tick.
- Wide: 1 line in words, for example "3 due today", and under it your next 2 actions (the first 2 lines of **Today's actions**). With no actions it says "Nothing due, nothing waiting".
- The line above the clock: words only, for example "3 due today".
- The words are "3 due today", "3 due today, 1 overdue", "2 overdue" or "Nothing due today".
- If PARAGON has written nothing for today yet, it says "Open PARAGON" and never shows yesterday's number. The round shape then shows the sun mark and no number.
- A task you ticked on a widget is already left out of the number and the lines.
- A tap opens PARAGON on **Today**.
- To add it: hold the Lock Screen, press **Customise**, choose the Lock Screen, press the widget area, and pick PARAGON.

Clicking or tapping a widget opens PARAGON, and since build 315 it opens the right place. A press on a line of **Today's actions** opens that action's note. A press on a number of **What is waiting** opens its list: *due today* and *overdue* open **Today**, *in the Inbox* opens the **Inbox**, *for a look* opens the **Weekly review**. Anywhere else on these two widgets opens **Today**. Overdue has no screen of its own: it lands on **Today**, and if the **Overdue** part is folded there, it stays folded. The small size can open only one place (Apple's rule): small **Today's actions** opens **Today**, and small **What is waiting** opens the first of its four lists that is not empty. **Today's plan** opens **Plan**, as before. Before build 315 a press only opened PARAGON, wherever it was.

Each side is fed by the app on that machine: the Mac widget by PARAGON on the Mac, the iPhone widget by PARAGON on the iPhone. They can therefore show slightly different things for a moment, until both apps have seen the same notes through iCloud.

### How they get their information

The widget never opens your vault. It cannot: the vault is a folder you chose, and only the app itself is allowed to open it.

Instead, **PARAGON writes a short summary for the widget a few seconds after a change that the widget would show**, and the widget reads only that. These changes reach it: you tick a task, you change a task's date, you add a task that is due today or is a next action, a line comes into the Inbox or leaves it, a sync finishes, or a file arrives from iCloud. Two more changes reach it (since build 317 on the iPhone too, before on the Mac only): you add, move or remove a plan block, or an event today changes in Apple Calendar. A change that the widget does not draw writes nothing. So:

- The widgets are right as of the last time the app noticed a change.
- The few seconds are on purpose. While you type, PARAGON waits until you stop. When you leave PARAGON for another app or the Home Screen, or quit it, it writes at once. It writes only when the widget would show something different.
- **After midnight** the widgets move on to the new day by themselves, as long as PARAGON is running.
- **Open PARAGON once after installing**, or the widget has nothing to read yet and says so. On the Mac that means PARAGON on the Mac; on the iPhone, PARAGON on the iPhone.
- If PARAGON has not seen today yet — it was not running at midnight and you have not opened it — the widget says **"Nothing since you last opened PARAGON"** rather than showing yesterday's list as though it were this morning's.
- When there is genuinely nothing due and nothing waiting, it says that too. An empty widget and a widget with nothing to say are different things, and it never leaves you guessing which one you are looking at.

The widget never writes into your notes, and nothing about your notes leaves the machine it is on. Since build 317 one thing is written by the widget: a tick on **Today's actions** leaves a short line in a second small file, `widget-ticks.jsonl`, in the same shared folder, and PARAGON itself then ticks the task in its note (see **Ticking a task on Today's actions** above). The plan is in the summary too: the titles of today's plan blocks and today's calendar events are kept in the same small file, which stays on this device, inside PARAGON's own shared folder. Since build 317 PARAGON on the iPhone writes them too, because the iPhone now has the **Today's plan** widget. The events are written only when PARAGON already has Calendar access and shows events.

### When the app asks for a vault folder

If PARAGON shows the welcome screen with **Choose a vault folder…**, it has no folder open. A
widget on that device stays empty, because the app writes nothing for it to read.

If PARAGON knows the folder you used last, the first button is **Open <your folder> again**.
That is the way back. If it could not open that folder by itself, it says so in orange above
the buttons: the folder may have been renamed or moved, or iCloud may not have it on this
device yet. Pressing the button again after iCloud has caught up is usually enough.

**Choose a different folder…** is the second button, for when you really do want another vault. Since build 308 PARAGON looks into the folder you choose before it opens it, says how many notes it has or that it is not a vault yet, and waits while iCloud is still listing it; see **Your vault**.

### When a widget looks empty

Every widget carries one small grey line at the very bottom, for example **PARAGON 315 ·
written 09:41**. It is there so that a widget can never be a blank box with no explanation.
Read it like this:

- **written 09:41** — the widget is working. 09:41 is the time of the last change that PARAGON passed on to it, not the time of the last look, so an old time is normal when nothing has changed. If the rest of the widget is empty, there is genuinely nothing due and nothing waiting.
- **nothing written yet** — the app has not written a summary on this phone. Open PARAGON once.
- **shared folder missing** — the widget cannot reach the small file the app writes. Nothing
  you do on the phone will mend this; it has to be fixed in the build. Tell me and I will.
- **Open PARAGON once and your plan appears here** — on **Today's plan**: the file has no plan in it yet, because it was written by an older build. Open PARAGON once.
- **no line at all, a completely blank box** — the widget itself is not running. That is also a
  build problem, not something you can fix yourself.

**Settings › Widget** says the same thing from the app's side: whether the shared folder was
found, when the summary was last written (that is, when something the widget shows last
changed), and how many actions are in it. On the Mac a line **Today's plan** says what that widget has to draw, like **3 blocks · 2 events** or **3 blocks · Calendar not shown**. There is a **Write it again now** button for when you
want to be sure. It writes at once, even when nothing has changed.

## Mac and iPhone together

Keep the vault in iCloud Drive and pick the same folder on both devices. iCloud carries the files across. Each device syncs with Reminders on its own.

### How iCloud moves the files

There is no PARAGON server. Everything the app knows lives in the markdown files in your vault folder, and iCloud is what copies those files between the Mac and the phone — the same way it does for any other folder in iCloud Drive.

That has one consequence worth knowing. iCloud does not push every file to every device the moment it is written. It tells the other device that a file exists and leaves the contents behind until something asks for them. Until then there is only a marker where the file should be: the name is reserved, but the note is not really on the phone yet. This is normal iCloud behaviour, and it is why "Optimise storage" on a phone with little space left can leave a lot of your vault as markers.

The app therefore asks. When it starts, when you open a vault, and once a minute while it runs, it looks through the vault for anything that has not arrived and asks iCloud to fetch it. A few seconds later the file is there and the app reloads by itself. You do not have to do anything. Since build 308 a re-read reads only the notes that changed and runs in the background, so the screen does not stop for it, however many notes you have.

**PARAGON never waits for iCloud while it starts.** It opens at once with the notes that are on the device and fetches the rest in the background, the Inbox and today's note first. While notes are still coming, a short orange line says how many — at the foot of the sidebar on the Mac, at the top of **Today** on the iPhone — with a button, **Ask iCloud again**. If the Inbox note itself has not arrived, the Inbox says **Still coming from iCloud** instead of **Inbox zero**. A capture that cannot be filed yet is kept and filed when the note arrives.

Some things wait until every note is here, because they reach across the whole vault. **The Reminders sync waits by itself**: if you press **Sync with Reminders** (or the timer starts a sync) while notes are still arriving or being updated, nothing goes wrong and no alert appears. PARAGON remembers the sync and starts it as soon as the first re-read of the vault finds every note here. If you pressed the button, a short note says "Notes are still arriving from iCloud. The sync starts by itself as soon as they are here."; a sync from the timer waits quietly. Since build 301 the foot of the sidebar on the Mac and iPad (under the orange iCloud warning, if that is there too) shows the same **clock ring** as the iPhone's sync button (build 300), and no longer the pill that said "Synced at 10:55" (a pill since build 292, before that a grey line). It is a small ring, 22 points wide, round the hand-drawn sync mark, with a short word beside it. The ring is full just after a sync and empties over two hours, like a clock running down. It is green for the first hour and yellow after that, and also yellow while a sync waits for notes from iCloud. It is a full red ring when the last sync had conflicts, and a grey empty ring before any sync since the app started. While a sync runs the mark turns and the ring is full. The word beside it is the time of the last sync today (**10:55**), **Yesterday**, the day (**2026-11-09**), **Syncing**, **iCloud** (waiting for iCloud), **Not synced yet**, or **2 conflicts** (**1 conflict**); warnings ("worth knowing") do not change the colour. On the Mac and iPad one press syncs now; right-click (press and hold on the iPad) opens the menu **Sync now**, **Show me what would change…**, **Last sync report…** and **Sync settings…** (opens Settings at **Reminders sync**). The iPhone's button at the top right looks and works the same way: the Mac, the iPad and the iPhone now show the sync alike. It never runs over half a vault or over an older copy of a note, and closing the vault forgets a waiting sync. The sync rehearsal, renaming or removing a tag everywhere, renaming a note with its links, and restoring a backup still say why they cannot start and ask you to try again in a moment. The same is true while iCloud is bringing a newer version of a note that is already on the device. The widget also keeps its last complete picture until every note is here.

### When something takes a moment to arrive

Make a note or a template on the Mac and it usually appears on the phone within seconds of opening the app. If it does not, it is nearly always one of these:

- The phone has no connection, or is on a network that is being slow. iCloud is doing nothing until it can.
- The Mac has not finished uploading. Look at the file in Finder: a small cloud with an arrow means it is still on its way up.
- The phone app has been open the whole time and is between checks. Switch away and back, and it looks again.

While a template is on its way, the Templates list names it and says "coming from iCloud" instead of pretending it is not there.

### If a file seems stuck

Open the Files app on the iPhone, find your vault folder, and tap the file once. That is the same request the app makes, and it usually settles it. If a whole folder looks empty on the phone but is full on the Mac, check that both devices point at the same folder under Settings › Vault, and that iCloud Drive is switched on in iOS Settings.

### Editing the same note on both devices

The app checks every few seconds whether files changed outside it and reloads them. It never writes over a newer file.

**If you were typing in a note that changed elsewhere at the same time (since build 308).** The editor remembers which version your text started from. When the two devices changed **different lines**, also lines next to each other, PARAGON keeps both changes in the one note, shows no message, and you have nothing to do. When both changed **the same line**, your text is saved as a copy named "… (conflict date time).md" next to the note, the note shows the other version, and a message says: "Trip" was changed outside the app while you were editing it. Your version is saved as "Trip (conflict 2026-11-06 2040)" next to it; the note now shows the other version. Merge those two by hand; it is rare. The hidden mark the Reminders sync puts on a task does not count as a change: a task typed right under one the sync has just marked is simply kept. The copy is not synced to Reminders while it has "(conflict …)" in its name, so its tasks are not there twice. (Before build 308 your text was always set aside as a copy, even when the lines were different.)

**Versions parked by iCloud (since build 308).** When both devices save the same note at nearly the same moment, iCloud keeps one version and parks the other where no app shows it. PARAGON looks for parked versions after each re-read, for the notes that changed, and every 10 minutes for all notes. It saves each one beside its note as "… (conflict date time).md" and tells you: "Trip" was changed on two devices at the same time. The other version is saved as "Trip (conflict 2026-11-06 2040)" next to it. With several notes: 3 notes were changed on two devices at the same time. The other versions are saved next to them, with "(conflict …)" in their names. A parked version that is the same as the note makes no copy. Open the copy, take what you need, and delete it.

A note that has not arrived yet is treated as a note that exists. The app will not make a fresh empty one in its place, which would leave you with two versions of the same note for iCloud to argue about. If you open one before it has landed, it says so and you can try again a moment later. If **Close the day** cannot save your line because today's note is still coming, it stays open with your words in it and says so; press **Done** again in a moment. If the note then turns out to have a line already, written on your other device, that line is kept and your words go below it. The goal for the week and **Plan** work the same way: nothing you typed is thrown away, and the box stays open until it can be saved. While a week's note is still coming, its goal box says so in orange and cannot be pressed. If the goal was changed on your other device while you were typing, the box does not write over it: a message says what the goal now is, your words stay in the box, and pressing **Done** again puts yours in its place.

## Getting new versions

Both apps come from TestFlight. When a new build is sent, TestFlight tells you and you press
Update: on the iPhone in the TestFlight app, on the Mac in TestFlight for Mac. The Mac app
lives in your Applications folder like any other app.

To see which version you have, look at **Settings › About**: **Version** says, for example, 1.0 (317). Since build 316 the technical lines are behind the row **Details** under it, closed at first: open it to see **Device id**, which you can copy, and one sentence about how the sync keeps its links. To read what a version changed, open **Settings › Help › What's new** (called "Version history" before build 315). Since build 316 a date in its headings is written like 2026-11-13. It lists every version, newest first.

## Keyboard shortcuts

- **Escape** closes an edit window without saving (Cancel). **⌘S** saves it (in **New** too). **Tab** moves to the next text field.
- ⌘N new entry, ⇧⌘N quick capture, ⇧⌘F search everywhere, ⇧⌘R sync with Reminders
- ⌘F Find in Note, ⌘G Find Next, ⇧⌘G Find Previous (since build 315; Mac)
- A key for quick capture from any app, if you chose one in **Settings › Quick capture** (since build 315; Mac): ⌃⌥Space, ⌃⇧Space or ⌃⌥⌘Space
- Go menu (since build 314): ⌘1 Inbox, ⌘2 Today, ⌘3 Plan, ⌘4 All actions, ⌘5 Aspirations, and since build 315 ⌘6 Projects and ⌘7 Weekly review; ⌃⌘↑ closes the open note (the list goes full width again)
- ⌃⌘← back to the note you came from, ⌃⌘→ forward again
- ⌘⌫ delete the open note (it waits in Deleted)
- ⌃⌘D copy diagnostics, a log you can paste when reporting a problem
- One click on the sync ring (since build 312; before, a right-click) shows **Sync now**, the sync preview and the last report

## The launch check

Since build 305, before every upload to TestFlight, the iPhone app (in the simulator) and the Mac app are started with a test vault and watched for 20 seconds. If either one is still busy (the processor working hard) or has stopped, the upload is stopped, so a build that hangs at start does not reach you. A screen test does the same check on the automatic test runs. You do not see it in the app.

## When something looks wrong

Help › Copy Diagnostics (⌃⌘D — control, not option) copies a short log: the build number, what was clicked, which section and note are open, and the window layout. Paste it into the chat with the developer. The version is also shown, as PARAGON 1.0 (315), at the foot of the Mac sidebar and at the foot of **Browse** on the iPhone, and as **Version** in **Settings › About**.
